Summary

Clumsy yet capable Kara manages to hurt herself on accident. Often, too. But now her favorite emergency room has a new nurse...

Or, the sexy spooky deep east folklore fic you didn't know you needed.

Caddo Lake Whoopsie Daisies

by AloneButNeverLonely

https://archiveofourown.org/works/63239692

    Uncertain, Texas

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter One: Uncertain, Texas


    Caddo Lake has a certain ambiance to some.

    At night, the cricket and bullfrog song can be so loud that one can scarcely form a thought, but in the daylight, the glittering gold of the sun can soothe creatures both large and small by spelling them right to sleep. You would be very unlucky indeed to hazard a wrong step over the right beast during the night— but during the day, one such as a gator would be hard-pressed to amble out of its sleepy sunbathing just for a human-sized bite to eat. Poachers sometimes have a way of going missing deep in the murk of the swamp at night (a fate supplied by a wayward lust for pelts, tusks, and skins) and are often never to be seen or heard from again. Most of the time the disappearances are chalked up to either snakebite or gator attack, though some would argue a creature much larger to be responsible.

    Upright it walks on two legs, they say. Some claim it to be massive, easily over ten feet tall. Covered in a thick, putrid-smelling fur and eyes that glow blood red in the dark…

    They call it the Caddo Lake monster.

    …or just “bigfoot”, depending on who you’re talking to.

    While many dare not to paddle the shallows of Caddo Lake for this very reason, either day or night, the locals know the tale to be nonsense. It was meant as fodder for campfire stories, something to spook the young and naïve with… Kara Danvers was one such naïve youngster. But no longer.

    She’d spent her whole life on the Caddo, but now as a twenty something adult with aspirations of reaching the big city one day to own her own construction company, she needed as much work experience as she could get. So, naturally, she took whatever was available… Which was the county road crew. It was summer, and Lord have mercy, was it sweltering.

    “Buckle your fall harness, please.” Kara said as someone strolled past with a chainsaw in hand. They ignored her outright, or maybe they just didn’t hear her… Regardless, she’d make herself heard. That was her job, after all. She took her trusty bullhorn from the hood of her Wrangler and cleared her throat, expecting complete and utter compliance this time. “Fall harnesses are to be worn and fastened correctly, gentleman! I don’t care for a visit from OSHA this time of year, do you?”

    The man threw an arm back at her with a harrumph and then clasped the front buckle of his fall harness across his chest. The swinging end of the line hook that traipsed along after him dangled just above the ground, bouncing off each of his bootheels as he continued to walk towards the nearest bucket truck. Kara would have no accidents here today. Not on her watch. Laziness or carelessness often resulted in accidents, and accidents always resulted in a visit from the state regulatory board. Kara didn’t like surprises. She liked good, dependable, monotonous hard labor. Or rather, supervising the good, dependable, monotonous hard laborers…

    “Do we have enough bar oil to last the rest of the week?” She asked someone else as she peeked into the bed of a pickup. There were more empty boxes than there were full ones, so she felt inclined to offer at least one of her crew an air-conditioned trip back into town. “No, this’ll barely last the day. Can you put in an order for ten more cases and then go pick them up from will call? The account number’s written in sharpie on the driver’s side visor.”

    The lineman nodded with an eager tuck of his chin (probably just happy to sit in front a blasting a/c for an hour) and away he went. Kara closed the tailgate of the pickup truck to see him off safely and winced with a sharp breath through her teeth for her trouble— its hot metal surface had burned her fingertips.

    Kara Danvers was many things: a daughter, a sister, a supervisor, as well as a host of other lesser-known monikers (that is, if you asked anyone around town) but above all, she was known far better for being one thing in particular…

    She was just a little bit clumsy.

    It was the main reason why she held the position of “supervisor” instead of “crewman”. The company she worked for was contracted by the county to perform landscape and road maintenance all year-round, and her inherent clumsiness wasn’t for a lack of awareness or simple disregard for safety procedures —those of which she had in spades— it was just that she had the worst sort of luck. Murphy’s Law was an abundant guest throughout Kara’s daily life, but that never stopped her from working with the rest of her crew even when they suggested that she just sit back and observe. What would be the point of that? Getting paid to do nothing but stand around?

    That sounds boring as hell… Kara mused inwardly as she donned her plastic safety glasses and straightened the crewneck collar of her white t-shirt. Its short sleeves wrapped tightly around the lean swells of her biceps, and she rounded her arm out from her shoulder, thinking that she just might cut the sleeves off to begin with since she abhorred a farmer’s tan more than anything. But there was plenty of work to do that day and little time to waste towards any alterations of her clothes, no matter how small. Besides, who would care? She wasn’t dating anyone at the moment. In fact, she couldn’t remember the last time she’d seen the inside of a restaurant. At the end of the day, a strict tan line that ran midway up her arm was just another hazard of the job.

    The task at hand was to clear the powerlines that ran alongside the wetland area of the lake of any obstructions, of which there were many. Cypress trees weren’t exactly known for being very “branchy”, so they would make quick work of the tall limbs that grew too heavily over the long, twisted cables that spanned from one pole to another. The lines were in danger of being pulled too taut with the way the branches rested against them and could end up costing the county much more in the way of repairs than it did to simply maintain the swampy growth on a regular basis. Kara could recall this same job not two years prior.

    The thing which made it all so difficult was all of the Spanish moss that dripped from each branch like wispy, infuriating curtains. It was a balmy heat here just half an hour west of Louisiana marshland, so if you were to have a moss or mite allergy— you were simply shit out of luck. Once it stuck to you, it would be with you until the end of the day. Thankfully, their work would end at the markers denoting the Texas/Louisiana border in a few days’ time, which, if their first experience with Caddo Parish’s line clearing crew was any indication of how a second interaction may go, they could (at the very least) expect an exchange of semi-professional heckling and the odd hurling of water bottles back and forth. Men had a strange way of interfacing with one another; Kara could never understand it. Nor did she wish to partake. Groups of women were also just as daunting sometimes. She usually just settled for the in-between, which meant, of course, that she mostly kept to herself on the weekends. Apart from visiting her sister Alex and hanging out with her best friend Winn, Kara didn’t get up to much with anyone else.

    She sweat in the shade with her hardhat and clipboard and squinted past the sun to watch her crew continue to work. They could stop for a break soon, as they were making good enough time. It was nice at least to be near the water for a change. It wasn’t like they were pouring hot asphalt all week down the main drag…

    Something squelched suddenly in the soggy mud behind her— almost like someone had taken a large step. Kara turned around to find that no one was there, but that there was evidence of something having slid in the mud just a few paces from where she stood. The leather of her work glove felt rough when she swiped at her brow, and she puzzled her expression for a moment as she looked around. The trees were dense in some areas, and sparse in others. Saplings grew up like long, skinny sticks in between them, obscuring her view with an even more gnawing sense that she wasn’t alone anymore.

    “Hello…?” Kara said into the gentle breeze. She closed her eyes to the wind as it brushed crisp and heavenly over the damp skin of her forearms, eliciting a sharp and almost painful pricking of gooseflesh there. Something foul wafted into the air at the tail end of her gentle breeze, making her scrunch her nose with a slight pinch of disgust. Probably just pockets of methane from all of the decaying matter that got swallowed up in the ankle-deep murk… When Kara opened her eyes, the sound of another footstep drew her attention again, but this time, it came from her left.

    Her eyes flitted there in an instant.

    “Winn? I swear, if that’s you—”

    Some of the men back at the bucket truck called out for a falling limb just then, and down it came crashing into the shallow water with a loud and inevitable -splash-. Kara twisted back around to see that the men were alright, and when she turned back to face the swamp, a peculiar looking tree trunk that seemed to be covered with a sort of fuzzy growth caught her eye.

    Then it took a step.

    Kara didn’t waste any time backpedaling through the mire. She made it as far as one of the work trucks and then clipped a little too closely around its toolboxed bed in her haste to get away— She didn’t happen to see the sharp end of a long, metal file hanging out from one of the drawers until it was too late. It sliced cleanly just between two of her ribs, which began to bleed profusely. Kara dropped to her knees and fought to keep a hand against the cut on her side, but it hurt too damn much to apply any pressure there herself.

    Winn just happened to come around the end of the truck about that time, likely in search of her to gossip about either this or that. He kicked into a run at the sight of his best friend holding herself up by her head on the ground…

    “Kara!” Winn skidded to a stop in the stumpy grass beside her, grueling one “oh God” and “that’s blood” after another until she was able to hold her head up. Kara couldn’t help but to look back towards the edge of the swamp where she’d been standing… The urge to flee was still running hot in her veins. Had she really seen what she thought she had?

    “I’m alright.”

    “What the hell happened?” He asked as he rolled her onto her other side, grimacing once more at the thick, sticky red color that streaked across her shirt.

    Kara shrugged with another wince. It didn’t hurt to breathe, so that was good at least. She was hesitant at first to feel how deep the cut was between her ribs, but judging by the way her shirt was already soaked through with blood meant that it was indeed deep enough for stitches.

    So much for zero accidents this quarter…

    “I dunno, I didn’t see the drawer—” Kara groaned as Winn applied more pressure to her wound.

    “I don’t think its gonna stop bleeding any time soon.” He said with an awful gurn. Not a fan of blood, this one… Even after being friends with Kara since childhood. He took it all in stride— Winn Schott was good like that.

    “Okay.” Kara lumbered up to her knees. It was hard to see the full extent of the damage no matter how far she craned her head beneath her arm, but she felt around with the tips of her fingers and hoped that she could close it herself. She wasn’t exactly a stranger to the emergency room that was just a short drive into Shreveport. Kara gave a toss of her chin towards her Jeep, “Listen,” She began calmly, “There’s a tube of super glue in the dash—”

    “What? No.” Winn immediately protested. “Get up. There’s gauze in the first aid kit- but we’re going to the ER. I’m driving.”

    “Aren’t you afraid I’ll bleed all over your shitbox?”

    “The shitbox has seen worse, trust me.” He meant his late eighties Land Cruiser, of course. Winn pulled Kara to her feet, earning himself a pained grunt through the hard clench of her teeth. “This way—” He began tugging her behind the long row of lifted four by four pickups, “So the guys don’t see us bail.”

    “Yeah,” Kara agreed, “Good idea. Thanks, Winn.”

     

     

    Kara couldn’t believe her eyes.

    (For the second time that day, no less)

    Getting a room to be sewn up in was less of a problem this time, as the sight of her bloodstained shirt made for easy passage straight through triage. A nurse had swept through the door quickly with a suture tray and required implements in hand, but Kara had never seen her before. She knew all the nurses here; they were sweet on her, too. Kara could charm most of them for a spot of fun, flirting back and forth being the usual method of payment for their time… but this one had her flummoxed. Kara’s tongue was all but tied into a knot.

    She had long, impossibly dark hair that was pulled back into a tight ponytail at the crown of her head, and smooth, alabastrine features upon her face. Magnificently thick eyebrows the same color of her hair, and a straight yet elegant nose. She wore a dark lip, but not too dark as it was still red (and a glorious contrast to the fine pinkish tint of her cheekbones). It was her eyes that had struck Kara at a loss for words. She’d spoken for a moment, but Kara had to shake her head as she fumbled her way back towards the shape of the English language…

    “Huh?”

    The nurse laughed. “I was just saying that I’m going to stitch you back together now?” She stuck her hand out with a smile, and Kara damn near fell off the edge of the bed. “My name’s Lena,” She said as the dimples in her cheeks grew further, “Apparently, you’re kind of a celebrity around here.”

    Kara hacked a laugh and then grimaced when she stretched too far to sit upright again. Somehow, she’d forgotten the entire reason that she was there in the first place… Kara ended up taking her hand with a squeeze instead of a shake. She didn’t like it when men shook her hand like that, as if sawing a log, so she made it a point to not do it to others. “Is that right?” She asked finally with a breathy laugh.

    “From what I hear, anyway.”

    “Hopefully good things.” Kara tried flirting. It was a groove that she’d never had difficulty with settling into, if she were being honest. She felt that it was her sense of humor that made her seem approachable, and from there it was only a small jump towards confidence. That or one blundering misstep straight into awkwardness. Thankfully, most admired her awkward nature just as much as her abundant charm.

    Lena tucked away the rest of her smile with a small bite of her cheek. She seemed adamant to rein in the way the corners of her mouth fought to turn upward. Seeing it only instilled Kara with a burning desire to make her do it again, and as soon as possible, too. Lena gave a pretty upwards tilt of her head then as a gesture for Kara to move towards her. “Can you lift your arm?” She asked as she pulled on a pair of bright blue examination gloves.

    “Bad idea.” Kara shook her head.

    “I need you out of your shirt.”

    “I’m aware, but when I lift my arm, it starts bleeding again.”

    “Well, that’s what I’m here for. I may not have muscles like yours but I’m stronger than I look. I’ll catch you if you pass out.”

    Kara sat and considered what it might feel like to be so close to the new nurse. Her mind began to wander, and then she felt an admonishing wave of better sense as it washed over her.

    Don’t be a creep, just take your damn shirt off.

    She lifted her arm to try and pull it through her sleeve, but at the first sign of pain, she relented. Aware now that she might end up being more trouble than this new nurse had bargained for, Kara decided just to be rid of her shirt in the only other way that made sense. She reached inside the torn fabric at her side and carefully pulled across, ripping the top half of her shirt from the bottom half. From there it was easy just to rip it the rest of the way from the trunk of her body in pieces.

    Kara smirked at the slightly dumbfounded look upon Lena’s face. It seemed to sit somewhere betwixt astonished and impressed. “Don’t worry,” Kara explained, smoothing her blonde hair out of her face, “I’ve walked out of here without a shirt before.”

    All Lena did was nod with a quiet “Mm.” Her cheeks ran flush again with a slight shade of pink as she rolled her lips into her mouth. Kara was thrilled. She hadn’t felt this excited in a long, long time, but then again, there wasn’t much excitement in a town with a population of less than one hundred.

    “You’re new.” Kara noted out loud as she carefully dabbed at some of the dirt which had settled across her long collarbones. At least her favorite sports bra was spared; the cut on her side was just beneath its stretchy band. That would be just a shade too naked for a first encounter, anyway.

    Lena nodded again as she went to work carefully inspecting Kara’s wound. Even the way she squinted her eyes was beautiful. “I moved here about a month ago.”

    “That tracks.” Kara replied, “I haven’t been here in about a month.”

    “And that’s a record for you?”

    “Well, I mean- it’s faster than the emergency clinic all the way back in Marshal.”

    “I’m guessing that’s where you’re from? You don’t sound very Cajun. There’s a lot of that here.”

    “Amazing what a thirty-minute drive will do.” Kara laughed. “And close, I live on the west end of the Caddo, in Uncertain.”

    “I’m sorry?” Lena asked with a slight rear of her head back.

    Kara smiled. Always a conversation starter, her hometown. “Uncertain. It’s a tiny little drop of a town, mostly marinas and cottages for people who wanna ghost hunt the lake.”

    “Wait, that’s a thing?”

    Kara winced suddenly, ill prepared for the way Lena adjusted the skin around her wound.

    “Sorry…” Lena apologized. “The good news is that the muscle seems to be fine, so we don’t have to suture that deep.

    “S’alright. I, um… I should probably tell you that I cry every time I get stitches.”

    Lena hid another smile. “You should probably try not to come up in need of them so often, then.”

    “Believe me, I’ve heard it all before.”

    “I’m sure you have.” Lena made a point to stare directly into Kara’s eyes then as she took her by the wrist. “I’m going to place your hand on your other shoulder like this, unless you want to rest it on mine. Would that be easier?”

    Kara gulped loudly. “No- I mean,” She fumbled for a second, “This way is fine.”

    “Okay. Deep breath…”

    Kara clenched her teeth tightly with the small stretch of her arm across her chest. She let go of her held breath then with a few painful flutters of her abdomen, and caught Lena’s eyes darting there not once, but twice. She wasn’t like the other nurses, Kara decided. Sure, they had fun with an hour or two of harmless flirting and profuse complimenting, a trip to the ER usually being a large boost to her self-esteem for such crummy circumstances… but they all had boyfriends, or girlfriends, or families, or school to keep them just on this side of playful. Kara never had any intentions of taking things further. How could she? Wasn’t there some nurse/patient boundary that was never meant to be trespassed? How unfair. The first time she’d felt her heart flutter like a slap-happy butterfly again and the object of her desire was completely out of reach.

    Oh well…

    “Can I ask why?”

    Kara gave a curious tilt of her head, “Huh?” She asked. Damn it to hell, that word… She wished to wipe it from her vocabulary for good.

    Lena smiled again, and Kara’s heart resumed its wild and relentless flapping inside of her chest. “You said you cry every time you get stitches. You just don’t seem like the type to do that.”

    “Oh… It’s the muscles, right?”

    “Maybe.” Lena answered, pulling her lips into an unmistakably thin line.

    “Well. Can I tell you a secret then?” Kara whispered, making sure not to lean too close. Lena was busy squaring away her instruments upon her little tray.

    “Sure.” Lena whispered back with a playful vapor.

    “I may look tough, but I’m actually a huge baby. Ask anyone. You should see me when I’m sick.”

    Lena laughed. “Then I hate to do this to you, but we’re going to have to clean this before I sew you back together.”

    Kara’s smile dropped like a ton of bricks. “Alright.” Then she sighed, “Right when I was beginning to like you, too. Shame.”

    “Oh, shush.”

    “…Yes ma’am.”

    The All-Seeing Cypress

    Chapter Summary

    Kara visits the ER in Shreveport again, but this time under different circumstances.

    Chapter Notes

    We're plowing our way towards an actual plotline here, people! I'm satisfied with it. Expect folklorish/cryptid stuffs every other chapter (or more). The creepiness will abound throughout, just as much as the budding of romance does. I like spooky things, okay. It's hot.

    CW: mentions of death, non-graphic depictions of corpses

    Chapter Two: The All-Seeing Cypress


    The work had gone by faster than Kara expected, which was always a good thing.

    Spurred along by a heat that soaked them from crown to heel, the final trees had been trimmed away from the last of the towering powerlines. They were officially done.

    The cleanup that came after was a job in itself, though. Kara was altogether unsurprised when her trailered woodchipper had decided to go on the fritz right after she’d made the last payment on it. Among the many machines and tools she either owned or leased, the woodchipper was one she did not wish to repair herself. Knowing her luck, she’d lose an arm. Or worse. Having to police all of their debris by hand now, she was looking at unbilled overtime for how long it would take to haul all of it away. Mucking around in the mud and dirt just made her legs all the more tired.

    Most of her crew had gone home, but thankfully, a few opted to stick around and help. These were the same guys she’d eventually hire whenever she owned her own business. They were loyal, and hardworking to boot. That, and they didn’t treat her as if she were incapable just because she happened to be a woman. There were the few bad apples in every bunch, of course, but Kara made sure to take care of them all in the best way that she knew how. She didn’t treat them like shit, for one thing. If they were sick, she’d let them off. If they needed time with their families, she’d let them have it. Treating her guys like human beings instead of mindless drones was how she retained so many of them in the first place. Turnover was low under her leadership, which only helped to solidify her position wherever her bosses were concerned.

    They were almost done picking up the last few limbs when Kara tripped over something sticking up out of the mud. She managed to land on her good side at least, down onto a bed of fresh green moss that was pillowy and soft. Nature seemed to have her back every once in a while; She was thankful for it. Her breath went out with an “oof” as she rolled over and laid there as still and as silent as the grave. She was so tired.

    “Y’alright?” One of the men asked as they walked by.

    Kara nodded. “Yeah. Let’s just…” She blew a few baby fine wisps of her blonde hair out of her eyes, “Let’s just take five, okay?”

    Five minutes would be one thing. Laying there for the rest of the day would be another. Kara knew that it was the getting back up that would be a problem. Her stitches had been pushed to the limit by the way they ached against her side. It was a foolhardy reminder that she hadn’t exactly been taking it easy… Kara had two speeds: 0 or 100. Unfortunately, there wasn’t a lazy bone in her body. She couldn’t help but to want to do a good job. She’d at least been careful for most of the week, but falling down, however, had been a bit out of her control.

    Kara blinked tiredly up into the tall, woody canopy and watched as the long, draping Spanish moss licked at the bald trunks of all the cypress trees. No matter the bug bites, or the itching vines, or the stinging nettles, or all the scrapes, bumps, or bruises she had amassed in all of her years… This place was still home, and it would be forever. At least in her heart. She loved the ethereal, almost gothic feel of the Caddo, especially between the twinkling hours of sunset. Deep down, Kara knew that leaving it all behind would be the hardest decision she would ever make.

    During her rest, something bright glinted in her line of sight from somewhere near a low branch. Whatever it was appeared to be shiny and metallic, and so very out of place for swampy woodland. Kara sat up carefully whilst cradling the stitches upon her ribs and squinted further into the trees. Her eyes must have been playing tricks on her… That tended to happen out here. Especially if one were to find themselves in an exhausted, or confused state.

    “Is that…” She mumbled to herself as she stood back up, “Well I’ll be damned. How’d that get up there?”

    A hunting rifle hung from its strap some fifteen feet up off the ground. It dangled there helplessly from the lowly limb, all by its lonesome. Kara went to traipse further in and inspect how such a thing could get caught up so high, but the object that had tripped her up in the mud met the toe of her boot once again. This time, she didn’t so much as stumble. Kara looked down and saw the ruddy sole of a hiking boot looking straight back up at her, and she froze.

    The boot, the gun…

    “Oh, shit.” Kara shook her head. A sense of dread quickly filled her up.

    She was quick to take her phone from her back pocket and to dial her sister. Alex Danvers was a forest and wildlife ranger, and always Kara’s first call for things of this nature. Unfortunately, this wasn’t the first time she had “stumbled” upon evidence of missing persons. Twenty-five thousand acres of cypress forest seemed to swallow up hunter and boatman alike; it harbored no prejudices. It took whoever it pleased, and held a grudge like you wouldn’t believe. There was a saying around these parts: Disrespect the wood, and the wood will disrespect you right back.

    After a few rings, Alex answered. “Hey, Kara. What’s up?”

    Kara cleared her throat as she kicked the upside-down boot again. It rang back solidly through her own sole, and her stomach turned. “You remember that group of hunters that got split up out here near the dam a few weeks back?”

    “Yeah…” Alex replied, “Why?”

    “You still lookin’ for one of ‘em?”

    “Yeah- Oh, God… Really?”

    “Yup. I think we found him.”

    Right about then, one of the men moseying by just so happened to lay his curious eyes upon the boot. He reached down to pull it free from the mud before Kara could stop him, and with a single hard tug it came loose with a sickening -shuck- sort of sound— only to expose the socked foot underneath.

    “You might want to call the coroner’s office on your way here.” Kara added.

    The man with the empty boot in his hand had turned a bright shade of green with his discovery. He swayed back and forth a few times, catching his weight with shaky steps, and Kara braced herself to lunge forward and catch him. Being nearly twice her size, she failed miserably. He went down like a sack of potatoes, falling into a clumsy heap of limbs only to crack the top of his bald head loudly upon a rock. The numbskull hadn’t been wearing his hardhat. But with the danger of falling objects from above being gone, Kara guessed that he’d removed it thinking the job was done. Who knew the danger to his pristine gourd would end up being on the ground all along?

    Kara tucked her phone back into her pocket and gave a long, dour sigh. At least he came to quickly enough, only having knocked himself out cold for a few seconds. The rock beneath him was split completely in half, though, right down its middle. Kara would have been impressed if she wasn’t already concerned for his well-being. She kneeled down and tried talking to him, but ultimately decided that she didn’t like the way his eyes rolled around inside of his head like a couple of loose marbles.

    “Alright.” Kara said as she tucked herself up underneath his arm and helped him to stand again. A large goose egg had formed almost immediately on the top of his head; another good sign. “Let’s go. But be warned… if you barf in my Jeep, that bump on your head will be the last of your problems.”

     

     

    Going over all of it in her head only left her with more questions than she had answers for.

    Kara had come across the odd quagmire over the years, but had never seen one swallow up a man before. The fact that he’d apparently gone in headfirst was as equally unnerving. It was as if hell itself had reached up to drag him down by the tops of his ears. Normally it was deer that would get caught in the soft, boggy murk, and the more they struggled to get free, the more they would sink in. There was a scar on one of Kara’s shins as evidence of such. It had come from a full set of antlers; the buck who owned them having been buried below, deep into the quickly sinking dirt.

    It wasn’t unlike the Caddo take a life under such mysterious circumstances, but sometimes it was just a little too much for Kara, if she were being honest. She’d always felt that it was a “who” rather than a “what”. Comprised of wetlands that were both swamp and marsh, not to mention the world’s largest cypress forest and the stretch of muddy, opaque water that was more bayou than lake between it all… the area was steeped in history, as well as tragedy. Kara had never known anything else (besides humans) to be more vengeful. It was why most of the people that lived there felt that the Caddo was no laughing matter, apart from some of the more farfetched ghost stories that came from it, of course. It really did depend on who you spoke to. Older folks seemed to worship the place, tarrying carefully around its edge while never daring to venture inside, while those much younger sought to test their resolve with an ill-advised call to adventure.

    There wasn’t much else to say about those who made their living off the Caddo, except for the fact that they turned up either dead or missing more than they didn’t. As for herself, Kara saw nothing wrong with harboring a healthy respect for something that had no qualms at all about eating you for breakfast.

    It didn’t take long to occupy her mind with something else, preferably of a happier disposition as well. The pretty smiles that sat on the other side of the nurse’s station seemed to work wonders.

    She had some time to waste.

    One of the good things about being a county contractor was the insurance. Kara didn’t like to take chances with her underlings and their state of health, so a bump on the noggin meant the full treatment. Triage, scans, the works. With nothing left to do but wait, she was plumb satisfied just to wag her chin until it was time to go. After a while, the glossy sheen of dark hair turned the far end of the nurse’s station and, much to her delight, a smile like the morning sun beamed in her direction. Kara clapped her mouth shut on her approach.

    Lena swaggered a bit to lean against the tall counter and folded her arms. “What did you do now?” She asked.

    Kara held her palms out in defense, “Not me this time. Came here with somebody else.”

    “Ah. Then how come you’re favoring that arm so much?”

    “I—” Kara stopped. Sharp, this one… Kara had been careful not to lift her arm in all the time she’d stood there. The subtle ache from her stitches had now changed to something more of a throb. There was no denying it. Kara shrugged, “We had work to do.”

    “You have got to be kidding me.”

    Kara gestured again with her palms, this time with an air of surrender, but before she could muddle her way towards a better excuse, Lena tugged her by her good elbow down the hall and behind a curtain. Kara felt giddy with the way Lena had snuck them off somewhere private, but with look she was given in the short moments that came after, that giddiness soon morphed into a dripping sense of unease. Lena slid the curtain closed again upon its noisy track and then spun to motion at Kara with a demanding wave of her hand. “Up.” She said.

    She meant Kara’s shirt, but Kara was too busy nodding towards the curtain. “Don’t you wanna leave that open?”

    Lena shook her head, “It’s fine. Let me see.”

    “Okay, but aren’t you gonna type on the computer first?” Kara nodded this time at the rolling computer stand, “Everyone else always makes me wait—”

    “Up.” Lena said again with another insistent wave of her hand. “I’ve never known anyone to pop my stitches, but with you, I can’t say I’d be surprised.”

    Kara shrugged as she unfastened one shirt button after another. “I’ll take that as a compliment…” Now that her stitches were exposed to new air, Kara winced when she leaned to toss her shirt over the back of a chair. Lena immediately stooped to inspect the somewhat healed laceration upon Kara’s ribs.

    “Oh, my God.” She guffawed, “What have you been doing? Fucking parkour or something?”

    “Ooh, she cusses—”

    “Oh…” Lena shook her head with a grumble and then strolled across the small space to don a pair of examination gloves. She crooked her finger once she was ready for Kara to join her near the small table of gauze and sterile water.

    “You’re gonna hurt me again, aren’t you?”

    “That depends.”

    “On what, exactly…?”

    Kara grimaced at the cold feeling of Lena’s fingertips moving around her wound again.

    “On whether or not you want to listen to me when I say you should rest.”

    “I’ll be good.” Kara gave a two-fingered salute straight out from her brow. “I promise.”

    “Sit down.”

    “Okay.”

    Kara sat quickly and cupped the back of her neck to keep her arm aloft while Lena dabbed gently at her stitches. It was no wonder that she was dissatisfied at the state of them— Kara craned her head down to get a better look and saw that they were indeed red and angry. A fair bit of guilt washed over her after that. She knew that Lena had worked hard to sew them in as painlessly as possible, and this was how she had repaid her kindness?

    “Sorry…”

    Lena shook her head. Calm and kind were the features upon her face again. “It’s alright. I know you’ll be more careful now.”

    A few moments passed after that where Kara was leaping at the next thing to say, only she didn’t quite know what to say. Maybe it was because she just liked the sound of Lena’s voice? It wasn’t often that Kara felt pressed to fill the sound of silence with her incessant rambling, but the way that her mouth begged to flap up and down was beyond bewildering. Thankfully, Lena took the burden from her in the form of a question.

    “So. What do you do, anyway?” She asked. “I’ve heard some stories…”

    Kara laughed with a following wince, “That doesn’t sound concerning at all.”

    “It’s nothing bad. Just that whenever you come in you’re always kind of…” Lena trailed off with a shrug.

    “Kind of what?”

    “…kind of dirty?”

    “Oh.” Kara nodded. That wasn’t so bad. It’s not like it was far from the truth, anyway. “Yeah, no, that’s fair… I’m a contractor. I work outside a lot.”

    “And that explains a lot.”

    “Haven’t you poked around in my medical history by now? My job’s gotta be listed somewhere in there.”

    Lena gave an adamant frown, “Nope. Well, not beyond my first time with you.” Then she pinched her face with a slight embarrassment, “I mean- The first time I treated you—”

    “I get it.” Kara laughed. She could feel a heat begin to race beneath her skin as if she were standing much too close to a very large flame. She was alright with it.

    “So, what sort of work do you do as a contractor? I’m curious.”

    Kara dared to lean just a little bit closer. “The kind that usually ends up with me being covered from head to toe in dirt.” Her heart gave a giant leap whenever Lena smiled in response. “Sorry if I don’t smell all that great.” She added, “There was a lot to do today.”

    “I don’t mind.” Lena replied quietly. Kara saw a pair of bright blue-green eyes flash upwards at her with a quick but shy glance. This feeling… it was almost like being terrified and drunk all at the same time. It was exhilarating.

    “Do you…” Kara chewed on the inside of her lip, “…plan on staying long?”

    “Yeah, I guess. The girls are nice.”

    “Have any family here?”

    “No.” Lena finally sat down upon the rolling stool beside Kara and pulled her gloves from her hands. Kara took it as means to pass the time with idle conversation, now that she was done being poked and prodded like a stubborn mule. The butterflies in her stomach returned with a vengeance.

    Kara studied Lena’s hands for a quick second, double checking for a ring. “Boyfriend?”

    “No.”

    “…Girlfriend?”

    Lena pinched back another smile, but her dimples were proof enough. “No.”

    “Well,” Kara chanced a deep breath, still wary of her inflamed stitches, “Everyone here’s pretty laid back. For what it’s worth, I hope you stick around.”

    “And here I was thinking I’d all but run you off with my poor bedside manner.”

    “Bedside manner’s decent…” Kara teased, “But we’ve really gotta work on those rough sailor hands—”

    “Oh, stop it.” Lena laughed and then took up Kara’s shirt to throw it back at her.

    Kara laughed along with her, and before she knew it, her mouth was running yet again with another question, “What are you doing later?”

    Lena’s face softened, and then her eyes fell downcast. Kara wanted to kick herself. The words had slipped out without her even knowing. She tried making sense of Lena’s silence for that small moment… It seemed that there was a part of her that had wanted Kara to ask. Or maybe Kara was actually the one to hit her head that day…

    “They said you were sweet.” Lena lifted her eyes again. “But don’t do that.”

    “Do what?”

    “You know what.”

    “But why?”

    “Because.”

    “Well, that’s not exactly a reason.” Kara crooked a smile. She would back off soon, she swore it…

    “You’re a patient here.” Lena explained, “I’ve treated you.”

    Kara hung her head with that. “Yeah, I figured. Sorry.”

    “Don’t be,” Lena took a quick step forward then, almost as if she were afraid of Kara leaving too soon. “It’s not that I don’t…” She shook her head, “I—” Then her mouth fell still again with a long and defeated breath through her nose. “I’m still glad you asked.”

    “Would it make any difference if I went to a different ER from now on?”

    Lena gave a slightly unamused -tsk- from the back of her teeth and then waved Kara off. “Put your shirt back on, handsome, and get outta here.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    The White Stag

    Chapter Summary

    A not-so-typical weekend for at least one of the Danvers sisters.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Three: The White Stag

     

    Kara’s toolbox went into the back of her Jeep with a heavy, swinging lunk. It was early. Too early…

    The sound of her various sockets and pipe wrenches had rattled around loudly enough to send a nearby kettle of hawks into the air, the beat of their broad wings disquieting the formerly serene sunrise. At least the weather was fair compared to the past few weeks of oven-like temperatures.

    October on the bayou was generally foggy; The first signs of a reprieve from summer. Where the shallows ended and the piney woods began, Kara’s stilted cottage was blanketed in the rolling, wispy brume. The cool air wafted onto the land, thick and ghost-like off the warm waters, and on most mornings, Kara admired the steamy effluvium. She was never unsettled by it, even as a child. To her, it was a mere yawn— the earth beneath her feet being alive, and all. While most believed the swampy fog to bear sinister intentions, Kara knew better. There was beauty in an omen.

    The spare tire on her tailgate gave a slight bounce when she shut it closed, and from out in the distance, deep in the low, roiling fog, a woody -snap- caught her attention. Kara twisted around to stare dead-on at the largest set of antlers she’d ever seen. Craning her head upward slowly to get the full picture of him, the stag that stood in front of her was massive. All white and nearly twice her height from crown to hoof, it had ceased the mindless chewing of its cud to stare back at her.

    Kara remained still.

    And so did the stag.

    Albino deer were a rarity. Even here. Some would say that catching a mere glimpse of one was a foreboding; a sign of an untimely death. Others said that the White Stag would only mean your doom if you were to kill it, and that leaving it in peace would instead bring about your life’s greatest upset. Kara would rather the latter, if she were being honest. “Upset” didn’t necessarily have to mean “bad”. For her, it could simply mean “change”. There were many stories of the slain White Stag, whose demise worked like a sweeping wind of death, bringing about the end of the hunters who had killed it, as well as their families, one by one until they were all gone from the world.

    Yes, Kara was sure of it. She’d choose whatever mercies the stag would allow her, if she but only show mercy herself first. An easy thing to do, really. She had never killed anything but fish.

    The stag took one step closer to her, and then another. Curious, but also wholly unafraid. Kara gulped loudly and kept her arms at her sides while it nosed around the bridge of her glasses, only to take them from her face with a toothy -crunch-, snapping them in half. Kara winced with the sound. She didn’t need them all the time, thankfully. Only those closest to her had ever seen her wear them. Seemingly dissatisfied with the way her spectacles had tasted, the stag shook his great set of antlers and left her just as quickly as he had set upon her in the first place.

    Kara finally let go of her breath. Just in time, too. The tingling feeling that beset her cheeks had come with a spell of dizziness.

    “Holy crap. Alex is gonna be so jealous.” She cheered quietly, bouncing her way to the driver’s seat.

     

     

    “You could probably use a bit of thread tape around some of these joints…”

    Alex crouched to get a better look at where Kara was tucked up underneath her cabinets. The steady -plink- into the catch bowl beside her sister’s head rang full; their only means of offsetting the leak beneath her sink for the time being.

    “But is that where its coming from?” Alex asked with a squint.

    Kara sighed. “No. It’s gotta be a problem with the main- and I’m not a plumber.”

    “You mean, under the house?”

    “Yes—” Kara scooted out from the damp base cabinet, “Under the house.”

    “Can’t you look there, then?”

    “I’m not really in the mood for crawling around under your trailer. There’s gotta be snakes down there.”

    Alex gave a dismissive wave of her hand and then tossed Kara a small towel. “Psh- I’ve got some antivenom from work.”

    “Seriously?” Kara huffed a laugh, “It’s crazy how me getting snakebit is still a better alternative to calling a plumber…”

    “It’ll be fine!”

    “Call a plumber, Alex.”

    Her sister finally held her hands up in surrender with a simultaneous bow of her head, “Alright, alright.” She then went to straighten the short, cotton collar of her forest green uniform shirt, checking the three buttons there just beneath her neck. “So, there’s been some talk going around, lately…”

    Kara sat down at the small kitchen table as she dried her hands. “What do you mean?”

    “We’ve been getting some more calls from around your neck of the woods- more coyote attacks. Picked off an entire brood of chickens near your place this time. People are saying it’s just one, though, and that its really, really big.”

    “Hmm.” Kara deflected poorly, making a grand and somewhat obvious gesture with her eyebrows in order to feign ignorance. “That’s weird.”

    “You haven’t been hearing anything at night?”

    “None of my neighbors are what you’d call ‘close’, so, no.”

    “Kara.”

    “…What?”

    “Tell me you’re not still feeding that ghost wolf.”

    “First of all,” Kara turned a palm upward as if to explain, “His name is ‘Dog’, and second, I don’t feed him.”

    “Kara!”

    “What?!”

    Dog was Kara’s oldest friend; she wouldn’t be giving him up so easily. Even to her sister, whose literal job description was the capturing and relocating of wildlife, especially those that were listed as endangered. What Alex called a “ghost wolf” was merely the hybridization of the near-extinct red wolf and coyote. They made for very large, very wolf-like offspring. There had been rumors of a few escaped red wolves during the 1970’s dragnet— an effort to save them from extinction. With no definite proof of their mixed breeding (other than Kara’s “Dog”) most folks just called them “ghost wolves” on account of their elusive nature. Kara was at least telling the truth when she said she hadn’t been feeding him. In fact, apart from meeting him for the first time in a gnarled-tooth stare-down deep in the forest, she’d never so much as touched him. Not even once. Dog was wild, but he was also very fond of Kara, and much too long-in-the-tooth now to do much harm to anyone or anything larger than himself.

    But Dog had to eat, obviously.

    “You need to be careful.” Alex went on, “That dog will be your kryptonite. You’re gonna get yourself seriously hurt one day.”

    Kara shook her head. “You don’t know him like I do.”

    “Yeah, that’s because if I did, I would have to tag him and take him to Saint Francis where he’d be safe.”

    “Alex, please…” Kara’s heart began to sink. “He’ll die in a sanctuary.”

    Alex sat down across from her with a huff, and then she gave an affirmative nod. “I just worry about you sometimes, that’s all. I’ll leave him alone, but you can’t keep encouraging him. It’s amazing that he’s lived so long to begin with… What were you, ten years old?”

    “Twelve.” Kara replied with a breath of relief.

    It wasn’t her fault that Dog kept coming around. He’d never even so much as growled at her. Sit beside her on her back porch and sleep beneath her steps was all he was willing to do for years. That and traipse around the wetlands with Kara on small adventures. Kara was fine with it. She did worry that one day he’d run afoul of a poacher, though. That would make her very sad indeed. Not giving him a proper name had been a failed attempt at keeping an emotional distance. Like it or not, they were very much attached to one another now.

    A comfortable silence wafted between the two sisters at the table for a while until Alex decided to speak up again. “Come out to the boardwalk with me tonight?”

    Kara groaned. The Louisiana Boardwalk was just a bit too chaotic for her tastes, especially on the weekends. It was always much too loud and rowdy; Kara could only stand about an hour or so before she was ready to go home. “But the drinks are so expensive—”

    “Then dance with somebody and get them for free.”

    “You could buy my drinks.”

    “No, see, I couldn’t even call a plumber…”

    “Alex.”

    “Kara.” Her sister flashed her big, brown, puppy dog eyes at her. “Please…?” She hung on the word for a long time, long enough for Kara to wave her hands back and forth in defeat.

    “Fine- fine.” Kara replied glumly. “But I’m not staying ‘til last call.”

    “Midnight it is.”

    “And I’m driving.”

    “I would expect nothing less.”

     

     

    Halloween is often a month-long occasion for the Louisiana native.

    The bars and accompanying nightlife are usually lit with warm, paper lanterns, and are filled with the sound of Zydeco music and dancing. Creole culture takes a long, celebratory run up to Allhallowtide at the end of October, where hundreds and sometimes thousands come to pay their respect to loved ones lost. Candlelight vigils held in cemeteries mark not a time for mourning, but a time for remembering. This typically goes hand in hand with food and drink, which can often be found in and around the boardwalk in the Shreveport-Bossier parishes.

    Kara could barely hear her sister over the sound of an accordion blaring from an overhead speaker. “What?” She shouted as someone shouldered their way past. The bar was packed, and Alex’s smile stretched from ear to ear.

    “This is the one! Let’s stay here!”

    “Yeah- Okay!” Kara agreed. They had been to three already just on this side of the river. “Can we grab a table outside?”

    “What?”

    Kara took Alex by the wrist, “Let’s go outside!”

    “Alright!”

    They began to weave past the bar and in front of the stage where a small band wearing straw fedoras and sunglasses (despite it being the nighttime) played animatedly into their instrument mics. Zydeco music was lively— a mix of rhythm and blues and what some might call “swamp pop”. A man strummed happily along a washboard hung from his neck and stomped with a polished shoe upon the stage as they squeezed their way through the crowd. The rows of paned double doors at the back of the building were open to a side street, where people strolled by with long, plastic-necked drinks and party hats. The street itself was paved with brick, a busy thoroughfare for tourists and casino-laden foot traffic alike. Mounted police were a silent presence here as well, content just to sit atop their steeds and keep watch. Music poured out from every corner, and from every wrought-iron tiered balcony. Those tabled patios were where Kara liked to be the most; she enjoyed very little the feeling of being packed in like a sardine.

    There was a tall table open just at the end of the enclosed space and Kara tugged at Alex’s wrist again to keep them moving forward. If she could just move around the group crowding the middle of the patio without knocking—

    “Kara?” Somebody said from somewhere near the middle of the rabble. “Kara Danvers!”

    More voices chimed in over the accordion coming through the large French doors, “Look- Kara’s here!” One said. “Kara, this way!” Said another.

    The group began to open up and face her, and soon enough, Kara recognized them. Some were still wearing their hospital scrubs. One of them (whom Kara knew by the name Maggie) approached with a drink in one hand as she used the other to wrap them up into a hug. Something more along the lines of a very quick embrace, rather. They had never seen one another outside of an emergency medical facility, after all.

    “I can’t believe you’re here! Come, come—” She took Kara by the hand and went to drag her back towards the others. Alex stayed right on her heels, most likely eager to meet anyone that reacted to Kara’s presence in such a joyful manner.

    There were a few more “Hi, Kara’s” and “How are you’s” once they sidled up at the skinny table. It was filled rim to rim with empty drinks— Kara guessed that they must have been here for a while already. Maggie necked her way around Kara again with another quick hug to stare at Alex for a moment, and had apparently been struck speechless in the way of a greeting. Odd… Kara thought. Never seen Maggie do that before…

    “Maggie, this is my sister, Alex.” Kara said with a shrug, “Alex, Maggie.”

    Maggie quickly cleared her throat and shot her hand out in the hopes that Alex might take it. “Maggie Sawyer. Nice to meet you finally.”

    “Finally?” Alex asked.

    “We treat Kara when she comes in every once in a while. You’ve picked her up a few times, but I’ve never managed to say hi until now.”

    The puzzled expression upon Alex’s face grew further until Kara chimed in to explain. “Alex, meet the Shreveport ER.” Her hand went out to gesture at the crowd of nurses.

    With that, Alex gave a surprised nod. “Oh…” She then turned to Kara with a not-so-subtle wink, “Oh.” The small but proud pat on Kara’s back made her want to leap towards the next and nearest subject of conversation, but Maggie Sawyer had her beat, like always.

    “So, Kara…” She swirled the melting ice around in her glass with the use of a lime wedge, “I’ve got a friend who works at the urgent clinic over in Marshal. She’s been telling me all about you lately.”

    Some words of encouragement and a few teases rose over the table after that, along with a “What, you don’t like us anymore?”

    Kara gave a hard swallow past the knot in her throat when a familiar head of dark hair worked its way back into the crowd. It was Lena. Kara felt her heart begin to race. She’d spent the past few weeks doing her absolute best not to think of that heavenly dimpled smile, but there it was, beaming straight back at her like a lance to pierce her heart.

    “Well, look who it is.” Lena said, the smooth and mellifluous tone of her voice raising the fine baby hairs upon Kara’s nape.

    Maggie stage-whispered playfully over her shoulder, “I was just asking Kara here why she’s been cheating on us with Marshal, Texas.”

    Lena pulled in a sharp breath through her teeth, “Ooh.” Then she shook her head with a few admonishing -tsks-, “I was wondering where you’d run off to.”

    Kara shrugged. “I had a… um,” She began to wring her hands together, “Had a run-in with a rogue fishing hook.” The bruised end of her thumb went up in display.

    “Mhm.” Maggie hummed with a delighted sense of victory. She took the opportune (and somewhat awkward) silence to speak to Alex again, “We were just about to go get more drinks, wanna come?”

    “Please.” Alex replied with a vapor.

    It took a minute or so for the group to filter back inside through the doors, but once they had gone, it was just Kara and Lena left at the cluttered table. Kara had never felt so nervous before, but she could see that Lena was as well by the way she thumbed around the rim of her empty glass.

    “Did you want another drink?” Kara asked, “I can go get—”

    “No, it’s fine. I don’t really care for how loud it is inside.”

    “Me either.” Kara huffed a laugh. She stood there for a moment longer and watched as Lena seemed to deliberate inwardly about whether or not to spark additional conversation, so Kara figured that she’d go ahead and take the leap in her place. “Would you wanna go somewhere a little bit quieter?”

    “Where on earth would that be in a place like this?” Lena chuckled.

    “C’mon,” Kara reached for her hand, “I’ll show you.”

    Holding Lena’s hand made Kara’s heart do backflips inside of her chest as they stepped through the small patio gate to turn down the alley. The feeling of it was just as strong as it had been before, if not stronger now. It was true that Kara had been avoiding the Shreveport emergency room… It didn’t help anyone to kick a dead horse, so to speak. It would only make her heart ache more for seeing Lena. She thought she’d been doing the right thing; sparing them both any future wants and desires. But they were here now, and Kara just wanted five minutes alone with her again. Just five minutes, and she would swear to never bother her again.

    Thanks to Alex, Kara knew these bars like the back of her hand. She also knew that many of their upstairs floors had a stairwell door down to the alley and were often left unlocked or even left ajar for those who wished to take their drinks to the balconies that hung over the street. The arched brick of the stairwell rumbled with the sound of the music’s jaunty bass, and soon they were stepping out onto the covered balcony to look at all of the carriages and passersby down below. Long strings of hanging lights drenched the balcony from end to end, covering the deep green and black iron railing with a soft hue of amber. The flickering of torchlight from below danced across the severe plane of Lena’s jawline, and from there, Kara’s gaze ventured upward once again to find herself stuck on the color of Lena’s eyes. They were green, but also blue in a certain light. Every time Kara looked at them, they were one and then the other, as if they changed of their own free will. Kara felt that she could stand there mesmerized all night, but her manners would surely prompt her otherwise. Idle conversation it was. That is, if idle conversation smacked of an apology…

    “About what Maggie said,” Kara began, “I’m sorry if she made it seem like I was avoiding you.”

    “Weren’t you, though?” Lena asked as she folded her arms over the railing.

    Kara found herself fumbling over her words again, still stuck on the fact that she was seeing the woman of her dreams in something other than patterned hospital scrubs. Lena wore a silky, dark blue top that plunged at her neckline. It billowed gracefully around her torso where it was tucked into a sleek pair of black slacks that were held together with a loose, silver chain. Her hair bore subtle waves instead of its usual straightness, and was pinned up with a large, toothy clip at the back of her head. A few small, wavy strands fell past her ears, and she went to tuck them back again with a sweep of her hand.

    Kara nodded guiltily. “I guess so.” She dropped her eyes to the round toes of her leather boots. At least she’d chosen to wear her best fitting jeans… A moment later, the music changed from its upbeat and tinny rhythm to something a bit more blues-y, and slow. “The last thing I wanted was to hurt anyone’s feelings…” Kara admitted as she looked back up, “Have I?”

    Lena took a deep breath and apparently chose not to answer. She did, however, turn to face Kara more directly. The way she ran her eyes up and down made Kara feel very, very naked for some reason, as if her insides had become her outsides, and the large crack in her heart was now on full display. This “want but can’t have” sort of feeling was excruciating. Kara didn’t like it at all.

    “Let me make it up to you?” Kara asked. Her stripy sleeveless top itched suddenly at the nape of her neck, and she went to paw there idly, praying all the while for Lena to oblige her just this once.  

    “Okay… How do you plan on doing that?”

    “Dance with me.” Before Lena could object, Kara sought to explain further, “Just a slow step here and there, I won’t spin you around or anything. What do you say?”

    “Oh, and I suppose you dance with all of your nurses?”

    Kara shook her head. “No. Just you.”

    “Really?” Lena deadpanned with a hint of a smile.

    “Ask them.”

    Lena crossed her arms again, and Kara went to stop her. She took a chance with pulling her close, and Lena landed against her with a small huff of a breath. Not so surprised, but also not so put off as to step away again… Lena eventually lowered her guard and brought both of her arms up to wrap around the back of Kara’s neck.

    “Fine. One dance, and I’ll forgive you.”

    Kara smiled as she gingerly placed her hands around Lena’s waist. “Sounds kinda like a win-win situation for me, really—”

    “Shhh…” Lena hushed as she laid her head upon Kara’s shoulder. They soon began a slow back and forth step to the music. “Let’s just pretend for tonight, okay?”

    Kara bowed her head to whisper, “Pretend?”

    “Mm. Let’s pretend that I’ve thought of nothing else besides you for weeks, and that you’ve done the very same thing.”

    “…Alright.” Kara agreed.

    After all, it would be only too easy.

     

     

    Getting Alex to stumble her way back up the front steps of her trailer without either hiccupping or giggling was more work than Kara had signed on for. But then again, that was usually how their boardwalk nights came to an end somewhere near three o’clock in the morning.

    Kara had spent the entire night on the bar balcony with Lena, talking very little and dancing more than she had in years. The image of Lena’s smile had been burned into the front of her brain, and she was thrilled about it.

    Now that her sister was safely in bed and bereft of her dancing boots, and snoring already to her heart’s content, Kara was free to head home and do the same. It was a night well spent; she figured. Though, the cloud she had danced upon with Lena was now dissipated back into the lovelorn ether. Their stark reality would soon return with the rising of the sun. Kara listened to the gravelly -crunch- of her sister’s driveway as she made her way back to her Jeep, just to hear the abrupt sound of a breathy -snort- from somewhere near the tree line.

    There the ghostly shape of the White Stag peered back at her for a long moment, as if addressing her very soul. It stared just a moment longer, and then wandered back into the wood, clipping the points of its antlers high upon the twiggy, needled branches that stood in its path. Kara leaned against the door of her Jeep, awestruck and unsettled down to the marrow in her bones. She thought of nothing else all the way back home…

    Her life, her luck, and the way the insides of her hands all but ached for one person and one person only.

    Change indeed was well on its way.

    She could feel it.

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    Little Yawning Girl

    Chapter Summary

    Kara plucks up the courage to ask Lena out, but a simple day in the sun is all she wants.

    Chapter Notes

    TW:
    very brief blood

    Chapter Four: Little Yawning Girl

     

    “Tell me Kara, why do you insist upon dragging me all the way out here every month just to not buy anything?” Winn asked as he kicked a small rock out of their path around the packed auction house.

    “Because, Winn, I might need your help if I do actually buy anything.” Kara replied, “Plus, I’ve seen some junkers haul off a few Land Cruisers here before- you know better than most how hard it is to find parts for those.”

    Winn had to agree with that at least, and he did so with a grumbling sort of nod.

    Everything from heavy equipment to livestock sales went through the Shreveport-Bossier auction yard. Once a month there was an opportunity to bid on equipment that was either repossessed or made available by way of estate sale— whatever the case may be, it was how Kara pinched pennies whenever it came to the construction equipment her crew needed in order to complete certain jobs. Buying straight from the dealer was almost never an option, not if she wanted to buy out the company she worked for within the next hundred years. She already had a small fortune saved, but in some cases, she knew that there was a need to spend money in order to make money. The more trailered equipment she owned (and the less that she leased) only earned her company higher paying jobs. Kara sought to make herself Harrison County’s first call concerning all municipal maintenance and repairs, as well as landscaping, line clearing, road rebuilding, you name it. Holding the top spot on the bidder’s list was quite the feather in her cap, for sure. She never had to undersell a job, either. She’d get it done right, and for the right price. The cigar smoking gentleman that owned majority shares in the company she managed were always pleased as punch with her, but it was Kara’s every intention to goad them into an early retirement within the next year or so.

    “Wanna come over and hang out for a bit at mine?” She asked, shrugging back towards Winn once he’d lagged far enough behind. “I’ve got a new lure I’ve been dying to try out.”

    “Sure…” Winn hummed a short note as if to mull the idea over, “As long as you bait my hooks. Slimy things,” He shook his head, “Not my scene.”

    “I always bait your hooks, doofus.”

    “And my freezer full of catfish fillets thanks you for it.”

    It was a fine day for a spot of fishing, to be sure. If Kara was lucky —which remained to be seen, for as long as she could remember she didn’t have bad luck, or good luck, it was simply that she had no luck at all— she could spend the rest of the week on the pitted, loose boards of her dock with her favorite rod and tackle, and maybe a six pack of beer to boot. She’d surely try to catch more than just a buzz… Kara smiled towards a cloudy sky at the mere thought. Life was good, but fishing was always better.

    The parking lot was less full than it was that morning, but there were still signs of equipment demonstrations going on from where Kara and Winn were walking. A pristine John Deere combine towered over all of the smaller tractor pieces, its iconic green paint like a proud flag amongst all of the dilapidated seeders and cultivators.

    Kara could smell it before she could see it— the foul, pitch black of asphalt mixing from a tar snake machine. Smell it she did, and hear it as well; it gave a godawful grinding noise from its cylindrical tumbler, catching every half turn and spitting smoke like an ornery, cartoonish bull.

    Both of them held their noses as they walked by, eager to pass it up quickly.

    But unfortunately, they weren’t quick enough.

    The damned and derelict machine chugged loudly at those who stood nearby it, and before Kara could even see it coming, it slung swaths of four-hundred-degree tar in a wide circle around them, causing the men trying their best to temper it back down to run in the complete opposite direction. A glob of steaming pitch landed with a hot -slap- against the inside of Kara’s wrist, and down she went to her knees in a mad scramble to scrape it off against the denim of her jeans. It seared her immediately and sizzled angrily into the top layers of her skin. Most of it had stuck to her like glue, though just the size of a half-dollar. Had she been standing any closer, it might have landed somewhere a bit more devastating… like her face, or her neck.

    Winn wasted no time at all shouting at the men responsible, charging at them with a head hot enough to try swinging a punch or two. “What the hell’s the matter with—”

    “Winn!” Kara hissed, clutching her wrist tightly against her chest. Two tears had leapt from her eyes and ran hot tracks down each of her cheeks. The pain had forced them out; she was still too busy trying to get the burning to stop. “Don’t- I’m alright.”

    He ran back and tried pulling her hand away to take a look, “Let me see,” He reached for her again, a sense of panic layering the sound of his voice, “Let me see!”

    Kara choked back a gasp once he introduced her burn to fresh air again. It was deep and red and already blistered well beyond anything she’d ever experienced in the way of burns before. Winn shook his head as he muttered a few colorful curses. Once Kara made it to her feet again, she went to shrug towards her Jeep on the other side of the lot. “My water bottle—”

    “C’mon.” Winn took her by the other wrist, and they began to run.

     

     

    There was no convincing Winn just to take her home after that.

    Granted, the likely second-degree burn on the inside of her wrist warranted more in the way of treatment than a mere dousing with left-over water. There was burn gel to be had at hospitals, that and cooling gauze, pain medication, and a certain smile like a spring sunrise…

    Kara swallowed down her trepidation as the sliding doors to the Shreveport ER opened up in front of her. She gave a quick wave towards the intake nurse and then made an attempt to lean over the counter before they could type very much into the keys of their computer, “I don’t need to go all the way back today, just a quick look and a bandage maybe?” She flashed her wrist then, to which the admitting nurse gave a wide-eyed and particularly surprised look at. “Please?” Kara begged, “Just in and out, if it’s possible.”

    Wait, what am I saying…?

    Eventually, the nurse nodded and waved her back to the small triage room at the front of the ER. The door to the hall on the other side had been left open, which provided a clear view of the nurse’s station. Just her luck. Kara peered as inconspicuously as she could towards the busy scurrying of one ER nurse after another, hoping yet not hoping to see one dark haired beauty in particular.

    I don’t get it.

    Why am I still avoiding her?

    Be an adult, Danvers. Get a grip.

    There were several minutes where Kara was left alone to her wallowing while an errand for pharmacy grade burn gel was made, and who should happen to pass the doorway while her head was hung low to her lap, but Lena herself. She seemed to have an uncanny knack for catching Kara unawares… She rapped quietly there against the open door with her knuckles, and Kara looked up; a deer caught in headlights.

    Gone all was her sense of self-pity. Lena should happen to smile at her and it was like the world went right side up again.

    “Hi.” Lena said, giving a shy bite of her bottom lip into her teeth.

    Kara couldn’t help but to smile back. “Hi.”

    “Doing alright?”

    “Eh. Got in a fight with an asphalt mixer.”

    “Let me guess… You lost?”

    Kara chuckled. “Oh, ye of little faith.”

    Lena took a quick look over her shoulder and then chose to saunter in. “Can I see?”

    “I mean, if that’s how you get your kicks. Sure.”

    Lena trapped a harp of a giggle somewhere in her throat once Kara obliged her the stretch of her arm. “Tell me, have you always been this dashing?”

    Kara shook her head. “I have no idea what you mean.”

    Lena sucked in a quick breath at the sight of Kara’s welted burn mark. It would heal with time, of course. It might even leave a glob-shaped scar in its place too, but all of that mattered little with the way Lena’s thumbs smoothed gently into the palm of Kara’s hand.

    Kara felt more relief with that single gesture than any amount of burn gel could ever offer.

    “Ouch…” Lena remarked with an empathetic tone. “How does it feel?”

    Kara nodded, slightly obtuse, “Said they might have to amputate.”

    “Poor baby.”

    “Yeah, it sucks.” She grinned, “That’s my good hand.”

    “I’m sure you’re just as skilled with your other.” Lena replied without a drop of hesitation, and then her cheeks began to flush with a rosy color. She released her tender hold of Kara’s hand then, almost as if she hadn’t meant to say such a thing at all. Not out loud, at least. “Somebody’s got you taken care of?” She asked with a sweeping gesture of her chin out into the hall.

    Kara felt the need to lick over her lips in order to speak again; the inside of her mouth now barren and dry like the floor of a desert. “Mhm.” She hummed in reply.

    “Okay.” Lena stepped lightly onto her toes towards the door again as she took herself out and back into the hall, “Bye.”

    “…Bye.”

    Kara hung her head again.

    God.

    She’s like puppies, and sunsets, and ice-cold beer all wrapped into—

    The brisk -swish- of hospital scrub pants returning with more gauze and burn ointment broke Kara out of her heart-eyed daze. She supposed the trip hadn’t been all bad, after all. Not for whatever reasons she still couldn’t make heads or tails of… Maybe she could stuff down that giddy, light-headed, weak-at-the-knees feeling she got whenever Lena graced her field of vision to a deep and hidden place somewhere within her subconscious. It would all pass eventually, right? Besides, they were adults. Kara knew restraint if Lena did. She was sure of it.

    Okay, almost sure of it.

    Trying certainly couldn’t hurt. Could it?

    On her way back to Winn somewhere in the oak tree littered parking lot, the sound of running footsteps grew in number behind her. Kara hadn’t made it ten steps out the front doors before Lena had come chasing after her, huffing and puffing for breath with her apparently hasty decision to head Kara off.

    Small wisps of her hair frayed out from the tight bun at the back of her head, shining with an almost brownish hue now under the light from the sun. Lord, how much prettier she was in the sunshine… Kara’s knees turned traitor once again. It was no small miracle that she managed to keep herself upright.

    “Hi, again—” Lena panted through another gorgeous smile. Her dimples were incredible in so much natural light. Kara could hardly tear her eyes away. “Can I see your phone for a second?” She asked.

    “Um, sure…”

    Lena didn’t take long at all to tap a series of numbers quickly into the face of Kara’s phone. Then —just as quickly as she had run out into the lot— she handed it back and then waved another hurried goodbye. “Okay, bye. Again.”

    And just like that, she was off towards the front of the ER with a bounce in her step.

    Kara looked down. A small green heart emoji accompanied Lena’s name and number; the newest entry in her contact list.

    “Lena Luthor.” Kara mused as she plundered her way happily towards her Jeep, nearly running smack into a tree first. “…Pretty.”

     

     

    —One Week Later—

     

    -ding- -ding-

    Kara’s phone chirped from the tall cabinet of her entertainment center as she rummaged around inside her small broom closet. It was less a place of storage for actual brooms and more of a place to keep her many fishing rods, the twenty-some-odd reels that she did have and used religiously for different parts of the Caddo. Her neck of the bayou was home to some good catfishing, chock full of flatheads the size of her leg, and blues that put up quite the fight. Kara possessed a Xena-like obsession for fishing; an inside joke known only amongst those that were closest to her.

    She opened a text message from Lena, having lost count by now just how many they had each sent back and forth over the past week. They had been messaging nonstop from the time she awoke to the time she laid her head down at night, with, of course, hours in between where Lena was busy working. Kara’s phone stayed no more than a short distance away. Attached to the infernal thing, she was. It was where her favorite nurse lived, after all. Her crush of a thousand burning suns, the woman of her dreams…

    Lena had sent another selfie to celebrate the end of yet another long shift. She’d made an adorable face as if she were exhausted, and Kara laughed. Errant locks of Lena’s hair the color of midnight seemed to stray loose from the messy clip at the back of her head, denoting her relentless exertion for the better part of twelve hours. Kara typed back quickly.

    Glad to be going home?

    Lena responded with rapid-fire speed.

    God, yes.

    Wouldn’t blame you if you slept the whole weekend after that.

    I won’t.

    No?

    No, I’d like to see you.

    Kara froze momentarily with the thought of seeing Lena again outside of a hospital setting. Taking her mind off her sense of surprise, she rattled around one reel after another in search of the anvil rod she used for bank fishing, its medium-heavy action fiberglass making it the prime contender for large mud cats. Before she could come up with a reply, though, her phone gave another two dings with a follow-up text.

    Would you like to see me?

    She may as well just forge ahead with the truth. Words always seemed easier to say if she just allowed herself the room to mean them. What was the point of getting to know someone if you constantly felt cradled within your own mind, or apprehensive of how you might be received? Kara supposed, if anything, she would just carry on by example. That was how Lena seemed to do it… She was direct. Most of the time, anyway. Kara’s mind tumbled backwards to all those shy smiles; the way Lena bit the insides of her cheeks but had absolutely no idea that her dimples all but gave her away. It was endearing. A breath of fresh air, really. There wasn’t any pretense or secrecy laden in their constant messaging. Lena was perfectly easy to talk to, so Kara figured that she would only respond in kind.

    Absolutely.
     What do you want to do?

    I don’t care.
     What do you like to do on the weekends?

    Kara smiled as she snapped a quick photo of all of her fishing rods tucked away in the corner of her small closet. The net from a handled catch net and the tiered stack of tackle boxes filled her with a sense of pride; These were the things that made her happy. Of course she would want to share them with Lena.

    I should have guessed. Lol

    So, how about it?

    Fishing?

    Yes, but more precisely, fishing with me.

    So, the opposite of boring, I’m sure.

    Bingo.

    Will you show me how?

    Of course.

    There was a short pause in their back-and-forth, nearly long enough for Kara to backpedal the idea of fishing altogether, but Lena eventually responded— this time with another selfie. She was sitting in her car, with her head held at a pretty tilt and with her hand beneath her chin as if she were pondering intently. It came with a caption…

    Okay. Sure.

    Kara pumped her fist so quickly that she nearly stumbled. The pull string of the lightbulb in her tackle closet tickled along her nose on her way out, and she gave it a quick -yank- before kicking the door shut behind her.

    Don’t worry.
    If you don’t like it, we can go catch a movie or something.

    That sounds a lot like a date, Kara Danvers.

    Yeah, that’s because I’m asking.

    So you are.
     See you this evening, then?

    I’ll send you the directions in a bit.
    Easy to get lost out here.
     Get some sleep.

    Will do.
    Goodnight.

    Night.

    Standing now in her living room again, Kara gave a wide look around at the state of her humble abode. Empty beer bottles, pizza boxes and a smattering of tools laid upon almost every surface. When was the last time she’d cleaned in here?

    Nice, Danvers.

    Ask the pretty nurse out and the first place you think of is a frog-infested swamp?

    She at least had the day to get her house in order. They’d spend most of their time on the water, anyway. Kara focused on one surface and then another, and before long, she was a whirlwind of rags and feather dusters. The place would be spotless, even if Lena weren’t around long enough to so much as step inside.

     

     

    Once the small trolling motor ceased its droning through the murky depths and they were free of both the bank and its thick swath of lily pads, Kara aimed to put just one bobber in the water so that she could focus the bulk of her attention upon the woman sitting in front of her.

    The swamping boat they lounged in was constructed almost entirely out of plywood sheets and epoxy, its squarish hull being maneuverable enough in size to navigate crowded, cloudy waters. The sprawling, palm-shaped roots of all the cypress trees made motorized propulsion nearly impossible in some parts, but that’s what Kara’s skinny wooden oars were for. This part of the lake wasn’t more than twenty feet deep, anyway. There was the odd -thump- at the muddy water’s surface that gave way to sets of ripples every now and again, the fish below coming up for one mayfly-sized chomp after another, but for the most part, the Caddo was glass, and the sun was warm, and the fish were biting.

    “This is nice.” Lena said, letting her hair fall back as she smiled towards the sun.

    Kara admired the way she seemed to glow, as if her sense of happiness was made manifest beneath every inch of her fair skin. Lena was painfully beautiful, so much so that Kara had the damndest time just getting herself to keep a watchful eye upon her bobber in the water.

    “You think so?”
