    “Mhm.” Lena hummed with a nod and then shook out her dark and wavy tresses again. “I like sometimes just to do nothing but exist. It’s so quiet out here, too.”

    “Most of the time it is.” Kara replied. There were rings of bubbles emanating at the water’s surface near a low hanging branch, right where the straggly ends of some Spanish moss dangled to meet it. Kara knew it to be signs of fishing lying in wait, and she slowly reeled in her line to cast it there instead. “The bugs usually just drive you a little crazy, but since its cooled off some, there aren’t so many. The fish get sleepy earlier, though.”

    Lena dragged her head up again to smirk in Kara’s direction. “You almost sound sad.”

    Kara shrugged. “Eh. Less to do in the fall, I guess.”

    “Have you lived here your whole life?”

    “Is it that obvious?”

    Lena huffed a laugh at that. “Well, yes.” Then another shy smile worked its way from ear to ear, “You’re cute.”

    “What about you? You said you’d only recently moved here. Where from?”

    “California. New York before that.”

    “Really…?” Kara gave an interested raise of her brow, “Why on earth would you come all the way out here- er, to Shreveport anyway?”

    “I suppose I just wanted a fresh start. Somewhere I could do some good and put in hard work at the same time.”

    “Admirable.”

    Lena gave another great big yawn then, her fourth or fifth since they’d rounded the bayou’s bend and drifted further into the tree-laden lake. Kara chanced a small brush of her hand against Lena’s shoulder in the hopes that she might keep her eyes open.

    “Don’t fall asleep out here…”

    Lena chuckled and then wiggled to sit up a bit more straight against the boat’s short edge, “Why? Is something going to come and snatch me away if I do?”

    “I take it you haven’t heard of the Little Yawning Girl yet.”

    “No, but that sounds terrifying.” Lena chewed on the inside of her lip for a moment, “Tell me more.”

    “I forget that Shreveport’s such a big city, sometimes. Y’all get mostly hoodoo voodoo stuff out that way, but out here it’s just something else people say to keep each other safe- fairy tales and spooky stories, stuff like that.”

    “I’ll admit it. I’ve never lived somewhere where there’s so many ghost stories.”

    “That’s because everything’s so old. It’s hard to separate truth from fiction most of the time.”

    Lena swept some of her hair behind an ear, blinking fondly at Kara amidst the waning sunlight, “I’m sure a lot of it’s rooted in truth, though.”

    “A lot of people would agree with you on that point. Like the story of the Little Yawning Girl, for instance. Legend has it that a long, long time ago, right after the Great Raft—”

    “What’s the Great Raft?”

    Kara chose to reel in her line just to set aside her fishing for the day. Talking was much more enjoyable, as it turned out. “Oh, that’s just what people call the logjam that dammed up the river and made the lake we’re sitting on. Somewhere like the early eighteen hundreds…”

    “Wow.”

    “But the story goes like this… A little girl and her dad went fishing one day, deep on the west end of the Caddo, right about where we are now, and for all his worth, the girl’s father just couldn’t get her to stay awake. The sunshine on her face had made her warm and the placid water of the lake had made her sleepy, and she yawned, and yawned, and yawned… So, the dad just decided to let her sleep while he caught their dinner.”

    Lena crossed her arms and rested them on her knees as she listened. It seemed that Kara had her completely spellbound.

    “Once the sun went down, he noticed that all of the yawning had stopped, and when he turned around in their boat to check on his little girl… she was gone.”

    “Gone?” Lena asked with a held breath.

    “Yup. She’d vanished into thin air, right under his nose. It was said that he’d spent every day after that out on the water in search of her, come rain or shine, following what he claimed were the sounds of yawns, but most who had tried to help him said that he’d gone mad, and that it was just the wind through all of the trees driving him even more crazy.”

    “What happened to him?”

    Kara gave a vague sort of frown. “Nobody knows. Or maybe I’ve just never heard the end of the story… Either way, I heard it a lot growing up whenever I went fishing with my dad. Scared me awake every time. I don’t blame him, though. It’s not safe to not be on your guard so far out on the water. They say that if you fall asleep on your boat out here, the Little Yawning Girl will come to take you to an in-between place where you get to watch all the people who love you look all over creation for you, but will never find you.”

    “Yeah.” Lena racked her spine with a shiver. “I was right. That’s fucking terrifying.”

    Kara laughed. “It’s just a story people tell their kids to make ‘em act right. You gotta learn early on to respect the danger here.”

    “No kidding.”

    Lena twisted some of the frayed ends of her denim cut-offs and sighed. She seemed to be at peace once again, and Kara fought hard against the thought of her eventually leaving once the day was over. She wanted more than anything to spend more time with her… But the sun was setting, and they’d need to make it back to the dock before long.

    Kara had just one more idea in mind… It wouldn’t take long.

    “We should head back soon, but do you wanna see something cool first?”

    “Um, duh?” Lena teased.

    “Alright.” Kara took each of the long oars out from along the bottom of the boat. “The sound of the troll motor scares them off, so I’m gonna row us there.”

    “…Scares what off?”

    “You’ll see. It’s just around another few bends.”

    “Okay.” Lena pursed her lips to hide another smile. “I’m trusting you.”

    Kara’s heart soared. “A decision you won’t regret. I promise.”

    A little further east and along the southern bank Kara rowed, making long and slow sweeps with her oars to pull them along the lazy water. “Golden Hour” along this part of the Caddo was a sight to behold, especially with what Kara had in mind… Before long, the trees began to spread further and further apart until a clearing came into view, maybe a hundred yards from side to side of nothing but mirrored water and one very large, very stately (at one time, anyway) paddlewheel steamboat. Lena gasped as they turned the last bend into the pond-ish clearing. It was something like out of an old movie. Or Kara thought so, at least. There were hundreds of stark white cranes perched along the vessel’s pillared stacks, as well as its grand paddled wheel attached to its stern. They spied the approaching boat quietly as Kara continued to row them closer.

    “Oh, my God. Is that—”

    “Really old? Possibly haunted?” Kara quipped, “Yes, on both accounts.”

    “It’s amazing… Look at the size of the wheel!” Lena rocked the small boat they sat in with how quickly she spun around to stare, “How long do you think it’s been here like this?”

    Kara smiled and gave a proud point of her arm towards the bow of the massive, double-decked passenger boat. There in fanciful, bold lettering were the words “Washington” and “1861”. Kara was thrilled that she’d earned herself another surprised gasp. “Every once in a blue moon, somebody funds an expedition all the way out here to take it back to some museum somewhere, but they always give up just short of destroying it. Better to leave it here as it is. Right where nature intended.”

    Lena continued running her eyes back and forth over all of it. “Look at all the birds…”

    This was the part that Kara liked the most.

    “You ready?” She asked, her heart jamming its way up and into her throat.

    Lena turned to look back at her, “Ready for what?”

    Kara brought both of her hands together all of a sudden with a loud, echoing -clap-, and all of the leggy, sleeping cranes that were resting peacefully upon the Washington’s upper deck burst into flight at once, flapping their wings with loud, honking calls of discontent. The sky filled with a feathery whiteness and thusly plunged the entire clearing into shadow. Lena laughed with a hand held to her chest. Kara couldn’t believe how much she enjoyed the sound of it. Lena’s laughter, that is. It was wonderful just to be the cause of it. A few moments later the cranes were gone, most choosing to seek other accommodations while some circled back to alight upon the boat’s wreckage once again.

    Lena turned around again and nodded vigorously with her satisfaction. “Okay, that was cool.” She admitted. “You get to do this all the time, too. I’m jealous.”

    Kara gave a hopeful quirk of her chin. “You’re welcome to it. Any time.”

    “What, to come out here and do nothing?”

    “Mhm.”

    “That sounds really nice, actually.” Lena rolled her lips into her mouth and then leaned forward to hug her knees again. “You sure you wouldn’t mind? I work crazy hours; I’d probably run you ragged.”

    “You’d be worth it.” Kara cleared her throat quickly after that, leaping towards the next possible thing to say. “I’ll take us the long way ‘round back to the house, it’s prettier when the sun peeks through all the trees this time of day.”

    Lena turned to lay back once more, but this time she leaned all the way to rest her shoulders squarely in the middle of Kara’s lap. “Is this okay?”

    “As long as you’re comfortable, Your Majesty.”

    “I am, thank you.” Lena agreed with a chipper voice.

    The way Lena seemed to be so at ease here made Kara feel almost light-headed. Had that been her way of asking to come back a second time? A third? It wasn’t hard to see the appeal in “doing nothing” with such a demanding job. Getting away from the chaos of life was what the Caddo was good for. It offered another world with which one could find escape in, and Kara had known it her entire life. Sharing it with somebody else suddenly made all the more sense.

    The water eventually skinnied out into a winding trail of tree-spotted shallows, and where the next turn became wide again, and a bit deeper as well, Kara remembered the small floating dock of a popular swimming hole that was just a short distance away. Posted warning signs to keep watch for alligators passed them up, and then the sound of a -splash- piqued Kara’s curiosity at last. Laughter filtered through the ends of the trees as she rowed them around, just a little bit closer. She hadn’t seen anyone use this swimming hole in years, not since she and her sister were young and—

    “Is that who I think it is?” Lena shot up straight and gave a hard squint into the distance at what appeared to be two brown-haired women swimming very close to one another. They seemed to be more than friendly. Lena gasped, “I can’t believe it… That’s Maggie.”

    One of the women splashing her way into a warm embrace seemed to have a reddish, almost auburn shine to her hair, and a noticeably familiar frame about her, as well. Kara bowed her head and pinched firmly at the bridge of her nose, wishing now to wash and wring out her brain for what it had just witnessed. “And that just so happens to be my sister.”

    Lena’s eyes went wide with that as she covered her mouth with her hand; an attempt to stifle her rising laughter. She shook her head, asking whether or not it was funny… Kara merely replied in kind with a stiff shake of her head “no” and an added (though slightly over-the-top) shiver up along her spine. Lena couldn’t help herself. She began to giggle from behind one hand as she shooed them in the opposite direction with her other…

    “Quick, go! That way!” She squealed quietly, peeking over her shoulder a few more times as Kara rowed them back the way they came. There was a bout of silence after that where Lena was very polite and very understanding of Kara’s rather confounded state.

    “Let’s make a deal.” Kara said, batting her eyelids as if to fling herself far from the image that was likely burned there. “I’ll agree to never mention what we just saw if you do the same.”

    “What, my coworker and your sister skinny-dipping with a bunch of alligators?”

    “Gators haven’t hung around that swimming hole since we were kids- and Jesus, you’re already bad at this.”

    Lena laughed out loud again. Suddenly, Kara was feeling much better. Lena gave a cute tilt of her head and then sighed agreeingly, “Okay.” Then she swiped her finger across her chest with another dimpled smile, “Cross my heart.”

    “…Thank you.”

    It’s not like they had seen anything, anyway. Maybe Alex and Maggie had been kissing? It had been hard to tell from so far away. Regardless, it wasn’t exactly how Kara had planned to end her first date with Lena. And it was for damn sure none of their business, as well. By the time they’d docked again just down the bank from Kara’s house, the last of the sun had dropped behind the horizon and washed the sky with a deep and hazy red color. It was cool for the end of October, but not so much that it set a chill upon their skin.

    “Here…” Kara reached down to help Lena out of the swamper, and with what was more than likely a mere lack of experience climbing in and out of boats, Lena lingered too long between her step up to the dock. The boat began to glide out from underneath her. On instinct, Kara bent down to scoop Lena up by her middle and pulled her to safety to stand on the dock with her— but in an overestimation of her strength (as per usual) she’d pulled too hard and brought Lena crashing against her front with a small “oof” sound.

    Lena’s hands had alighted upon the tops of Kara’s shoulders in an effort to brace herself, and she blinked rapidly in surprise for how close they were now. She showed no signs of wanting to free herself from Kara’s firm grasp and seemed merely content to flit her eyes between Kara’s baby blues instead. Or maybe she was just stuck there, much like Kara herself was. Lena’s jaw dropped just enough to part her lips, almost as if she felt the need to say something, but there they stood, wrapped up tight and speechless for sheer want of each other. Kara thought she should let her go…

    Now I’ve gone and done it.

    She probably never wants to come back now.

    Kara’s voice felt breathy for how quietly she had said the words, “Are you scared?”

    “Of you?” Lena asked.

    “Yes. Of me.”

    The features upon Lena’s face seemed to soften again, as if she were melting— her defenses crestfallen and bared to the world now. She smoothed a hand up along the side of Kara’s neck and went to lay a gentle palm against her cheek, sighing yet again. Something about her countenance made Kara feel much less nervous to still be holding her so close. There was a moment where Lena just looked at her, rather adoringly, like she’d been doing so for her whole life, and then she leaned closer…

    “No.” She replied. Her eyes fell to Kara’s lips. “I’m anything but scared of you.”

    Kara had little time to react with the way Lena closed the distance between them. She’d brought herself up onto her toes and sifted her fingers deeper into Kara’s hair, only to press their lips together with a steady breath through her nose. The feeling of it as it skirted across Kara’s cheekbone made her feel more weak in the knees than she’d ever been, and soon she found herself taking a step back along the dock, and then another. Kara pawed her way to Lena’s waist the longer they kissed like that. She wanted to hold her tightly, and to feel the way her stomach fluttered against her own. The sound of a breath passed between them before long, and Kara flailed with a hand to keep them from walking right off the dock and into the water. But the dock, however, seemed to have something else in mind.

    A sharp pain ran through the heel of Kara’s hand and she winced reactively, taking it back from the post she’d caught their weight upon. A long sliver of wood ran out from the base of her thumb as she began to bleed there. Not a lot, just enough to drip a few times against the warped planks of her fishing dock once the shard was pulled free. Lena was quick to lay her hands upon Kara again, this time hoping to soothe some of the pain radiating from her palm. It felt better now that there was no longer anything sticking out of it, at least.

    Kara gave a quiet groan whenever Lena applied a bit of pressure to staunch the bleeding. Not much seems to faze this one… She thought to herself.

    Lena shook her head and huffed an astonished laugh, “I do not understand how you do that…”

    “Do what?”

    The off-duty ER nurse merely bobbed their clasped hands between them in response.

    “Ah.” Kara replied with a sheepish grin, “It’s a talent, really.”

    “I’ll say.” Lena looked up again and then froze, staring longways just past Kara’s shoulder. Immobilized by what looked like fear, all the color drained from her cheeks in an instant. “Kara…” She whispered.

    Kara twisted around to see what the matter was, but felt a wave of relief at what stared back at them from along the pinecone littered tree line. Dog gave a furry tilt of his head, seemingly concerned with Kara’s new form of company. There weren’t many other people besides Winn who he’d bother to show up for, and Winn himself would be the first to admit it; he was scared shitless of Dog.

    “Don’t worry, that’s just Dog.” Kara craned her head to capture Lena’s gaze again. She seemed to settle a bit with that, but remained just a shade apprehensive. “Probably best not to make any sudden movements though.” Kara added, “In fact, just stay still until he leaves. He’s just checking in.”

    Lena nodded with an altogether clumsy flapping of her mouth, “Dog—” She huffed, “You have a wolfdog. Why did I not expect as much?”

    Kara grinned. “You can tell he’s part wolf? Oh, he’ll like that- don’t let him hear you.”

    “He’s huge, Kara. Do you just have zero sense of self-preservation, or what?”

    “I do just fine, thank you.” Kara chuckled. “And he only comes around every once in a while. I think he can track me by the scent of my blood, though. Probably just wanted to see what was up.”

    “That’s… comforting.”

    “Don’t be afraid.” Kara pulled Lena’s focus again by the point of her chin. Her fantastically blue-green eyes flitted to her in an instant, and she swallowed the rest of her misgivings down with a throaty -gulp-. “Don’t be afraid.” Kara repeated. “Just look at me.”

    “Okay.” Lena replied shakily, “What do you think he’ll do if I kiss you again?”

    “Mm… I guess there’s only one way to find out.”

    Lena seemed to burst with a quiet laugh then, as if the dam of her anxiety had finally spilled open. “You are something else, you know that?”

    “So I’ve been told.”

    Kara looked back just as Dog went to paw around his nose, scratching there idly with an itchy sort of -snort-, and then he licked his jowls closed with a great, toothy -chomp-. Appearing to be satisfied with his surveying of Kara’s sprawling back forty, he stood up on all four legs again and wandered off into the wood whence he came. Lena let go of the breath she’d been holding and then dropped her brow against the plane of Kara’s sturdy collarbone.

    “Holy shit. Well,” She lifted her head then with an attempt at nonchalance, “He seems nice.”

    “Just do me a favor?” Kara asked.

    “Okay?”

    “Don’t mention to anyone that you saw him?”

    “As if anyone would believe me.” Lena admitted with a laugh.

    “Do you still wanna hang out after all that? I get it if it’s a bit much—"

    “I do.” Lena leaned up onto her toes once more, giving Kara a quick peck of a kiss upon her cheek. “I’m already looking forward to doing absolutely nothing with you again.”

     

     

    The bullfrogs croaked loud and monotonous from where Kara rocked upon her back porch, palming her phone back and forth between her hands.

    Lena hadn’t texted her back in hours. Not since she’d left Kara’s house earlier that evening. Kara was busy thinking to herself (more like commiserating with herself) that maybe Lena had just been nice long enough to get herself the hell out of Uncertain. Maybe Kara was beating herself up for nothing, though… Maybe Lena had gotten home and went straight to sleep. The more reasonable part of her brain begged the latter.

    The -scrape- and -tack- of large paws as they ambled their way up the tall, wooden stairs of Kara’s back porch gave way to a mass of reddish-brown fur, along with a pair of large, yellow eyes that seemed to glow under the filtered cover of moonlight. Dog turned thrice in a small circle upon the dingy mat that he likened to a “bed” at the end of Kara’s porch and then flopped down there with a beastly grumble— not so much as a “growl”, as everything that came out of his mouth sounded like something straight out of The Hound of the Baskervilles. After so many years of just sitting in silence with him, whether it was night or day, Kara knew that he meant her no harm whatsoever. She still wouldn’t dare to pet him, though. Dog was to remain wild. This was how they shared a healthy level of respect for one another… by leaving each other the hell alone.

    Kara had begun to lean more towards the fact that Lena had just fallen asleep, and that she would probably text her the next day like usual, when her eyes began to droop heavily with the thought of sleep. She was tired, that much was certain with the way she slunk crookedly down into the seat of her rocking chair. She craned her head back with a long, breathy yawn…

    …and then came the sound of another. Only it hadn’t come from her at all.

    Kara’s eyes shot wide open again, alert and aware. Dog had lifted his head from his mat, too. He seemed interested in staring down towards something near the flat surface of a tree stump that Kara had felled the year before. Both of his deep yellow eyes trained hard there, and he began to float upwards onto all fours again with a hair-raising and bellied growl. Kara’s heart began to pound.

    Nope.

    Time to call it a night.

    She leaned forward out of her chair slowly and then pointed towards the ground down below. Her fingers gave a patient -snap- and with one command, Dog was off like a bolt of lightning down the stairs again. “Git’em…” She hissed.

    His claws scraped and scratched all the way down, and Kara could even hear the slap of his giant paws as they met the bare earth once he tore off across the yard. She didn’t wait around to see what had raised his hackles in the first place, apart from the unexpected sound of another (very human) yawn, that is. She tried not thinking of it as she scurried across the wood of her porch towards the back door. Living here for so long had bled that kind of curiosity right out of her. She didn’t need to know.

    There was a ringing that came from her back pocket once she was safely inside her house, and Kara gave out a surprised -yelp- at the sound of it. She made a quick swipe at her phone to get it to stop— It was Lena calling.

    The heavy gallop that was the intense beating of her heart began to slow. Kara pulled in a deep breath and tapped her screen to answer.

    “Hey, what’s up?”

    Hey?

    What’s up…?

    Kara smacked the flat of her uninjured palm against her forehead.

    “So,” Lena began, “There’s this TV show we could watch, if you’re interested. Something about vampires and werewolves right here in Shreveport.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    Full Up with Sugar

    Chapter Summary

    Lena spends time at Kara's house on the Caddo, doing mostly nothing.

    Chapter Notes

    Hello, happy weekend. Chapter title implies a sweetness. Be wary of cavities. >;D

    Chapter Five: Full Up with Sugar

     

    Winter on the Caddo was a fairly uneventful time, as far as winters go.

    A wool-lined coat with an outer covering like denim worked wonders to insulate the body, paired with the occasional beanie or gloves to keep both head and hands warm. But with the cold weather came a stagnation in activity. Much to Kara’s chagrin, there would be no fishing until early February, at least. Long walks around the tall pines with Dog would suffice here and there— Though, truth be told, it was the boredom that sought to unhinge her from ear to ear. That was normal for this time of year.

    But this winter in particular was anything but.

    Kara’s homely lake cottage welcomed regular visits from Lena. Before shifts, after shifts, drawing long weekends, as well as short ones… She never stayed over, though, and always made a point to leave just after dark. Sometimes this meant entire days spent on Kara’s couch, snuggled up in a mess of fleece blankets and hot chocolates with one episode after another of their current binge.

    The frequent “looking forward to” kept Kara in high spirits. Days on the calendar began to fly by. Weeks passed. The bitter cold had dug in its heels. Kara began to wonder if she should broach the subject of her and Lena’s growing affections, as they’d not surpassed what she could only (indelicately) refer to as “second base”. But the thing of it was, she wasn’t sure if she wanted to in the first place. Not yet, anyway. Kara liked making out on her couch. She liked it on her back porch too, when it wasn’t so cold, that is. They’d kissed in her kitchen, and at the small lakeside firepit at her back forty, and in her Jeep— Oh, so many times in the Jeep.

    What they hadn’t done was round third and head for home.

    Surprisingly enough, Kara was alright with it.

    Sure, there were occasions from her past (which she wouldn’t quite categorize as “relationships”) where sex was a hardy staple, but like most things that were very good and plenty, they tended to burn up quickly. The flings had ended just as fast as they’d began. The more Kara thought about them all, the more she realized that none of them really knew her to begin with. What was her favorite color? Day of the week she was born on? Childhood pet name, and furthermore, why she named them what she did as opposed to how many she had. Kara began to doubt very much whether or not any of her old paramours could even recall her middle name, or the fact that she actually didn’t have one at all. No, Kara was settled on it. Lena would make clear her intentions before long, but until then, she was perfectly content just to make available her lips for kissing, and her hands for petting. Things were just as they were meant to be— As were most weekends they spent sheltering with one another from the bite of frosty mornings.

    “I’ve brought you sugar.” Lena said as she stomped the remnants of frozen mud from her tall, rubber boots at Kara’s front door. They went to perch neatly beside one another next to the scratchy hemp welcome mat, and the rusted spring from the screen door objected noisily once Lena stepped inside.

    “Yeah? Show me.”

    Lena kissed Kara upon her cheek with a bounce from her toes and then smiled. Something she held behind her back seemed to give her cause to giggle. Once Kara furrowed her brow in question of Lena’s burgeoning secrecy, she presented her hands out in front of herself. In them sat a five-pound sack of granulated white sugar.

    Kara’s brow gave an unsubtle raise with her flooding realization. “Oh, sugar sugar.”

    Lena giggled again. As it turned out, she fancied herself quite the minx whenever it came to wordplay. “For your coffee and my tea,” She explained, “But don’t worry, I can supply the metaphorical kind as well.”

    Her downy North Face jacket shrugged its way onto an antlered coat hook, leaving her in a bright blue cable knit sweater with a turtleneck for a collar, and a pair of cozy black leggings. Lena was the perfect wintery contrast to Kara’s black, unzipped hoodie and dark wash denim jeans.

    “You are most generous, Lena Luthor.” Kara replied as she ran her hands about Lena’s waist, staggering her feet apart just a bit to further to bring her in nice and tight. Lena hummed with a warm note and seemed happy to situate herself inside of Kara’s embrace again after a long night shift at the Shreveport ER. “That reminds me—” Kara went on, “What’s your middle name, anyway?”

    She’d sort of blurted the question, but Lena merely blushed at her asking. Kara had deliberated for some time how best to go about getting to know the woman who made herself at home here for the past several weekends in a row. When all was said and done, she concluded that the simple act of “asking” was truly the best way forward. Lena dimpled her cheeks with yet another happy smile.

    “It’s Kieran.”

    “Wow…” Kara squeezed her once more with a long sigh through her nose, “Super pretty.”

    “What about you? Wait, no, let me guess—” Lena pursed her lips, “Kara ‘Cautious’ Danvers…”

    Kara began to laugh. “You think so?”

    “No… Daring.” Lena continued.

    “Very funny.”

    “I know,” Lena gasped, “Risky—"

    Kara cherished the way Lena squealed inside the stretch of her arms just as she went to dig her fingertips in at her sides. The tactile push-and-pull to escape her tickling was as earthly and wonderful a feeling as it was a symphony of sounds— a giddy screech, a gasp for air, a heehaw of a laugh. Lena’s hands once again found purchase at the tops of Kara’s broad shoulders and she squirmed her way upwards to steel her defenses with a kiss, sighing audibly there once the ticklish strumming of Kara’s fingers had been pacified. Her strings were plucked easily enough, Kara had but to stroke once over the delicate ends of Lena’s ribcage, her hands having now somehow traversed up inside of her sweater. They stilled for a moment in awe of one another; a single, weak-kneed breath apart.

    Lena hummed sweetly against Kara’s lips and then whispered with her contentedness, “I missed you.”

    “I don’t understand how a week can be so long.” Kara whispered back. She’d not yet opened her eyes. It was better that way, being deprived of one sense after another save touch.

    “Poor, poor baby…”

    “I don’t wanna do anything but kiss you all the way ‘til Monday.”

    “Do you think that will hold you over?”

    Kara shook her head and then pushed in for another long kiss, discarding the bag of sugar with a careful lob into the kitchen, “No.”

    The way Lena mewled into her mouth, calm and quiet and full of a familiar and well-established intimacy… Some days, that was all they did. Kiss. Heavy, and fluid, and passionate, barely bothering to actually watch whatever was on the tv. Today, however, Kara’s hand had been laid against the soft skin of Lena’s torso. Daring indeed, Kara had put it there without much forethought but found that it fit perfectly whilst spacing her fingers betwixt the long rows of Lena’s ribs. A deep and slightly anxious breath took up space there, billowing Lena’s chest outward like a full sail to catch the wind. She wanted Kara to touch her…

    …and Kara would acquiesce only too happily.

    Backed up into the kitchen, the pairs of their feet fought in tandem to keep them upright until Lena’s hip met the edge of a counter. A small -squeak- of surprise slipped into Kara’s mouth with the rest of her pretty, whimpering noises… She was lithe, and loose, and practically vibrating everywhere that Kara touched. Kara’s heart shot like a fist into the middle of her throat, pounding like the swing of a hammer against anvil. They were a barreling train with full steam, pawing and whining and scratching for more track— but damn it all if Kara’s phone didn’t chime with a loud and interrupting alert. She groaned blithely into the smooth crook of Lena’s neck which was slender like that of a swan’s… Pulling herself away was hard, but she was mindful of any noise that her phone made lately. She’d not heard from Winn in several days, closer now to a week if she were counting. Which she was. Winn always texted her back eventually, but his lack of a response had begun to weigh heavily upon her mind.

    Kara reached to thumb at the screen of her phone and then grumbled when she found that it was only a spam email; one that made it through her many filtered settings.

    “What’s the matter?” Lena asked, running both hands down the firm wall of muscle that spanned Kara’s back.

    “It’s Winn. He hasn’t called me back in days… I think something’s wrong.”

    “Okay—” Lena scooted out from between Kara and the counter. She went to grab her jacket from its curved antler tine near the front door and gestured with her brow towards the wintery wonderland that was just outside. “Let’s go.”

    “Go?”

    “We’re checking on him, right?” Lena asked as she ran her arm through a sleeve. It seemed less of a question and more of a statement of fact. Kara nodded, catching up to her now.

    “Yeah- Okay. We should just go and check, just for a minute.”

    “No offense, but if you say he’s as half as stubborn as you are…” Lena trailed off with her insinuation.

    Kara agreed more fervently that time and decided to make for her coat near the door as well. “Well, when you put it like that, I should have checked on him yesterday.”

    “It’s alright. We’re doing it now. That’s what counts.” Lena gave a reminding flourish of her hand back towards the kitchen counter just as Kara was halfway out the door, “Keys- keys!”

    Kara chuckled, backtracking with nimble steps and a quick kiss at the edge of Lena’s lips, “Heh… Thanks”

     

     

    “Winn?” Kara called into the house once she’d run her key into the lock. “We’re coming in…”

    Where Kara’s home was a mishmash of scattered laundry and tackle boxes, Winn’s was the very same but in the way of computer parts and things of a soldering nature. Winn Schott was an engineer of sorts— A laborer by day, but an inventor by night. He’d managed to secure one patent for some sort of electromagnetic field reader and had been chasing a second for years after that. One patent in a lifetime was more than most, Kara had reassured him of as much, but the man was persistent, if anything. He had a brain that just wouldn’t quit, as well as an attitude to make things work whether they wanted to or not. It kept him busy enough in his spare time, if the heaps of spare parts and jungle of cables that ran throughout the halls of his large home were any indication. There was enough space to house a family here, but Winn chose to live alone. Something about his father’s shadow… Kara never got to see much of Winn’s family, even when they were children. She was all the family he needed, apparently.

    “I didn’t exactly picture ‘mansion’ on our way over here…” Lena admitted with a broad gesture of her hands.

    “Yeah,” Kara replied, taking up the wide pile of mail from beneath the slot at the front door. “He’s good with machines. Made a mint off some ghost reader thingy—”

    “An EMF reader?”

    Kara chuckled. “Exactly. Also, you watch too much tv.”

    “Hey, I’m handy to have around during trivia nights. Just you wait and see.”

    Lena went to catch Kara by the elbow just as she was about to stride into something hanging longways off an arm of a chair. It looked to be part vacuum, part backpack at first glance. Kara juked around its nozzled end and thought immediately of Ghost Busters— a film they must have watched together as kids more than a hundred times over. Kara tossed the mail in yet another clumsy stack upon an end table and then cupped her hands to holler up the grand staircase towards the second floor.

    “Winn!”

    A cough came back. Deep and chested, riddled with phlegm. Kara and Lena both spared a surprised look at one another before scaling the wide treads of the stairs. Winn’s bedroom was a short jog towards the north side of the house, which was more of a manor, really, since it had actual wings that acted more like storage rooms for his many inventions. Kara swung into the open door and stared at Winn where he lay huddled in his bed. He was curled up into a ball and fastidiously hugging a pillow as if it were of a mind to abandon him at a moment’s notice. He sweat profusely upon his brow and appeared to be just a shade warmer than that of the white sheets of his bed.

    Lena was at his side immediately, cradling his wrist to get the measure of his pulse. Kara was at least satisfied to find him exactly as she feared; Almost once a year Winn got sick, and almost always during the winter. He had brains to spare, to be sure, but he also possessed the immune system of a fruit fly.

    “Eliza? Where the devil are my slippers?” Win muttered to himself, blinking one eye slowly after the other. His eyes seemed distant and most definitely bloodshot, likely from a high fever.

    Lena palmed his damp forehead and looked back up at Kara, her expression hinting at her level of concern, “Who’s Eliza?”

    “That’s my mom’s name, but he more than likely just has the flu.”

    “I assume that means he’s prone to getting sick… You’re sure it’s the flu?”

    Kara nodded just as Winn unclutched his pillow with one hand to wag a pale finger in the air, “I'll have you know, Doolittle, that Mr. Higgins' intentions are entirely honorable!”

    Lena reared her head back in surprise, unsure of whether to laugh or not. “He’s delirious.”

    “Yeah.” Kara kneeled down to swipe some of his mussed hair from his eyes, “If he’s quoting My Fair Lady, then it’s definitely the flu.”

    “Okay… I’m still a little worried about how warm he is. How objectionable do you think he would be towards a visit to the ER?”

    Kara shrugged, “Not very, but they usually just churn him out a few hours later after telling him, ‘yep, you’ve got the flu, be sure to drink plenty of fluids’. I usually just stay with him until his fever breaks and then I get some food in him.”

    “Alright.” Lena pulled at the sleeves of her coat. “Would you mind getting me a few things? Something cold, like a wet cloth with some ice, and any Tylenol you can find, just not Aspirin…”

    “Sure. I’ll be right back.” Kara went to take herself back downstairs but turned around upon the heel of her boot just as she reached Winn’s bedroom door. “Hey, thanks for this. For…” She shrugged again, “Being here.”

    “Of course.” Lena smiled.

     

     

    It was a hair past two o’clock in the morning before Winn was coherent enough to speak in full sentences that were of his own volition, and not that of 1950’s Broadway era musicals.

    Lena was apparently so exhausted that Kara had pulled back up to the house, shut off the engine of her Jeep, and walked around to try and wake her before she stirred. She ended up coming to on her own, rubbing just beneath her eyes like she did whenever she was tired, yet always mindful of her dark eyeliner. “We’re here already?”

    “You slept the whole way across town. Which, isn’t all that far, considering—”

    “Alright,” Lena yawned, sitting up further in her seat now to pat over her pockets, “Where did I put my keys…”

    “Oh, no.”

    “What?”

    Kara shook her head. “You’ve been up over twenty-four hours, last time I checked.”

    “It’s enough to keep me—”

    “Runnin’ on fumes? Asleep behind the wheel?” Kara crossed her arms. There was no way she was letting Lena drive half an hour into Shreveport on so little sleep.

    Lena sighed and gave pretty bats of her dark eyelashes at Kara. “No- You’re right.” Then she dropped her head back tiredly and tucked her hands between her knees as if she were too comfortable to get out and walk.

    “Stay here tonight. Sleep.” Kara begged.

    Lena nodded with another tear-jerking yawn. “M’kay.”

    Since the house stood on stilts, much like the rest of the structures that sat so close to the lake’s wetland-type flood plains, that meant that there were stairs in their near future. Kara didn’t want to have to urge Lena to stay the rest of the night just to make her climb an ungodly number of steps, so she did the only logical thing that came to mind, of course.

    She reached inside the Jeep and slid one arm beneath Lena’s knees, and the other behind her shoulders. Taking her from her seat in one swift motion was easier than expected— Lena all but clung fast to her and without a hint of objection as well. She laid her sleepy head against Kara’s shoulder, leaving it to rock back and forth there as they took the tall flight of steps up to the front door. Soon enough, Kara deposited Lena beneath the covers of her bed and was able to take her jacket and shoes from her without much of a fuss, and by the time that she was done, Lena was good and asleep again. Kara was no stranger to sleeping on her own couch— She just knew that it was too forward or too bold to climb into bed beside the other woman without at least discussing the idea first. Lena’s rest was Kara’s top priority. They could chew on the implications of said idea later, after she’d slept for a full eight hours, at least.

    Kara hardly remembered letting her head hit the small, lumpy pillow that lived on her couch before she succumbed to her own tiredness as well. She slept hard, feeling as though it were a mere blink and she were awake again— Lena stood just beneath the threshold of Kara’s bedroom door, her hand holding her weight upon the river rock-lined mantel of the fireplace. Kara sat up. “What’s wrong?” She asked.

    Lena had traded her sweater and leggings for one of Kara’s collared shirts. She’d bothered to at least button it halfway down. Her legs were bare, though the shirt covered her down to her mid-thigh. Its long sleeves dwarfed her slightly shorter arms, rumpled in their middles from where Kara had a habit of rolling them up by the cuff.

    “It’s too quiet.” Lena said.

    “Yeah,” Kara pinched some of the sleep from the corners of her eyes, “Sorry- No bugs in the winter.”

    “I heard some coyotes earlier.”

    “Nothing to be scared of.”

    “Kara?”

    “Hm?”

    Chapter Seven: The Chef in 29A

A lifetime of guilt and “I should have’s” had made Alex hard, but not hard enough to where she couldn’t gentle her more prickly nature whenever it mattered. Alex went to give an endearing squeeze at Kara’s shoulder.

    “Listen… We’re doing everything we can, but we’re only half a dozen strong right now. Budget cuts have us running pretty thin. I want to do more, but my hands are tied.”

    “I know.” Kara replied. “It’s okay.”

    “If it makes you feel any better, we’re working with the governor’s office to raise the penalty for trapping in the off-season. There’ll be real jailtime involved for these assholes if things go our way.”

    “Well, at least that’s—” Something like a loud scratch against wood cut Kara short just then. She whipped her head around, listening for the repeated -scritch- sounds as they came in loud and clear. “What’s that?” She asked.

    Alex gave a noncommittal frown and averted her eyes, guilty as sin. “What?”

    The sound of scratching came again, this time with a lonely and pitiful mewl.

    Kara stood up from the table, “…That.”

    “Weird. I don’t hear anything. It’s probably just- wait- Kara—”

    Alex flung herself in front of her pantry door, blocking it with a sheepish grin and a matching shrug of her shoulders. She huffed an anxious breath, “Promise you won’t tell?”

    “Who the hell am I gonna tell?” Kara dove past her once they began to fumble for the knob, “Lemme see! You always hide the super cute ones…”

    “That’s because the super cute ones always have super sharp—"

    Kara managed to shove her sister out of the way and then opened the door, just as a small cat-shaped flash of tawny brown fur and black spots shot right between their legs.

    “…claws.”

    Kara gasped animatedly. “Holy crap. Is that a panther cub?!”

    “She’s about ten weeks,” Alex replied as she scrambled after the sapphire-eyed cub, “And teething like you wouldn’t believe.” She managed to scoop her up into her arms finally and then expertly dodged a flat, retracted paw which went with a -thump- right against her chest. “She likes the ducks.”

    Kara squealed and went to tickle at the tiny predator cat’s soft underbelly, “I’m sure you mean she likes them for breakfast. Isn’t that right cutie—"

    The cub’s whiskered upper lip scrunched then with a snarl, and an almost pathetic sort of whistling sound came out in the form of a hiss. She was small in the way of a panther, yes, but large enough to be mistaken for a domesticated house cat. If domesticated house cats had incredibly long tails, bright, sky blue orbs for eyes, and paw pads the size of catcher’s mitts. She had some razor-sharp claws as well, Kara didn’t doubt that for a second, but the cub seemed only slightly miffed to be captured and held aloft like a furry, rambunctious child.

    “Let me guess, you were supposed to hand her over already?” Kara assumed.

    “She’s just so cute…” Alex drawled, “I can’t help it.”

     “You’re gonna get in trouble again.”

    “It’s a slap on the wrist, Kara. No biggie.” Alex nuzzled the cub a bit more and then dropped her back into the newspaper lined pantry with a swift swing of the door. “Besides, I was bringing her in this afternoon. We kept her over the weekend to make sure she was fit for transport.”

    Kara quirked her chin, “We?”

    “Yeah, Maggie named her—” Alex suddenly clapped her mouth shut, and then her shoulders drooped with an obvious air of sadness. She shook her head, seemingly uninterested in finishing her sentence.

    Kara could tell that something was wrong. She knew her sister. “Oh, no. I know that look. What’s up?”

    Alex frowned again and then cast her eyes down towards her feet. Her hands went with a shove inside the pockets of her khaki-colored tactical pants, and she sighed. “We sort of broke up. I mean, I think we did.”

    “You mean you’re not sure?”

    “I don’t know.” Alex looked back up with a squint, “I- we—” Then she blustered for a moment, “I think we just want different things.”

    “I’ve never known that to stop you.”

    Alex went to sit down in one of the chairs at her slightly off-kilter kitchen table with an awkward -fwump-. “As it turns out,” She began to explain, “In order to get to know someone, you have to talk to them first. Like really talk. Not just ‘hi, how was your day’ ‘oh, fine thanks, how was yours’.” She threw her hands up into the air then as if nothing else in the world could have made any more sense, “Kara, I miss her so much…”

    “Then just talk to her.” Kara replied as she sat across from her. The cub in the pantry made a small yowling noise right about then, apparently displeased to be shut up by herself again.

    “That’s the thing, though.” Alex went on, “I don’t know if she wants to. Our relationship was mostly a physical one—”

    “We can just skip over that part.”

    “I mean, I didn’t even know she was married…”

    “She’s married?” Kara gawked.

    Alex gave some dismissive waves of her hands, “No, no… Not now. She used to be. To a man.”

    “That’s not exactly unheard of—”

    “How can your heart tell you that you love someone when you don’t even really know them? Is that so incredibly naïve of me? Or just crazy…?”

    Kara deflated a bit with that and paused for just a moment. Her sister’s despairing fell by the wayside; She had heard the most important part. “You love her?”

    “Yeah.” Alex’s cheeks had begun to flush slightly red, as did the whites of her eyes. She apparently felt like crying, and it broke Kara’s heart to see it. “And now she’s gone.”

    “Have you tried calling her?”

    “…No.”

    There was a moment where Kara felt the urge just to hurl advice at her sister, but then she realized that she didn’t exactly have any to give. Apart from Lena, she’d not done much in the way of “talking” with any of her past relationships, either. That’s when it hit her…

    Lena knew Kara’s favorite color. She also knew about her absent middle name. Her childhood broken bones, her favorite meals, her favorite brand of beer, her opinions on films— She even knew what truly scared her. Lena knew that Kara’s mother’s name was Eliza, and that her father’s name was Jeremiah, and that they’d both been gone for years now. She also knew that her best friend Winn had practically lived at their house while they were growing up because his own parents apparently couldn’t be bothered to notice just how skinny he was.

    And in return, Kara barely knew about a thing about Lena beyond the fact that she was a nurse who worked in emergency medicine.

    Why hadn’t she just asked? She was comfortable enough in doing so by now, but why hadn’t she made more of an effort? Kara suddenly felt very selfish, and very foolish to boot. She slunk out of her chair to go and kneel in front of her sister, laying her head there within the space of her lap.

    “It’ll be alright…”

    “I’m just sad, Kara.” Alex said with a sniffle, “I’ll be fine.”

    Kara nodded. “I know.”

     

     

    “Something tells me your apartment is very nice if you get to take an elevator up to it.” Kara said with a meek grin.

    The invitation to Lena’s high-rise apartment in Shreveport was secured easily enough— Some earnest and repetitive puppy-dog eyes were apparently all that it took. Kara knew it was a long overdue landmark in their growing relationship… She should have found her way here months ago. There had been ample opportunity for visiting, it was just that Kara had been careless (or otherwise mindless) whenever it came to the time they spent with one another. She sought to change that, starting today.

    “It’s comfortable.” Lena replied. “What did you think about the movie?”

    They had just come from seeing a film at one of Louisiana’s only drive-in theaters. Kara chuckled, knowing full well that they hadn’t actually watched much of the movie at all. It could have been Ferris Bueller’s Day Off for all she knew— She’d spent more time fighting with the seats in her Jeep just to recline.

    “What movie?” Kara asked as she wrapped a long arm around Lena’s waist. It earned her a pretty smile, and at least one dimple.

    The elevator gave a -ding- before long and then worked to slide its door open wide, offering a view of the long hall with warm sconce lighting and commercially manufactured landscape artwork. Kara’s first thought was to leap at the question of how in the hell anyone got their furniture all the way up here…

    “I just need to make one quick stop first.” Lena said as she darted out of the elevator and around to their left.

    Kara followed her to a door numbered 29A and then waited once she’d given a quick rap of her knuckles against it. Even the way she knocked at a door was pretty. Crisp, repetitive, and polite. An elderly black gentleman with an oxygen tank in tow eventually greeted them with a full-dentured smile, and then he waved them both inside. Kara stood very still in the entryway of the gentleman’s apartment, keeping herself as small and as quiet as possible. He didn’t seem to mind the extra warm body in his home, as his attention was wholly focused upon Lena and the rummaging around she did in her shoulder bag. They seemed friendly enough with one another… Almost as if Lena made herself a regular guest here. He spoke with a rich Creole accent that made his sense of happiness seem nearly twice as big as himself. Kara tried to keep from eavesdropping too much and instead focused on her surroundings. There were endless picture frames upon the walls that depicted a happy couple, some black and white in composition and others in vivid color. A husband and wife— though, the gentleman did seem to have been living on his own for quite some time.

    True to her word, the errand didn’t take long. Lena had produced a small handful of pill bottles and gave them over with some instructions; how many, with or without food, this one at nighttime, this one in the morning, and to call her if he needed anything. The man thanked her profusely and then shuffled in his slippers to see them out. Kara could only guess at what had just taken place…

    “He seems nice.”

    “He is.” Lena replied with a cheerful note, taking Kara’s hand into her own to whisk her back in the other direction down the hall. “My favorite neighbor by far.”

    Since Kara was now of the mind to ask more questions in a true effort to get to know Lena, she at least thought better of the first few of them to happen upon her lips. Perhaps it was best to tread carefully around certain subjects. She would learn more in time. Or maybe, Lena would volunteer the information all on her own. Kara wasn’t exactly subtle when her sense of curiosity abounded…

    “You’re not going to ask what that was all about?”

    “Do you want me to—?” Kara shook her head, “I mean, it’s just- I guess I’m not sure if I was supposed to see that or not.”

    The sound of a key running into a lock brought Kara’s attention forward again, and her jaw fell slack at the apartment that opened up in front of her. It held sleek, modern furnishments. All of it in a pleasing yet monochromatic palette. There were tall, marbled vases that held fluffy white pampas grass, and the accompanying statuettes along with them seemed to be an artform all of their own— something Kara couldn’t quite describe in words. The lighting was a bright white color that drenched every surface, giving it all a sterile yet incredibly old sort of feeling. Lena’s apartment felt very Roman; a shade of well-to-do that had caught Kara completely off-guard. She was stunned. Of all the things she’d expected to see here, none of it was what she had pictured…

    “Wow.” Kara drawled, “Comfortable, she says.”

    “Do you want something to drink?” Lena asked.

    “Uh, water, I guess.”

    “No, don’t be boring.” Lena shook her head, taking two crystal glasses from a shining silver tray. She poured into them from a decanter and then handed one to Kara, “Here.”

    “Thanks.”

    Lena smiled. “So?”

    “So…” Kara sipped quietly. Whatever it was tasted incredibly expensive. “Your neighbor?”

    Lena smiled again. “He makes a great breakfast quiche. Brought me one right after I moved in. His wife passed away some years back and ever since he’s been living on his own. He was a chef for most of his life. Quite the card player, too.”

    “Is that right?” Kara ran her fingers through her hair, pulling it to one side and down over her shoulder.

    Lena nodded. “He says he’d like nothing more than to make breakfast for the both of us more often- if, of course, we lived in a world where my work hours weren’t so crazy- but his arthritis makes doing anything in a kitchen almost impossible.” She looked down at her glass and thumbed patiently there around its rim. “He can’t afford the meds he needs.”

    “But I’m guessing you can?”

    “I…” Lena buffeted her lips with a guilty breath, “Not exactly. I borrow them from work.”

    Kara tried her best to limit her look of surprise. “…Oh.”

    “Does that change your opinion of me?”

    “No.” Kara was very quick to answer as she placed her glass down upon an iron-ribbed trunk. “No, it doesn’t. It just tells me a little bit more about you.”

    “You’re looking at me differently.”

    “I don’t mean to.”

    “I know.”

    Kara sighed. “What happens if you get caught?”

    “I get suspended.” Lena huffed a laugh, “Or fired… Or worse. I would pay for them, but the medicine that does him the most amount of good is a bit out of my price range.” Kara looked around in awe of the inside of Lena’s apartment, and Lena laughed again, “The truth is that most of this came with me whenever I moved here, and the guy that owns the building gave me a break on the rent because I helped his daughter with her asthma. Natural remedies can often work wonders for respiratory conditions. She’s fine now.”

    “So, you’re… formerly rich?” Kara went as far to hazard another guess.

    “I come from money, yes.” Lena replied, “I also do a lot of things that I shouldn’t, but I guess that just runs in my blood.”

    “What do you mean?”

    “I mean that my family made their fortune by making things that hurt people, and I wanted so badly to have nothing to do with it that I moved all the way to the other side of the country. They cut me off, but it was worth it. Now I just try to make up for all the pain they’ve caused.”

    Kara was suddenly rocked with the urge to put her arms around Lena. So, she did. She hugged her tightly, and loved the way that Lena responded in kind. They were silent and still with one another, all except for the sounds of their deep and contented breaths and the back and forth running of their hands.

    “I get it now.” Kara said near Lena’s cheek.

    “Get what?”

    “That unwavering part of you that not only wants to help- but needs to help. I understand where it comes from now.”

    “Do you…?” Lena’s voice painted quite the picture; it pitched helplessly as if she were near to crying.

    Kara nodded and pressed a firm kiss against Lena’s temple. “No matter what, or where, or who you came from, the part of you that’s good is worth more than all the money in the world. The part of you that’s good is the part that I—” Kara stopped herself short, careful not to let her mouth run away with itself, “Well, it’s the only part that matters.”

    Lena took another deep breath with the full use of her shoulders and then drew her head away to look Kara straight in the eye. Her cheeks were awash with a rose color, and her eyes were beset with a precious sort of glistening. “Would you like to see my bedroom now, handsome?”

    Kara nodded her head up and down playfully, nearly throwing her glasses from the bridge of her nose in the process. She pushed them back up with a shy finger and then smiled from ear to ear, “Sure.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Thanks for reading <3

    The Haunting of Blackberry Cabin

    Chapter Summary

    Kara and Lena enjoy a Juneteenth parade together.

    Chapter Notes

    Fair warning, this will probably be the scariest chapter in the story. (Also, the additional tags have been updated.)

    Chapter Eight: The Haunting of Blackberry Cabin

     

    Like clockwork, each and every year, Juneteenth was a fine day to be outside.

    Food, bands, parades, and more were all things to enjoy on the crisp, summer-cusp day— June 19th being the celebratory event which marked the end of American slavery. It was an occasion to promote community cohesiveness, to educate, and to remember. Kara loved the fire department’s annual fireworks display for its simple irony; the force tasked with putting fires out also happened to be just as skilled at putting them on.

    “There’s Hank- Hank!” Kara shouted from the sidewalk. She waved excitedly with one hand and tugged Lena along with her other.

    An older black man with a short, cropped afro waved back at them. He was turning what looked like boudin sausages on a charcoal grill and wore bright red suspenders that held up a pair of yellow, flame-resistant pants. The emblem over the breast of his t-shirt read Uncertain Fire Department. Kara’s eager smile grew wider as they approached the city’s one faded fire engine: Cindy, named after a more serpent-like version of the Caddo Lake Monster.

    “Salut, ma chéri!” He said once they gathered around the grill. “Ki moun sa bel zanmi ou la?”

    Kara shrugged shyly towards Lena, “This is Lena. She’s an ER nurse over in Shreveport. Lena, meet Hank Henshaw.”

    “Oh, la! You’re from Shreveport?” Hank gave a proud point towards his chest with his barbeque tongs, “Grew up in N’awlins, but I moved to Bossier parish after the Flood.” He stuck his other hand out with a grand sense of politeness, “It’s nice to meet you.”

    “Likewise.” Lena replied with a cheerful shake of his hand.

    “I met Hank here at a crawfish boil in Bossier.” Kara explained, “Convinced him that we needed more good men like him on this side of the Caddo. That was, what, four years ago?”

    “Senk.” Hank replied with one hand splayed to the number five.

    With that, Lena gave a curious quirk of her chin towards Kara, “Do you speak Creole?”

    Kara merely scrunched her nose as a vague attempt at an answer. Truthfully, the words sounded very square-shaped and awkward coming out of her mouth, which was why she avoided speaking the language unless absolutely necessary, but she understood it well enough.  

    “That would be my fault,” Hank chimed in whilst turning another sausage, “English is not my first language- but Kara asked to learn. Makes the ‘getting along’ get along, konprann? Turns out she’s a great- kijan ou di—” He fumbled for a moment before gesturing towards his eyes, “…pupil?”

    Kara laughed. “Don’t believe a word he says. I’m a terrible student.”

    “Some words don’t translate, but we still understand each other just fine.” Hank added.

    Lena folded her arms over her flowy blouse and then gave a twist of her torso in Kara’s direction again, “I’m impressed.”

    “Yeah?”

    “Mhm.”

    “Something to eat?” Hank asked them both, waving his tongs towards the grilling sausages, “Vini, manje, selebre.”

    Kara looked back at Lena and gave a small shrug of her shoulders, “Hungry yet? Or do you wanna walk some more first?”

    Lena nodded briefly, “I’d like to see the rest of the parade- I’ll have worked up an appetite by then.”

    “Alright.”

    “It was great to meet you, Hank.” She said towards the smiling firefighter, “I’m sure we’ll be back shortly. I’d love a plate.”

    He gave a humble bow in reply, “Men wi! Bon moman, you two- pass a good time!”

    Main Street being not all that long of a road to begin with, the many tightly spaced shops that inhabited it made for a quick journey from start to finish. The music faded soon into the distance. With the parading march come and gone, the road gave its long bend under the train tracks and stretched towards faster avenues, and higher speeds. Eventually, you’d hit the highway if you just kept driving…

    Lena darted in and out of Kara’s hand for a moment to help an elderly woman step down from the rubbled curb. It was a shame that she had to do so in the first place— Kara was keen on restoring some of the brick laid roads down this end of the historic street, but moving contracts along that involved getting stamps of approval from preservation offices took eons. Tree resin could amber in less time, but Kara knew just what she would do with the place if given half the chance. A wheelchair accessible sidewalk here, brighter lamp lights there… She would save what amenities she could whilst giving the project a boost from the twenty first century. It was still years in the making, unfortunately, though she did like to dream. Most of the time, certain projects just came down to a vote. Kara was adding more ideas to the blueprints which lived in her mind when Lena leapt from the curb to bounce happily down a skinny alleyway. Its wrought iron gate was rusted open, and Kara recognized it immediately. The thought of where it led made a chill run up her spine.

    “Look at that—” Lena gasped towards the lonely cabin in the distance, “It’s beautiful. Can anyone just walk up to it and look around?”

    “Mhm.” Kara nodded, rolling her lips into her mouth. Lena tugged at her arm again, but her feet stayed firmly planted on the other side of the gate. Kara’s hesitation was noticed straight away.

    “What’s wrong?” Lena asked.

    “Nothing.”

    “Kara…”

    “What?”

    “Has anyone ever told you that you’re a terrible liar?”

    “Yes, actually. Literally everyone I’ve ever known… And now you.”

    “Oh, baby…” Lena cooed with a small pout. She went to wrap her hands around Kara’s face and kissed her there from cheek to cheek, “I’m sorry. I didn’t mean it.”

    Kara smiled as she felt a warmth race beneath her cheekbones. She was blushing. Funny how she was still doing that almost a year later… “I just don’t come down this way very often.” She admitted.

    Lena twisted to look back at the cabin, and then again at Kara. “Because, let me guess, its haunted?” She asked with a small huff of a laugh.

    “You laugh now, but nobody’s touched this place in over a hundred years for a reason.”

    “Except for you.” Lena guessed.

    “And my sister. And Winn. And a few others…”

    “I want to get a better look, but I won’t be scared if you’re with me.”

    “I dunno…”

    “Please?” Lena begged.

    Lord, this woman knew how to bend Kara to her will. Kara considered for a moment just to turn back and head for the street, but with one last look down the dilapidated alley and the brambled dirt path that lay beyond it, she eventually set her jaw and gave a quick, affirmative tuck of her chin.

    “It is actually really cool—” Kara held a finger up between them to forewarn Lena’s resulting bounce of excitement, “But if we break anything, we were never there. Okay? The place has been falling apart for years.”

    “Ooh, rebel Kara. Sexy.” Lena gave a playful bite of her lip, “Alright. Deal.”

    Kara sighed. “Just be glad it’s still the daytime.”

    Lena laughed as they began to slowly amble towards the cabin. “I can tell that you’re not really afraid, by the way.”

    “Mm, it’s that very clear line between fear and respect I keep telling you about. It just gives me a bit of the heebie-jeebies, that’s all.”

    “So, I take it you’re pretty familiar with it?”

    “Kind of.” Kara replied as she stepped over an old wooden chair that was turned upon its face at the edge of the path. The overgrown yard was littered with similar furnishings; a split table, more broken chairs, a bullseye lantern missing its glass… “It’s sort of a rite of passage for kids around here- to go in and then come back out. Alex still holds the record for the most speed runs through the front door and out the back. She went upstairs one time… Came back down as white as a sheet. Never told me what happened.”

    “How old were you?”

    “I dunno, I remember not quite being in high school just yet.”

    Lena stopped once they reached the rickety wooden steps up towards the front. “It doesn’t seem so scary…”

    “Like I said, the sun’s still up.” Kara chuckled. “We usually did our trespassing at night.”

    For what it was worth, Kara didn’t feel much of a clambering in her bones to run away like she had when she was younger. Looking at it now, it was just a rotted, old log cabin with a missing front door. Wildflowers had grown up through the grooves of the porch, and large, thorny bushes full of blackberries encased the place around its every corner. Kara was certain of it after a while of just standing there; there was, in fact, nothing to be afraid of.

    “I wonder who used to live here…” Lena mentioned as she craned her neck to get a better view of the inside.

    “The deed on file says it belonged to a commissioned officer from the civil war.” Lena gave an almost comical turn of her head to look back at Kara with wide, surprised eyes. Kara merely shrugged. “What? Contractor, remember?”

    Lena smiled. “So, you snuck a peek at some old papers. What’s the real story?”

    Kara spied the tattered curtains from one of the upstairs windows as they gave a sudden shift, but chalked it up (and quickly too, lest her imagination run away with itself) to a wayward breeze.

    “I always heard it that after the war, he was granted this plot of land for his commission- a lot of black men enlisted back then as a way to ensure their freedom, as well as their citizenship. Being on paper was priceless during those days. Anyway, he waited and waited for a love of his to show up after the fighting was over, but it turned out that she never came.”

    “That’s more sad than it is scary.” Lena admitted.

    “Yeah. It is.”

    Lena took a step up onto the porch, and then another. “Let’s go inside.” She said it as if it were nothing.

    “…What?”

    “I just want to see what all the fuss is about—”

    “You said you wanted to get a better look. I thought you meant from the outside.”

    “I’m not going to tease you, if that’s what you’re thinking.”

    “No.” Kara shook her head, “That’s not—”

    “I’ll be quick, then.”

    Before Kara could get another word in edgeways, Lena was across the porch and through the opening of the cabin’s front door. A shudder terminated at the base of Kara’s neck, but after a strict word with her feet, she was up the steps and following Lena inside against her better judgement. Better to be near her, she figured— in case anything come falling down on top of them.

    Kara took a deep breath once the floorboards began to creak underfoot. “Just be careful…”

    Lena was palming the smooth, egg-shaped banister end of the cabin’s narrow staircase, admiring it for its workmanship and many years of supposed use. Kara knew it to be hand-carved, having set her own eyes upon it many years ago.

    “You can just feel how old it all is.” Lena said.

    Kara felt like hurrying her, but ultimately, she didn’t wish to push. It wasn’t a matter of maintaining her usual stoic demeanor— There would be no shame at all in bolting for the door. She just didn’t want to linger any longer than necessary. Respect was owed here at the very least, if sheer terror wasn’t enough.

    “Just try not to lean too heavily on anything and stay under these load bearing beams, and no matter what, don’t even think about taking—” Kara gave a sharp twist at the sound of another creaking step. Lena was at least two full strides up the brittle steps of the staircase. “…the stairs.”

    “Seems sturdy.” Lena shrugged as she continued climbing.

    “Those are some famous last words if I’ve ever heard any. Gee, and I really liked you, too.”

    “Come on, goof.” Lena offered a hand back.

    Kara had to force herself to swallow past the hard lump in her throat as she followed suit; She knew that leaving Lena by herself was out of the question. Stepping near the anchored ends of each tread seem to make the stairs protest just a bit less with their combined weight. To her great relief, the staircase had indeed been built to last. There were two doors at the top of the small landing when they arrived there; one which laid open towards what looked like a bedroom, and another which remained closed. Kara had absolutely no interest in learning what was behind door number two. They made their way into the wide bedroom before long, and Kara felt another note of relief at the fact that it was, actually, just the wind coming through the curtains. What panes of glass that were left inside the frame of each window seemed to keep naught but the pinecones from dropping in off the branches hanging just outside. There was a bird’s nest laying in the candle space of a chandelier that was left to wrack and ruin upon the middle of the floor, and Lena bent at her knees to study it a bit closer. She huffed a laugh and looked up at Kara then, a look of delight spread clean across her face.

    “What’s that mean?”

    Kara puzzled for a moment. “What does what mean?”

    “That song you were just singing… More Creole?”

    “Uh, I can barely string a sentence together let alone sing anything.”

    “Then…” Lena stood up, “Wait. I could have sworn—"

    Suddenly, the sound of the creaking that they’d just confirmed belonged to the stairs made them both turn for the door.

    It was shut.

    Kara felt a tingling sensation strike the flesh of her palms, “Did you close the door?”

    “I didn’t touch it.” Lena replied quickly. Her voice was both light and feathery; a welling up of terror was close at hand.

    More creaking steps began to reach the top of the stairs, and without thinking, Kara darted for the door with her hand outstretched for its dingy, brass knob. She stopped just short of it.

    The sound of feet had shuffled to a halt just on the other side.

    With her heart in her throat again, Kara turned to grab Lena by her elbow and ran with her behind the slowly wafting curtains. They draped long towards the floor, concealing them both with nothing but the bat of an eyelash between them. Lena’s quickened breath was hot upon Kara’s cheek, and she muttered something akin to “what was that?” whenever the brass knob of the door gave a turning -squeak-. Kara squeezed her eyes shut and pressed her their foreheads together. She held her breath and decided just to wait for the strangeness to pass. Maybe someone had followed them up? It wouldn't be unlike some of the townsfolk to stick their noses where it didn't belong.

    They stood there huddled and wrapped around one another inside the curtains until Lena’s bones ran stiff with a jolt. Kara opened her eyes again to see that she was staring straight into them, wide and terrified.

    “Do you hear that?” She whispered.

    Kara managed to give a very quick and careful shake of her head “no”. Whatever it was, she couldn’t hear it… Not until the sound of far-away singing began to echo in and out of the room.

    Creole is probably the language that I understand the least, but I've heard it a lot at different times in my life- depending on where I lived. Cajun, Haitian Creole, and Louisiana French, you'll hear it all in the pineywoods region of east Texas... And more so the further south you end up. I miss the food.

    Thank God for St. John's Wort and Sisters

    Chapter Summary

    Kara experiences the closest of calls.

    Chapter Notes

    For those of you who have read Morning Glories All the Way Up: Firstly, thank you. Secondly, I have recycled a few lines from there and incorporated them (nearly verbatim) into this chapter. Without going too much into detail, these words mean a lot to me. I hope it's alright that I've reused them. I don't see myself doing so a third time for future stories. Anyway, please enjoy.

    TW: blood, violence, animal injury/non-fatal

    Chapter Nine: Thank God for St. John’s Wort and Sisters

     

    Now that trapping season was officially over, Kara usually felt a bit of relief emanating through the woods that surrounded her home… But not this year.

    So far, both she and her sister had tripped all of five snares during their long walk through the wooded west end of the Caddo. Kara had seen them all year long it seemed, and had been anxiously awaiting the end of the season so that she could breathe easy once again. Much to her chagrin, the poaching persisted, just as it had in the run-up to spring. It was all she could do to swallow down her anger, but picking small herbs for Lena along the way seemed to help. Alex noticed all of Kara’s bending and plucking and decided to unwind their comfortable silence with more questions.

    “What about that one?”

    Kara scrunched her nose in inspection of the knotted cluster of white bulbs in her hand. “Boneset, I think… Makes you sweat out a fever.”

    “Huh.” Alex replied. “Could’ve used some of that when you were a kid. I’m curious, do you remember being sick at all?”

    Kara shook her head as she stuffed the plant into the front pocket of her jeans. “No.”

    “I thought not. You were really little, but you woke up with a fever one day that just wouldn’t quit. Ended up being my first time in a hospital.”

    “You have a way of doin’ that.” Alex joked. “It was actually pretty scary, though. Mom and Dad were scared, so I was terrified. But you were sick for days, and then one morning I came back in the room and you were awake and jumping on your bed like nothing was wrong.”

    “Seriously? Sounds like me.”

    “Yeah, it was super weird.”

    “I’m not jumpy—”

    “If we were being followed, I’d know.”

    “What?”

    “Nothing.” Kara held her hands up in surrender, “You’re right.”

    “Seriously, what’s the problem? You’re creeping me out.”

It was more of a feeling, if anything, one that didn’t much scare her now in her adulthood. She’d had many years of practice traipsing all throughout these woods while something breathed patiently down her nape. She would leave it alone if it only did the same for her in return. Whoever it belonged to could come and take it away in pieces for all she cared. This one, and all the rest that would come after.

    “Haul ‘em back to base if they don’t put up much of a fight. These guys just don’t like to pay their license fees, that’s all. That and they overhunt. Police department posted the warrants this morning- so it’s up to us.”
