    Unsurprisingly enough, the massive wolf-hybrid chose to do anything but listen. He’d bitten up the length of the man’s arm to keep him subdued and unable to fight back, and then he lunged for his neck. That, apparently, was enough to make Alex take matters into her own hands. She took up her shotgun, and in the split-second that was required for her to aim, Kara threw a bloody hand out to stop her—

    “Alex- wait, no!”

    The sound of Dog’s fighting came to an abrupt stop. It was the absolute thunder bolt of Alex’s shotgun that made Kara trade the gripping of her stomach for the cupping of her ears, and the painful and dizzying ringing noise that came after… Dog had screeched loudly and then limped a few times upon one of his hind legs, and then he reached back to lick frantically at his flank. Rock salt was an effective enough deterrent; His only interest now was to chew at his hind quarters until the stinging stopped. Alex finally made a move towards the poacher who was now sat up and bleeding against the trunk of a tree. Dog took notice of her, so she stopped and slowly crawled back. He began to growl again. Kara could feel her sister shaking like a leaf whenever she went to huddle over her once more.

    “Hey boy,” Kara mumbled, feeling as though she were only a few mere seconds away from passing out, “…it’s alright. Go.”

    He seemed almost hurt that Kara was turning him away, but after another long moment of his second-guessing and the eventual uncurling of his upper lip, he limped back off into the brush, likely to go and tend to his wounds.

    Kara felt that she might be sick, so she dropped her head again and began to breathe long and slow. Her vision seemed to pick up its heavy in-and-out strobing right where it had left off, but at least she was less afraid than she had been before her friend-on-all-fours had shown up. It wasn’t his fault… With Kara’s blood on the wind, it was no wonder that all he did was see red. Soon she noticed that Alex’s voice had started to sound very far away— almost like she had been shut in a very cold box and lowered down towards someplace safe. Kara figured dying wasn’t so bad, after all. If anything, it felt a lot like falling asleep…

    Then, just as she expected it might, everything went dark again.

    …And the pain finally stopped.

     

     

    If heaven was somebody holding your hand, then Kara supposed it was alright.

    She’d felt that there was always something so ineffably wonderful about holding hands. No matter whether it was with family, or a best friend, or somebody she loved. The way fingers were meant to intertwine, and thumbs were meant to caress… They could squeeze and say “I really mean it” or brush together and ask “Do you?”

    Holding hands while sitting, standing, walking, or lying in bed, Kara had loved it all. Yes, she was quite sure of it now…

    This had to be heaven.

    “Mm…” She groaned. For one thing, heaven was apparently very bright. “God?” Kara asked, “Would you mind turning down the lights, please?”

    “Kara?”

    Kara opened her eyes. Not heaven… She blinked. Alex.

    “Kara. Oh, my God—” Her sister choked past a breath. She’d lifted her head up quickly enough to display the subtle sheet ruffle imprints upon her reddened cheek, and Kara soon recognized the scratchy feeling of them in the palms of her hands. Alex began to cry. “I’m so glad you’re awake, you have no idea how scared—”

    “What’s going on?” Kara tried sitting up, but a deep pang of intense pain ran right through her middle, stopping her just short of moving a single inch.

    Alex’s hands went out to press her back into her hospital bed by her shoulders, “Do me a favor and don’t move.”

    “Got it.”

    With that, Alex seemed to settle back into her chair a bit more calmly. “Do you need anything?” She asked.

    Kara blinked bleary-eyed around the room and took stock of where she was at. Something terrible had happened, that much she was sure of, but the expression upon her sister’s face had given her pause… Why couldn’t she remember anything?

    “Just,” Kara swallowed with a hard grimace, “Tell me what happened.”

    Alex covered her mouth in an effort to stifle a sob, and a tear ran from her eye without warning. “Kara. You died.”

    Flabbergasted, Kara was careful to shake her head, “What?”

    “In the helicopter- you were in shock. We had to bring you back.”

    “Oh. Is that why it feels like someone’s danced a jig on my chest?” Kara reached up to rub at what was likely her very bruised sternum.

    Alex’s face turned hard, and then it went beet red. “It’s not funny.” She scowled.

    Kara deflected immediately. “I didn’t say it was.”

    “You were gone. Meaning you weren’t in there anymore.” Alex stabbed a finger hard in Kara’s general direction.

    “Wow, why does it feel like you’re mad at me?”

    “I’m not!”

    Falling into their back-and-forth routine of habitually misunderstanding one another was easy, at least.

    “Then what the hell, Alex?”

    “I’m…” Alex began to bluster and then threw her hands up with an exhausted breath, “I’m mad at the world, I guess. It keeps trying to take my sister away from me- The only family I have left.” She confessed, “There was a moment where I thought I was never gonna get you back, and it made me feel crazy.”

    Kara craned her head up towards the stark white tiled ceiling and gave an audible -gulp- past the shooting pain from deep within her torso. She chanced wiggling her toes a bit, and then her legs, and then sighed with her relief that those seemed to be on the short list of things that didn’t hurt. “Speaking of dying, my stomach is killing me.”

    “What do you remember?” Alex asked.

    “I dunno. This morning, maybe? Eating breakfast at your place?”

    “We were searching the woods around your end of the lake… The poachers?”

    “Mm—” Kara immediately regretted the surprised gasp she took once the landslide of a memory hit her. She winced through the pain and answered, “Guy with a limp. And another with a…” She paused, “A crossbow. Oh… yeah. Now I remember.”

    Kara looked down.

    “You were shot.”

    “Yeah. That sucked. Do not recommend.” Then, as if struck with lightning, she remembered what had taken place right after. “Dog—” Kara narrowed her eyes into daggers at her sister, “You shot my dog.”

    “Kara—”

    “Is he okay?”

    “He’s fine.”

    “Are you sure? Have you looked for him?” Kara began to despair. “I can’t believe you actually—”

    “I didn’t want to- Jesus Christ, Kara, he was gonna rip that guy’s throat out if I didn’t do something. I had to choose.”

    Kara withered once more into a passive state, begrudged nonetheless. “Still. He could be really hurt.”

    “I’m telling you, I’ve seen predator cats with worse wounds. He’ll be fine. I promise.”

    “Fine.” Kara weathered another deep-ish breath, “Is it still ‘today’, at least?”

    Alex nodded, the edge of her mouth finally quirking upward and into a smile. Kara was relieved to see it. “Yeah. ‘Tonight’, to be specific.” She replied, “You were in surgery for a really long time.”

    “Can I keep the bolt?”

    Alex shook her head. “I called dibs already.”

    “What? You can’t call dibs- I was unconscious!”

    “Sorry.” Alex shrugged.

    “…That’s not fair!”

    There was a prim -knock- at the door then, drawing Kara’s pitiable arguing to a speedy close. She’d eventually loot her prize for “not dying” whenever Alex’s back was turned. Such was to be expected with sisters. Alex went to stand up and answer the door, but not before splaying her hands over Kara’s injured body in a very protective manner…

    “I mean it- don’t move. They just had to stuff all your insides back into you.”

    Kara rolled her eyes with a smirk, “Whatever.”

    Across the room the door came open, and in walked a familiar, sunshiny face. Only it smacked of a little less sunshine, and a little more of jaw-clenching concern. Lena made an apologetic gesture after having nearly darted inside the room once the door was open. Alex merely nodded her in and then looked back at Kara one last time.

    “I’m, uh…” She scratched at the top of her head, drawing her hair back over where it was parted, “I think I’m gonna go call Maggie.”

    “Alex—” Kara called out, “Love you.”

    Alex let go of a breath, “Love you too.”

    The door shut behind her soon after that, and then it was just Lena, Kara, and all of the beeping and whirring noises that came with her many attachments. She was hooked up like a supercharger on a 67’ Shelby. It was hard to tell which wires led where, exactly. The IV in the crook of her left elbow tugged slightly whenever she went to reach for Lena.

    “I finally leave to go get something to eat and of course you wake up…” She murmured into Kara’s neck. She took great care not to lean, or pull, or do anything that might end up causing any kind of pain. That, Kara had in spades, but she didn’t care. Not with the familiar smell of Lena’s flowery perfume lingering over her. “How are you feeling?” Lena asked.

    “Like I’ve been tickled with a very sharp stick.”

    Lena smiled, and then she dropped her face into her hands. She pulled at the corners of her eyes to keep from crying, but that appeared to be a battle she was slowly losing. “You are the luckiest unlucky person I have ever met, Kara Danvers.”

    “…Sorry.” Kara replied, running her lips into a thin line.

    “I knew something was wrong when your sister called me from your phone. It was that feeling in my bones again- the one that keeps pulling me towards you.” Lena sniffled, “I was also told how hard you fought to stay alive.” She finished with another thankful kiss upon Kara’s cheek.

    Kara tried her best to keep her brow from furrowing with all of her pain. It radiated from her stomach all the way through to her back, making even too deep of a breath painful enough to spot the backs of her eyelids with stars. She didn’t wish to worry Lena any more than she already had, but that was easier said than done, apparently.

    “I’m just glad you’re here.” Kara admitted.

    “I’m glad you’re here.” Lena replied. It seemed she couldn’t help herself from kissing Kara, having pressed her lips many times from her cheek to her nose, to her chin, and then to her lips. It was there that she likely felt a measure of tightness, because in the next breath she was backing away to hover her eyes right in front. “How’s your pain?”

    Kara merely pursed her lips and gave a defiant shake of her head.

    No.

    She’s worried…

    Make it stop.

    “I got acquainted with your nurses while I was out.” Lena began to explain, “They brought me up to speed on your recovery, and what exactly you’re going to be dealing with in the near future.”

    “What do you mean acquainted? Don’t you already know everyone here?”

    Lena gave a curious raise of her eyebrow and then a quiet “ah” with her realization. “You’re not in Shreveport, Kara.”

    “Wait- Really?”

    “Mhm. They had to wait and make sure you wouldn’t go into shock again, but transporting you to Dallas was Alex’s decision. You were already headed in that direction, anyway. The urgent clinic in Marshall wasn’t equipped for the kind of surgery you needed.”

    “Woah.” Kara pulled in another breath through her nose then, but far too much this time. She broke her mostly undisturbed façade with a severely pained grimace.

    “It’s better if you just sleep as much as you can.” Lena said.

    Kara shook her head adamantly, “I don’t wanna sleep.”

    “Kara…”

    “I don’t wanna sleep.” She felt her chin begin to quiver, “What if I don’t wake up this time?”

    It was pointless to try and hide how scared she was; Kara was petrified. Dying certainly hadn’t filled in any blanks. It was the not knowing (or not being able to remember, for that matter) that racked her from head to toe with fear. It was that and the jarring, full-bodied pain she felt if she so much as blinked too hard.

    “Don’t worry, baby,” Lena smoothed her hands carefully down each of Kara’s arms to calm her, “I’ll be right here.”

    Kara gestured for her to lean down again with a nod of her chin and then kissed the taste of tears from Lena’s lips. A mournful saltiness. Kara was just glad to have a second chance at them. Her dying had been ill-timed, indeed.

    “Sleep. It’ll hurt less.” Lena whispered. Kara watched as she snuck a hand up into the mess of wires hanging from a nearby machine and readied her thumb over a button. “I love you…”

    Kara froze.

    And then she felt herself begin to melt.

    A warmth raced from her left arm to her right, and then it washed further down towards her torso, and then her legs. The smooth, calmness of it all terminated at the soles of her feet, and then, somehow, she was as light as a feather. Everything was suddenly so much better. Her eyes grew heavy now that she felt relaxed, but she worked hard to keep them both open at roughly the same time. She’d only just gotten Lena back… She didn’t want to have to leave again. Kara was unafraid, at least, of dying this time. She trusted Lena.

    Hell. She loved her.

    Chapter End Notes

    Not me nearly typing "Kara dies" as a chapter summary. I wouldn't do that to you! x'D We're heading into "stubborn yet adorable golden retriever refuses to convalesce" territory now. Stay tuned.

    Do Not Follow the Feu Follet

    Chapter Summary

    Kara recuperates.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Ten: Do Not Follow the Feu Follet

     

    “Have I ever told you how pretty your eyes are?”

    In truth, Kara could recall very little of what had transpired over the past few days. Beyond being run through with a crossbow bolt, that is. Each day consisted mostly of a morphine-fueled haze that swung between the deepest sleep she’d ever had and what were, admittedly, very short-lived blips of the most intense euphoria— the latter being decidedly more dream-like than reality.

    She hadn’t meant to be so difficult. Her leg itched, and then her butt cramped, and then she had to pee… The days (however many of them there were, she hadn’t exactly been counting) ended up weaving together into one big blanket of “loopy”. Apparently, keeping Kara sedated was the only way to keep her still.

    “Yes. Many times. Aren’t you feeling sleepy yet?” Lena had asked her.

    On the plus side, Kara remembered —at the very least— each and every single one of Lena’s smiles. Every time she saw one, Kara could feel that obnoxious thumping feeling from somewhere behind her ribs— her heart, or perhaps a stampede of elephants. At that point, it was anybody’s guess. The tip of her finger had found its way into the most perfect of dimples in lieu of an answer, along with the word,“…Boop.”

    “I assume that means you’re not in any pain, then.”

    “Nope.” Kara had replied with a -pop- sound to the letter “p”, and there the absolute fog of a memory came to an end.

    It was both a gift and a curse, her tolerance for pain. Kara could bear more than most. Hell, many of the men on her crew knew that she could stay standing where a horse could be knocked down, but it didn’t do her any favors when the pain became too much and her tolerance for painkillers was just as withstanding. In and out of her chaotic dreaming she could hear Lena’s voice troubling over the state of her kidneys, having the need to consume so many prescription pain relievers, but Kara would be able to reassure her a bit more once she was “out of the woods”, so to speak.

    At least she was at home and in her own bed now. She’d heard it mentioned somewhere in her more coherent moments; a couple of weeks had gone by in a blur.

    Kara gulped audibly past the roiling knot of pain in her stomach, and she tried adjusting her head where it lay upon her pillow. She didn’t like sleeping on her back. Something about it made her feel vulnerable and open to attack… Though, she wasn’t sure what from. There was a shift in the bed beside her before long, and that was when Lena sat up to pull at the chain of Kara’s bedside lamp. A warm amber color spread across her room like filtered sunlight, and Kara squeezed her eyes shut to it. She felt Lena thumbing at the beads of sweat upon her forehead, and then came a sigh.

    “You don’t seem to have a fever.” She yawned, “It hurts that bad?”

    Of course, Kara disagreed with gusto. Stubborn as always. “Mm-mm.”

    “Stay still- I’ll be right back.”

    Kara opened her eyes and reached to grab at Lena’s wrist, “Don’t leave—”

    “I’m only going to get your medicine.” Lena reassured her with a small gesture, “It’s right there on your dresser, see?”

    Kara blinked into the now less offensive light of her bedroom and then nodded. She felt silly for being so quick to beg. “I told you, didn’t I…?”

    Lena had gotten up and returned to Kara’s bed in the matter of seconds, haste in her movement and pill bottle in hand. “Told me what?”

    “When we first met- that I turn into a huge baby when I’m sick?”

    Lena smiled. “Well, you’re not sick.” She explained, “You were shot with a crossbow bolt through your small intestine.”

    “Oh, nurse lady, you make near-fatal injury sound so sexy.”

    “Here.” Lena waited for the parting of Kara’s lips so that she could drop a small white pill into her mouth, and then she cupped at Kara’s chin while she tipped a glass of water there. She rubbed sleepily at her eyes once Kara was able to swallow. “What I find sexy is how hard you fought to come back.”

    “Really?”

    Lena nodded. “You don’t want to know the mortality rate of hemorrhagic shock. Remember how I said you were lucky?”

    “Mm,” Kara hummed. There still wasn’t much she remembered after first waking up in her hospital bed, if she were being honest. “…not really.”

    “Had it been a bullet, there would have been nothing to keep most of your blood from spilling out. You still bled enough internally to force you into shock, yes, but I’m still so amazed sometimes- you were not willing to let go. Alex said it was the most incredible and terrible thing she’d ever seen.” Lena sighed as she carefully tucked her bare legs back underneath the covers and scooted closer. Kara had plenty of oversized t-shirts for sleeping in, of which Lena seemed to prefer the faded blue of her Bass Pro Shops tournament shirt. “I don’t know if I’ve said it before… but thank you.”

    “For what- for staying alive?” Kara asked.

    Lena nuzzled sweetly against the side of Kara’s face, pecking a small kiss there. “Mhm.”

    “Nothin’ to it—” Kara gave a vague wave of her hand and let it fall back to the mattress with a dull -thump-. The motion sent the softest of shockwaves through her middle, but it was still enough to make her wince around the word, “…Ow.”

    Lena hid a few giggles into the warm curve of Kara’s neck and then took another deep sigh as if she were right at home there. “Shh, be still.”

    “I just want a cheeseburger.”

    “Cheeseburger smoothie?”

    “I swear to God, if you make me laugh again—”

    “Okay, okay,” Lena brushed Kara’s cheek to cease the spreading of her smile, “I’ll be good.”

    “But not forever, right?” Kara asked with a sly smirk, turning her head to look adoringly at the woman who lie next to her, “I like it when you’re bad, too.”

    Lena gave a few pretty, unbelieving flutters of her thick lashes, “Nothing slows you down, does it?”

    “Nope, ‘fraid not.”

    “There’s…” Lena averted her eyes then, pulling them anywhere and everywhere all except for Kara. “There’s something I’ve been wanting to tell you. You’ve just been so out of it…”

    “Yeah, I feel awake for the first time in a while. Question- If I’m at home, how did I get up the stairs?”

    “Hank carried you.”

    “Wow. Remind me to split my catch with him the next time I get out on the water.”

    “Sure.” Lena agreed, “But I want you to know something.”

    Kara readjusted her head against her pillow again. There was only so much she could do to get comfortable for the time being, but she tried not to let it get to her. Growing frustrated with her bed-ridden state certainly wouldn’t help her case any. “Okay. What is it?” She asked.

    “I’m sure you’ve figured out by now that I’ve been staying with you. There’s someone else that comes by as well, but just to help me with your clothes and other things. You’re really quite heavy…”

    “Fishing builds muscle.” Kara tried a half-shrug. “What are you trying to say?”

    Lena pursed her lips for a moment. “I’m not saying it’s a bad thing, or that I was cornered into it, or that it will change anything at all, but I had to come clean to some of the nursing staff and our administrator about us. Not that I’ve admitted to anything wrong. Personal relationships with patients are more frowned upon where there’s long-term care involved, so there’s a lot of gray area when it comes to emergency medicine.”

    “So… us.”

    “Us.” Lena affirmed.

    “As in, together.”

    Lena raised an eyebrow at that. “Haven’t we been?”

    Kara was quick to nod, “Yes. Absolutely. It’s just… Well, I just like hearing it out loud, I guess.”

    “I did tell you that you could have all of me.”

    “You did.”

    “I’m yours, you goofball.”

    “Ditto.”

    Lena finally huffed a happy laugh. “You should have seen their faces. I think Andrea was a bit jealous.”

    “Of you, maybe.” Kara replied, “I’ve seen how moony eyed she gets around you.”

    “Tell me, handsome,” Lena asked with a bite of her lip as she shrugged a bit closer, “Do you get jealous?”

    Kara gave her a knowing squint. “I am human. You’ve seen me bleed.”

    “Okay. Well. I wouldn’t have known otherwise.”

    “What, about me being human or me being jealous?”

    “You know what I mean.” Lena dotted another kiss beneath one of Kara’s heavy-lidded eyes. “I’m sorry.”

    Kara craned her neck as far as she could to finally steal a kiss straight from Lena’s lips. She blinked warmly at her then, feeling the way her heart beat at rest— still a steady gallop in Lena’s presence. “Don’t be.” She admitted, “She can be over the moon all she wants. I’m just happy to have you- whenever I get you.”

    It occurred to Kara then how incredibly blasé she’d been about their apparent relationship. They’d been seeing each other for a year now, for crying out loud. Weekends, and even some days in between… Had Kara tread so carefully over the word “girlfriend” that she’d all but passed it up? She was madly in love with Lena for one thing, so how could she just leave the writ and stamp of it all upon her shoulders like that? Kara soon realized that she’d done it again; She’d set the pace for both of them a second time. What was the harm in just saying things? Had she been able to stand, she just might have kicked herself. It was clear to her by now that Lena wasn’t going anywhere. As for the words “I love you”… Kara at least had the presence of mind to save those for a day where she wasn’t glued to her bed and having to pee in a rather embarrassing way.

    “Lena,” She began, “It’s important to me that you understand how I feel about you…”

    “Okay.” Lena replied. Try as she might, there was no amount of lash-batting in the world that could hide the subtle hint of hope from her eyes.

    “I’d do just about anything short of killing for you.” Kara admitted.

    “Kara, ‘killing’ is a strong word…”

    “I mean it. I also know you’d never want that for me, and that’s why I—” Kara stopped herself short, “I’ve never felt this way about anyone. I didn’t think it existed, honestly. I always aspired to feel what I thought I was supposed to feel, when you’re with someone, but I never thought it would be so easy- the way it is with you.” Kara could see the features on Lena’s face becoming softer and softer, “The problem is, I don’t ever want to be without you now.”

    Lena shook her head, giving a preemptive sniffle through her nose to ward off the threat of tears, “You don’t have to be. I already told you. I don’t want to be anywhere else.”

    Kara felt satisfied with that. For now, at least. “Alright.” She replied.

    “Try to go back to sleep. Dream of cheeseburgers.”

    “I’m over cheeseburgers already. I want potstickers now.”

    Lena giggled as she tucked an arm beneath her pillow, “Once you’re cleared for solid foods, I will make you so many potstickers.”

    “You’re right…” Kara replied with a smile.

    “Beg pardon?”

    “I am lucky.”

     

     

    —One Month Later—

     

    With the sun draped far behind the needled tops of all her piney woods, Kara breathed deeply from where she sat in her fold-out chair and watched as the red and white bounce of her bobber fought to stay atop the calm and glassy surface of the water.

    She had been hard pressed thus far not making her way out to her dock for some prime summertime fishing, so as soon as she was able to walk on her own, she began to idle further and further from her back porch under Lena’s watchful eye. Her girlfriend had since returned to work, but was still lodging at Kara’s stilted cottage on the Caddo to make sure that she was taking care of herself. For reasons such as this, no less— Kara may have been able to walk and to dress herself and to even get herself up and down the tall stairs of her porch now, but fighting the large blues that sank her bobber deep into the muddy waters directly off her dock was a different story. She’d lost three bites so far, not to mention nearly losing her rod on the last attempt. The fish had it out for her tonight, it seemed.

    Kara could hear the disappointment drip with Lena’s every word. She’d all but snuck up on her…

    “How on earth do you get yourself all the way down here and still have the strength to reel in these huge fish?” She leaned to take a peek inside Kara’s still very empty cooler. “…Oh.”

    “Well, if I can do at least one of those things, then that’s good enough for me.”

    “Bad luck?” Lena asked.

    “No. Just not up to it yet, I guess. Last one almost took me out of my chair.”

    “I swear…” Lena flipped the lid of the cooler closed and then sat down upon it. She smoothed the long, lacy hem of her skirt as she crossed one leg over the other and then tucked her chin into her hand to stare directly at Kara. “You’re persistent, I’ll give you that.”

    “I’m being careful.” Kara gave a three-fingered salute, “Promise.”

    “As careful as you can be, I’m sure.”

    “Here, these might make you feel better about it.” Kara picked up her grabber tool that Lena had gifted her with recently. It had made reaching things both above her and below her much easier while she healed. She waved a little more to insist that Lena place both of her hands beneath it, “Close your eyes.”

    Lena furrowed her brow a bit, but Kara smiled once she acquiesced with a patient sigh through her nose. Kara pulled at the grabber’s release and dumped the whole of its contents right into Lena’s hands, eagerly awaiting her reaction.

    “Okay. You can open your eyes now.”

    React, she did. Lena gasped once she saw them.

    Smooth, round and shimmering… Some white, some not so white. A few even looked to be just the slightest shade of pink. Marvelous little pearls, they were, the dozen or so that Kara had plucked straight from their shells that day.

    “Are these actual pearls?” Lena guffawed quietly, “Kara, they’re beautiful. Where did you—”

    “They’re everywhere.” Kara gestured towards the water, “Used to be a mussel under every step you took on the bank. Before people came and fished most of them away, anyway. Now they’re the Caddo’s biggest secret. It’s why we don’t fight the lily pads growing over everything. Keeps people from wading around and finding them.”

    “I see what you mean. If people knew they were here…”

    Kara tucked her chin with a nod. “I don’t think we could take another pearl rush. Everyone just assumes they’re all gone, and that’s the way we like it.”

    “Well, in any case. I’m glad to have them.” Lena rolled the small and perfect pearls around again in the space of her hand and then gasped a second time, “Look at this one- it’s gold.”

    “Secret gold.” Kara reminded her with a smile. “So, do you want a bracelet or a necklace? Winn’s pretty good at making—"

    Kara’s head was suddenly rocked backward with a kiss. Lena had leapt from her seat and wrapped her arms around Kara’s shoulders, hanging carefully there like a proud veil of Spanish moss. Kara reveled in the split-second way that Lena’s hair tickled at her nose, and the way that it never failed to do so whenever she hugged her very tightly. Lena kissed across Kara’s cheek one more time and then went to sit again atop the small cooler.

    “Thank you. I’d love that.” She said with a gleeful bob of her chin, “You may tell him to surprise me.”

    “Oh, he’ll love that.” Kara chuckled and then adjusted slightly in her chair. She’d slunk a bit too far down and was uncomfortable now. Sitting up was still a bit of a battle; Anything that used any portion of her core muscles or lower abdominals was much of the same. Much to her surprise, Kara had been unaware of just how many muscles were involved in something as simple as “sitting”. She ran her eyes over Lena’s outfit again and felt her mouth begin to water. It was a welcome feeling. A missed feeling. Lena’s skirt was topped with a matching blouse; thin, and flowy, and sleeveless. It had a nice, ruffled collar that wrapped around her slender, swan-like neck, and more than anything in the world, Kara wanted to run her lips there in a straight line all the way from Lena’s jaw to her collarbone.

    Chapter End Notes

    As most of you already know, my Supergirl fics have very little in common with anything "Arrowverse". There's a few admirable (or goofy) character traits I pluck from the show, but what you read here is mostly borne from my love of comics. See: Maggie Sawyer, and know the truth. If you like to read it from an Arrowverse POV anyway, that's fine. Do your thing. Most of it still absolutely fits, suspending certain disbeliefs (which isn't hard). Also, obviously, I’ve made Kara and Alex related by blood- for no other reason than I simply wanted to.

    So thanks for reading it, even if you have a different opinion.

Under the house was a boggy expanse of soft, slippery mud and support timbers. Each support was anchored by a concrete base, much like pylons beneath a lofty river bridge.     Kara puffed her chest out a bit with that, denoting a sense of pride that swathed her from the shoulders down. The absolute high she got whenever Lena called her anything other than her name… It was pride and gratification, relief and pleasure all rolled into syllables. Most of the time, Lena would offer her something to drink before the words “my darling”. Or other times, she’d follow up other words of affirmation with something like “baby” or “sweetheart”. Whenever she was pleased, surprised, enamored, or sometimes even a mixture of all three… it came out with an infectious sort of tone. The woman had a way with words, and that “way” was to simply speak them in Kara’s direction. As far as Kara was concerned, everything that came out of Lena’s mouth was gospel.

    “After you…” Kara gestured politely as she held the front door of the clinic open, and Lena’s eyes went heart shaped once again as she stepped through.

    Oh, what on earth was she to do with her now? The possibilities were endless. Each day was a blessing.

    “Feel like going for a walk?” Lena asked.

    Kara beamed at the idea. “You read my mind.”

    There had never been a more beautiful and sunny spring day. A walk was a fine idea, indeed. Kara would hold Lena’s hand down this very sidewalk without a care in the world. She knew each crack in each brick, and which turns to take at which moments (granted there weren’t many to begin with) and could recall, at best, which storefront upon which street was best to stop in for a spot of frivolous shopping. One narrow lane over was a novelty shop which had attracted Lena’s gaze the year before like a moth to flame. She’d been obsessed with the giant amethyst that sat there in the window ever since. Kara could still remember what the little incense shop smelled like on the inside. That, and the street corner just past where they were parked, how (for many years) Kara watched an elderly, bent-shaped gentleman hobble down from the curb every single morning on her way into town and then, one day, didn’t. There was the street sign she’d stolen just for Alex to take it back the next day… And the post office that was half demolished from a string of bad tornados one rainy season…

    They strolled aimlessly down the crooked sidewalk for a while in complete silence, taking it all in. It was a rundown little speck of a town, but it was Kara’s rundown little speck of a town. Perhaps her luck had improved, after all. She got to share it with somebody else now. Somebody she loved beyond all rhyme or reason.

    The sun was shining, and the birds were singing, and a man was slamming the tailgate of his rusted pickup truck a bit too hard—

    Kara stopped on a dime.

    When Lena’s hand came up with her having to drag Kara behind, she stopped as well. “What’s wrong?” She asked, her eyes following Kara’s line of sight.

    Everything was perfect. Just as it should have been. The world was definitely the right way up, and people were kind to one another because they were happy here… To Kara, it was all worth protecting at any cost.

    She just couldn’t believe her eyes. Was this some sort of cruel trick?

    “Kara…?”

    The man across the street headed for the curb again, and when he walked, he did so with a noticeable limp.

    Something inside of Kara jolted to life all of a sudden— like a dormant ember meeting the sweep of fresh, oxygenated air again. It glowed red and angry and filled her all the way out to her extremities. She knew that limp.

    “What—” Lena gasped once Kara darted into the street. She’d leapt right in front of a passing car, but she didn’t care. The car gave several perturbed honks before Lena found the sound of her voice again, “Kara!”

    There was little anyone could do to stop her. Kara felt that she would barrel past anyone that tried; Her feet were not her own. And neither were her fists.

    Lena continued calling out for her as if she were right on her heels, but the noise of the world soon tuned out to the rhythmic, heavy thud of her heart. The nerve this man had showing his face here again… How was he not still in jail? Sure, he hadn’t been the one to put a crossbow bolt through her gut, but he was still responsible for so much other suffering. He was a black stain upon the crisp image of her little hometown; its perfectness marred and made ugly again. It damn near turned Kara inside out.

    “You—” She seethed through her teeth as she grabbed him from behind, “Getting your traps ready again so soon?”

    The poacher’s eyes were as big as saucers once she spun him around to face her. Her hands went into the collar of his shirt, and his feet came up off the ground. Kara landed the man flat against the side of his truck… hard enough to rattle its oversized tow mirror from all of the force.

    Lena shouted again, louder this time, “Oh, my God- What are you doing?!”

    “I wasn’t staying—!” The poacher groaned behind the tight loop of his shirt collar. He sputtered and flailed with his hands for Kara to let him go, but it was futile, like beating the side of a mountain with a feather duster in order to get it to move.

    “If you even think about setting up shop near my end of the lake again…” Kara growled.

    “Kara! Stop it!”

    “I wasn’t!” He pleaded.

    “Maybe you think you can get a good price for my dog’s pelt—”

    “Put him down!”

    Kara felt both of Lena’s hands as they wrapped around the swell of her bicep… But she was, indeed, quite like the mountain. She would not be moved.

    The man continued clawing desperately at Kara’s arms for escape. His feet even kicked where they dangled helplessly above the ground. Kara felt a tingling sensation in her face like all of her blood had rushed there at once, and there was a loud ringing behind her ears, but much to her surprise, she wasn’t afraid of any of it. Not like before when she’d thought Lena had been hurt. That river of rage had gained speed again quickly; the delta of her restraint eroding just as fast.

    “I was leaving!” He cried out, “Came- came to grab some things!” Kara shook him as if she didn’t believe him and he screamed again, “I don’t want your mangy wolf dog- dammit- let me go!”

    Kara dropped him to his feet then, and he groaned painfully from having landed on what was more than likely a bum knee. She didn’t care. For all she knew, “Other Kara” had squeezed her way inside once again. She seemed intent on staying, too. It was folly to try and argue— but why did throttling this man have to feel so goddamned good? She should have felt ashamed, Kara knew as much…

    But she didn’t.

    Not yet anyway.

    “Kara!” Lena yelled as she threw herself in between them. “What the hell is the matter with you?!”

    “He was there—” Kara huffed with a -slap- against her thigh, “When I was shot, that’s the other guy!”

    The poacher was rubbing at the red ring around his throat and muttering to himself, “You crazy bi—”

    And Kara barreled for him again.

    Lena stood her ground. “Don’t!”

    The thought of him stepping foot inside of her woods again was simply too much to bear. For whatever reason, Kara felt a strong aversion to anything that came out of his mouth. How much of it was the truth? What was the word of a man who killed and skinned innocent creatures for a living? Kara felt another surge of anger, and before she could even think, she reached past Lena’s shoulder to hook her hand inside the front pocket of the man’s shirt. She ripped it from his breast with one clean swipe and balled it into her shaking fist. Lena was pushing against her with all of her might, but Kara felt that she was done anyway.

    “Yeah- you’d better leave,” She hissed, “Because if I ever see you here again, I’m gonna go and get my dog…” The shirt rag in her fist came to shake a little closer towards his face, “And I’m gonna tell him exactly where to find you.”

    “Don’t worry,” The limping man coughed, “I’m gettin’ as far away from this damn place as I can.”

    Lena gave Kara one more shove away from him, and even though it took all of her strength, it only forced Kara back a step or two. Kara wanted the last word.

    “Good luck with that. After everything you did in those woods, there isn’t a place in this world you could run to where it won’t eventually catch up with you. I hope it follows you for the rest of your miserable life!”

    “Stop, Kara!” Lena shouted. Her brow was set hard over her eyes as if she were staring daggers right at her, and finally, Kara relented.

    The whistling rage that had filled her up soon began to exit. Kara could feel it in her hands from how tightly she’d furled them into fists. Had she been just one fiery breath away from punching him in the face? Or the gut? At least she knew the pain from one of those things… Something inside of her had ignited and burned hot like the sun, but it sat inside of her bones like something twisted and malformed, anathema to her nature like rot is to timber. Kara’s heaving chest had slowed by now, and she winced at the sound of the poacher’s truck tires spinning upon loose pavement.

    Anger billowed out, and remorse slowly made its way in.

    “I cannot believe you.”

    Kara leapt at an apology, “I’m sorry, I- I don’t know what came over me—”

    “You just assaulted a man!”

    “Lena, please—”

    “Take me home, Kara,” Lena recoiled as soon as Kara went to take her by the hand, “…and don’t touch me.”

    Tears came to foist themselves into the corners of Kara’s eyes. “Don’t say that.”

    “I don’t know who you are right now…” Lena shook her head in disbelief, “What did you just do?”

    “It’s still me, please- It was a mistake.”

    “I said take me home. Right now.”

    “…to Shreveport?” Kara asked with the godawful sinking of her heart.

    “No.” Lena replied after a moment of skin-crawling silence. “But I need a moment to think.” And just like that, the warm vibration of her voice trailed into quietness once again.

    Kara hung her head with a nod. “Alright. Just… please don’t leave.”

    Instead of answering, Lena took herself back across the street and started walking without Kara. There was a look of haste about her that made Kara’s chest feel two breaths from caving in. She was hurrying to get away from her.

    Kara had survived many things. She’d been shot through the gut and had broken more bones in her body than she could count, but still, none of that compared to the feeling of watching Lena leave her behind. It felt like dying all over again. Because that’s what it was…

    It was all over.

    The sunshine, and birds singing, and mornings filled with happy smiles. Lena’s warmly panted breath against the side of her face, the sounds of their lovemaking… Coming home to her… Waking up to her…

    Kara had gone and ruined it all.

     

     

    “Crunchy or creamy?”

    Kara shook her head back and forth from where she sat at Alex’s kitchen table. The pit of guilt in her stomach had all but demolished her appetite for the past two days. Her sister gave her a knowing squint, and then she sat her hands upon her hips like she always did.

    “What happened?”

    Not “what’s wrong” or “what’s the matter” but “what happened?” specifically. Kara supposed that Alex knew her better than she knew herself sometimes… Especially when it came to matters of the heart.

    “You’re never not hungry. Tell me what happened.”

    “I fucked up.” Kara said with a drop of her head against the table. It went there with a clumsy -thunk- as she sighed.

    At this angle, Kara could see the legs of the chair across from her as they scooted back against the floor. Her sister sat quietly and waited for an explanation. She was patient like that in her adulthood— younger Alex used to be relentless in the pursuit of answers.

    “I thought you said they both went to jail.” Kara said. Why she’d landed on such an accusatory tone, she had no idea.

    Alex reared her head back with confusion, “What are you talking about?”

    “The poachers. You know—” Kara lifted her head and ran the point of her finger into her side with a “ffft” noise from her mouth.

    Suddenly, Alex understood. “Oh…” She tossed her reddish-brown hair back and forth as she shook her head, “Well, they did.”

    “They did?”

    “What, did you think they’d be in there forever? The maximum sentence for destruction of wildlife is 180 days, but I doubt they stayed the full stretch… It’s not like they were violent offenders or anything.”

    “Yeah, sure. Tell that to my gut.”

    “That man who shot you went through hell, Kara. And the other one…” Alex heaved a deep breath with the full use of her chest, “I don’t actually know much about him. We handed them off and that was it. They were sorry about what happened, though, if you can believe it.”

    “I don’t know if I can…”

    “Kara—”

    “I attacked him.”

    “What?” Alex huffed, “You mean the—”

    “Not the one who shot me. The other one with a funny gait.”

    “You…” Alex flustered, “I’m sorry. You attacked someone? Are- are you in trouble right now?”

    Kara’s eyes dropped once again to the table. “Not with the police, no.”

    “Oh.”

    The unwelcome sting of tears had come back for maybe the tenth time that day. Kara closed her eyes to it, unwilling to let them take root. “I really fucked it up. Lena’s barely talking to me.” She muttered again beneath her breath.

    “So, I take it you did it in front of her. Did you hurt him?”

    “Just tossed him up against his truck as he was leaving town. I don’t know- Something just made me so angry, like I didn’t want him anywhere near anything I loved.”

    “I see.”

    “It was like I wasn’t ‘me’ anymore…”

    “That must have been scary.”

    “That’s the thing, I don’t know if there was any part of me left to be scared. When I tell you I wanted to hurt this man—”

    “But you didn’t?”

    Kara exhaled a long breath and then shook her head, “No.”

    “Then that’s what should matter.” Alex scooted her chair closer around the battered edge of her kitchen table. “You didn’t end up doing something that you could never take back. Don’t you see?”

    “What do you mean?”

    “You can still take it back.”

    “I think maybe you missed the part where I said he was on his way out of town.”

    “No, no—” Alex waved dismissively in Kara’s direction, “Not with him. With Lena… That’s what you’re upset about, isn’t it?”

    “Well… yeah. I mean, I still feel pretty awful about him though—”

    “Don’t. Guy had it coming.”

    “Wow.” Kara huffed a half-hearted laugh.

    “Kara, I know you.” Alex went to give a small squeeze at her shoulder, “You’d never hurt anyone. Now, do you want some advice, or did you just plan to mope around my kitchen all morning?”

    “Geez, okay- I’ll take the advice, then.”

    “Make a grand gesture.”

    Kara furrowed her brow. “Huh?”

    “Do something big, and bring a promise with it.” Alex replied, drawing her hands apart to convey her meaning. “Something only Lena will know. There’s sacredness in a vow, too, so make it count.”

    “Okay.”

    “But think about it first. Make sure whatever it is fits your purpose exactly, down to the letter.”

    “It’s like I understand and don’t understand at the same time…” Kara replied with a squint, crinkling the space between her eyebrows.

    Alex laughed and then sat back to rest in her chair. “You’ll know it when you see it. Trust me.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Puppy dog Kara found her teeth much too sharp. She'll make it right.

    Thanks for reading. <3

    Amber Eyes and Fireflies

    Chapter Summary

    Kara makes amends.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Thirteen: Amber Eyes and Fireflies

     

    Kara’s feet were spaced evenly with her shoulders, with her arms held out and at the ready.

    The floor beneath her seemed to move this way and that as if it were two steps from caving in. The smell of rotted planks and the foul, metallic tang of rusted iron filled the air as she looked around… How had she gotten here? The steamboat’s large wheel stared back at her with a single, solemn request; To do what nature could not.

    To tear down its remnants.

    To wipe clean from the earth its tortured and weary footprint.  

    Tiles from the vessel’s ceiling began to fall down all around her. She tried catching them one at a time, but they all but turned to dust inside of her hands. They cried out for oblivion, beseeching Kara to rid them of their watery purgatory once and for all. Stuck in time and lost to history— The Washington begged with every groan and creaking noise made available to its remaining framework of timbers.

    “Have mercy on us.” It said.

    Kara spun around, catching herself upon a decaying bulkhead near the boat’s helm. “Who’s there—?"

    When her foot went through the floor, the voice like a howling wind came back. “Mercy—” It cried out again, “Mercy—!”

    The splintering of wood pulled Kara’s eyes forward again. She was standing in the middle of a long hall below deck now, a grayish whipping cloud taunting her as it streaked past a foggy porthole. She held in her hands a large piece of a door— its ornate glass knob seeming so out of place amongst so much decay. Kara had ripped the cabin door right out from its frame as if it were nothing.

    “What- How…?”

    The same kind of ruination followed a blind and blustering path down the length of the hallway— evidence of her annihilatory journey. There was destruction everywhere, like the sweeping of a hand through a grand spider’s web. Kara’s heart sank to the murky depths as she dropped the door half from her hands. It spat a cloud of dust back up at her, thrusting painful flecks of the past deep into her eyes. She blinked and blinked, and rubbed there through her tears, but the once-illustrious paddle steamer continued to fall out from beneath her feet. Kara fought to hold on as she sank further in, clawing at the floor the more it split apart. This couldn’t be happening, she thought. The Washington couldn’t just disappear… Could it?

    Further down she went, through plank and sheared nail and tile, every crumbling handhold dissolving right through the spaces between her fingers…

    “Wait—” She yelled, “No… Stop!”

    Then, the floor gave way.

    …And water filled her lungs.

    When Kara opened her eyes again, she was in bed.

    The bullfrog song from beyond her bedroom window made quiet the thundering of her heart. She palmed at the damp of her brow where it was slicked with sweat from her cataclysmic sort of nightmare. Surely it had to mean something… But perhaps it was exactly the thing she feared above all.

    The more she tried to make safe the things she loved, the more she risked hurting them.

    But to do nothing? Kara shook her head. Impossible… She despaired inwardly, how do you just do nothing?

    To say that Kara was frustrated would be a severe understatement, but she was glad at least to be in bed. She was only so fond of her couch for so long— sleeping there had put her terms of self-inflicted punishment on full display, as well as the crick in her neck. Lena had eventually convinced Kara to sleep again in her own bed; It seemed the woman had little patience for the way that Kara flagellated herself day in and day out for her violent outburst. Gone was the fear of Lena leaving, at least, but how could Kara look her in the eye now and say anything without it falling flat between them? She had upset their perfect balance of trust, rolled it heavy onto its hull and run it aground upon rocky shore. Was that why she’d dreamt about the derelict Washington? Was Lena’s faith in her a very old boat?

    Kara sighed and gave a pinch to the bridge of her nose. All of her helpless dithering must have stirred Lena awake beside her, because before Kara even realized it, her arms were spread apart and then wrapped around Lena’s waist. A hand searched blindly in dark until it fell upon Kara’s cheek. It was soft and warm, just like all of Lena’s affections. She’d forgiven Kara, but still the guilt remained. There wasn’t a lack of affection or any added silence between them… Lena was still there, and that should have been enough. Even though the love they shared was strong and resolute beyond measure, Kara just couldn’t shake the feeling of things being irrevocably changed between them.

    Change —especially the unexpected kind— was a thing that usually came as a great upset to her, but perhaps the White Stag had shared his omen in good faith.

    It just seemed that everyone knew what to do with it except for Kara.

     

     

    “Are you sure you wanna do this now?” Winn asked as he stepped down from the weathered steps of an aboveground crypt.

    Kara turned back around from having stared into space for too long. They were there to survey the old footbridge that lead towards Uncertain’s one and only cemetery. It had fallen in on itself over the skinny creek that circled the cemetery grounds, closing off the only way in or out for those visiting their loved ones. The climb across to the shallow island full of columbariums and mausoleums was difficult, making the need for a footbridge ever apparent to the both of them. Kara nodded once she circled back around to respond to Winn’s growing uneasiness.

    “The sooner we give the city an estimate, the sooner we can get everyone paid. The job’s ours- I just don’t see the point in waiting.”

    “Or… You just don’t wanna go home.”

    Winn Schott’s audaciousness did tend to go hand in hand with his wit, Kara had to give him that.  

    “What?” Kara shook her head, “N-no—”

    “C’mon, Kara.” He insisted, “You’ve spent more time at my house these past couple of weeks than you have all last year. What’s up with you?”

    Kara grumbled lowly to herself. She didn’t know why she thought she could fool her genius of a best friend… Winn might have been small and mousy in habitus, but his personality and the friendship that came along with it stood at least ten feet tall.  

    “All I want is to work, Winn.” She replied, “It keeps my mind busy.”

    “What, so you can eat me out of house and home four days a week but talking to me is suddenly off the table?” Winn chided as he folded his arms. Somehow, the cicadas loud and monotonous evening call matched the very deadpan expression upon his face. He was stern. Kara knew he wouldn’t back down until she came clean.

    “It’s…” Kara allowed herself a bit of honesty, “I don’t know what to do about my relationship with Lena.”

    “I thought you said things were fine?”

    “They are. I think. I mean, sort of… Technically.”

    “Kara…”

    “I don’t know how to explain it. Everything just feels so paper thin.” Kara threw her hands into the air as she turned back towards the collapsed bridge. It stared back at her as proof positive of the dangers of being tread upon without caution. For years the little footbridge had always been there, held up by its small, warped arch over the trickling water below. Its absolute value was only made evident now that it had fallen through, and that made Kara sad. What was there to say about the bridge between Lena and herself? “I’m too clumsy for this—” She shook her fists, “…and not just in the literal sense. I break everything.”

    “No, you don’t.” Winn replied as he walked closer. The hard features of his stern expression had fallen, leaving an open window of sympathy in their place. “You’re good at fixing things. That much I know for a fact.”

    “Thanks, Winn.” Kara sat down upon a slightly off-kilter stone bench then with a -fwump-. Her face soon went into her hands with another long sigh. She’d tried taking her sister’s advice, but everything she came up with seemed to fall just shy of a miracle. That’s what it felt like she needed, anyway. Lena wouldn’t admit it, but her eyes gave her away on most days. There was something missing in her gaze. Something broken. A light had gone out.

    “I’ve got faith in you. Don’t worry.” Winn admitted as he sat and then leaned into Kara’s shoulder.

    Kara lifted her head with that and gave him a weak smile. Then she pulled her eyes around to the rickety bridge once again. Work seemed to expel her from her inner lament like a charm, so she straightened her back where she sat in an effort to get right back to it. Changing the subject as such would be abrupt, but Winn knew to eventually let her have her peace when and if she could find it.

    “So, I was thinking yellow pine—”

    Winn caught on immediately like the sharp -snap- of a whip. “I would expect nothing less.”

    “And some ethically sourced river rocks for the abutments. We can send a truck there and back in a day with enough to build all four ends.”

    “Sounds good.”

    “I really like the wrought iron fixtures that top the fence around the place, too. I think there’s a storm cellar nearby that has a bunch of old oil lanterns from God knows when. They look similar… Gives the same kind of feel, at least. Let’s see if we can find those, and we’ll mortar them into the abutments.”

    By now, dusk had reached far and wide over the small shoal of mausoleum scattered land, casting grave shadows like long teeth into the fading light. Graves had not been dug here for centuries, and that was because of the awful business of unearthing that tended to come with wetland regions. Storms had a way of resurfacing coffins and displacing them in people’s front yards, or in the middle of busy streets. The secure niches inside of a columbarium were ideal for the interment of urns, as well as the gated stone archways of mausoleums to house the deceased.

    Off in the distance there danced a swarm of fireflies, flitting clumsily here and there with faint and intermittent flashes of yellowish light. Kara followed their aimless bobbing in the air towards the wooded area that lay beyond the small burial island, and all of a sudden, the solution to her problem was made abundantly clear.

    “I, um—” She stood up and gave a slight tug at the collar of her thin sweater vest. “I think I know how to fix it.”

    Winn puzzled his brow for a moment as he looked up at her. “…The bridge?”

    “No—” Kara fumbled, “Well, yes, but also—” Then she waved off her rambling as she pointed her boots towards the tumbledown remains of the footbridge, “I’ll tell you about it later. Can you put the numbers together for the materials and then send it to me?”

    “Sure…”

    “Alright. I’ve gotta run- you okay here?”

     “Hell no,” Winn replied with a vapor, “If you think I’m gonna hang around some creepy graveyard—”

    “Not a graveyard.”

    “…Huh?”

    Kara gestured towards all of the stone monuments. “They’re tombs. Not graves.” Something slithered along the ground near the end of the short bench right about then, and Kara tossed her head towards the exit. “Come on.”

    “Whatever! I’m right behind you, smart aleck.”

    Kara laughed. “Okay. Hey, want me to throw you across the creek like the elf guy did to the dwarf in that one movie?”

    “Wow…” Winn gave an unsubtle raise of his brow at the reference, “Remind me to thank your girlfriend for turning you into such a geek. I feel like we’re bonded on a totally different—”

    “Winn?”

    “…Yeah?”

    “There’s a snake behind you.”

    Winn gave a shrill “yipe” and took off into the dark, the heels of his sneakers kicking up clods of soft dirt in his wake.

     

     

    “Seriously, Kara. Where are you taking me?”

    Kara looked back through the brush where her hand was firmly grasped around Lena’s. She’d taken some convincing at first —it being so late in the evening and all— but now they were traipsing through the dense, thicketed part of the woods near Kara’s house. The trail went skinnier and skinnier until there was no trail left at all, and that was when Lena began to voice her concerns.

    “I don’t know, this doesn’t feel right—”

    “Don’t worry.” Kara replied, “There’s nothing out this way to be scared of.”

    “Probably because nothing else can get in…” Lena mentioned beneath her breath as she ducked below another twiggy branch of a sapling. She had to scoot past more of them sideways just to fit between them all. Kara made sure to draw back as much of the brush as possible for them to duck under, but there was only so much to skirt along without getting their clothes caught on broken branch ends and hung up in vines.

    “Except for us.” Kara smiled. More small, dependable flashes of yellow light guided her in a somewhat winding path until the trees began to appear larger in size. Thankfully, the snagging thicket had come to an end. Kara stopped in front of a tall bush and turned to Lena with her fingers twisting the small knob of her kerosene lamp. “Alright. You ready?”

    “What are you putting the lamp out for?” Lena asked, “Kara—”

    The anticipation of it all had Kara sweeping the tall bush away with her long arm before Lena could protest any further, and there beyond it stood an ocean of bioluminescent light. Lena blinked at all of it with her mouth held slightly agape in awe.

    More fireflies. Except they were everywhere.

    Kara reached to pull Lena inside the small clearing, who went along with a barely audible “oh, my god” uttered between them. The absolute jungle they’d just waded through in order to get here made it seem like they had stepped into a completely different world. There must have been tens of thousands of the small luminary bugs, all floating in one bright and glowing synchronous cloud. There was enough of them to make the dead of night seem like daylight. Kara cherished the way Lena’s face lit up with wonder. Seeing it only confirmed the fact that she’d made the right choice, after all. Lena stuck her arms out wide in the middle of the glowing expanse, laughing at the way each of the tiny bugs alighted upon the tops of her shoulders like a yellow-green coat made of pure light. The fireflies strobed from light to dark in unison as they undulated proudly around the clearing. Kara had found these firefly grounds one night as a child and had long since been visiting whenever the outside world ceased to make sense.

    In a place like this, the rules were very simple: Glow, and know thyself.

    “What do you think?”

    Lena beamed another smile from ear to ear. “I think I like very much being surprised by you, Kara Danvers.”

    Kara tried to hide the frown that sought to turn her mouth downwards. “But not every surprise is a good one.” Her eyes cast themselves to the square toes of her leather boots then.

Kara would make a trade here. A trade, and then a vow. She would give the woods her guilt, and in return, she would promise to never lose her mind to the lunacy of anger ever again.     “I know you wanna give it to me. Look at me.” Kara demanded, “I want you to watch me while I take it.”

    “Oh, it’s yours, Kara, it’s all yours—” Lena gasped, opening her eyes against every urge to squeeze them shut.

    The absolute surrender apparent in Lena’s haunting blue-green gaze had Kara enraptured from beginning to end; it was the shaking of her entire body, the intermittent, broken moans, and the way her head wanted so badly to fall back against the countertop…

    A warm, slippery feeling spilled into Kara’s palm then, and she decided that it was the best feeling she’d ever have in her entire life. Nothing else would ever so much as come close to it ever again. Truly, it was the most glorious thing to have happened to her— the flowing of her true love’s heart and soul into the very palm of her hand.  

    She wanted it again.

    And again… and again…

     

     

    Lena had made friends with the skulk of foxes that had denned in one of the tall, nearly hollowed out cypress trees.

    Its wide, gnarled base made for a grand shelter, easily accessed by the soft earth that rose up to meet it. Kara had saved one of the family’s kits from drowning after a storm had washed away the little land bridge that was left to climb down inside, and from then on it had been easy just to walk by and see them grow with each passing day. Lena liked to toss them small pinecones and to watch them play back and forth on afternoons she had free from the clinic, and Kara was very often not too far away, smiling at the sight of so much joy.

    “Getting hungry, yet?” She called out towards the fox den.

    Lena turned to look over her shoulder where she knelt upon the ground and then stood up to go and meet Kara on the other side of the wooded trail. She sidled up next to her with a little hop in her step, still giddy from the night before.

    “Not really. I’m okay to walk a little more. What about you?” Lena asked.

    Kara smiled. “Fine, fine. I’ll admit, though, I’m a little surprised you’re up for so much walking after last night.”

    Lena gave a small pinch to the point of Kara’s chin and then tossed her long ponytail over a shoulder with a -flick- of her wrist. “It’s not without some degree of difficulty, I can assure you. Sort of feels like one of those haunted houses where the floors move up and down beneath your feet—”

    “I don’t know what kind of haunted houses you’ve been to, but…” Kara trailed off once she received a playful -smack- against her arm.

    “You know what I mean.”

    Lena’s attention seemed to be pulled elsewhere suddenly. Her eyes were fixed upon a cluster of smallish, white flowers with spear shaped petals, and she kneeled down again to pluck one or two in admiration. Their blood red pistils greeted her with a warm "hello", and she smiled.

    “What are these?” Lena asked. “I’ve never seen them before… Why do they look so much like daisies?”

    “Because they’re whoopsie daisies.” Kara replied. Lena tilted her head with an air of curiosity once she stood back up. She pinched a stem between her thumb and forefinger and rolled the little flowers around some more, inspecting them a bit more closely. Kara thought to explain further before the cute pinch of Lena’s brow set in. “That’s what my mom used to call them, anyway. She’d find them and bring them to me whenever I would scrape my knee, or, you know… break something.”

    “Really?”

    “Yeah.”

    “I wish I could have met her.”

    Kara sighed. “She would have loved you.”

    “You think so?”

    “Oh yeah, without a doubt. My dad would have, too.” Kara nodded, “Actually, you and he would have gotten along great. He was the first smartest person I’ve ever known.”

    “I love it when you talk about your parents. I wish you would do it more often…” Lena brushed her hand against the side of Kara’s face, “You make them sound so wonderful.”

    “They were. And I’ll try to, just for you.” Kara agreed.

    Chapter End Notes

    I believe it's time to reveal that there are at least two chapters left of this tale. I have updated this in the fic settings as well, to end with the number sixteen. Thank you all for tuning in, and for all of the wonderfully intelligent, emotionally mature and meaningful comments thus far. It's been my absolute pleasure.
