    A Tall Pine's Lament

    Chapter Summary

    Kara is dealt a blow.

    Chapter Notes

    As a warning, this chapter will deal with the pain of loss, and is very sad. You will journey through denial, anger, bargaining, depression, and then acceptance. Feel free to skip it and wait for the next, if you so wish. If you choose to read on, please be sure to catch the chapter end notes as well.

    CW: character death, stages of grief

    Chapter Fifteen: A Tall Pine’s Lament

     

    “Here you go, my darling.” Lena said as she offered Kara a hot cup of coffee where she sat outside.

    Early mornings in the late spring were a fine time to drink coffee and listen to all of the birds sing. The sporadic mockingbird would fade to the curious finch, which then would whittle down to the calm monotone of the grasshopper; Each one playing into the next until the sun was high overhead.

    Kara sipped carefully from her cup and placed it upon the little glass table next to where she lounged. The view of the lake was beautiful from this height, as it was nearly a bird’s eye view of the goings-on of nature down below. Lena sat near the back porch’s tall wooden railing and crossed one knee over the other, blowing the steam from her tea without a care in the world.

    Mornings like these were easy. No meetings, no appointments, nothing to do but to sit and be at peace. Kara could feel the faint stirrings of a nap in her near future. The long, cushioned wicker lounge she laid upon was comfortable, and her eyes began to grow heavy.

    “Before you nod off, do you want to make plans to go into town today?” Lena asked.

    Kara stretched, just shy of avoiding a yawn, “Sure. We can catch a movie or something. Oh- and then we can stop at the craft store afterward—”

    The sound of a repeated -scrape- and -click- made its way up the porch steps then, lumbering patiently from one heavy footfall to another— Except they weren’t feet at all. Kara would recognize that padded step anywhere. She gave a surprised tilt of her head and went very quiet, as did Lena.

    The sound of Dog’s unhurried ascension made Kara second-guess whether or not he knew that the porch was already occupied. He almost never came up to lay on his mat at the same time that Kara and Lena were there. It was always either one or the other, he often never obliged both of them at the same time. Dog was a “personal space” kind of guy, through and through.

    Except for today, it seemed.

    Lena muttered a quiet “woah” once he landed at the top of the stairs and walked right past her without so much as a second glance. He was headed straight for Kara with his head hung low as if he were tired. There was nothing about his demeanor that displayed a sense of curiosity, or even hesitance— in fact, he came right up to Kara as if he’d been doing so for years, but it seemed to take him forever and a day just to make it there. The way he was moving was so slow, as if he could barely amble from one long drag of his leg to another.

    Then, what he did next barreled straight past “surprising” and arrived promptly at “impossible”.

    Dog made one low grunt as he flopped a huge paw up onto the lounge with Kara, and then another, and then with a great deal of effort, he climbed the rest of the way up and allowed his full weight to come crashing down on top of her with another grunted -fwump-.

    If Kara had indeed any air still left in her lungs, it would have gone out with how heavy he was lying right on top of her. Her shock had all but left her breathless in the moments beforehand. Dog heaved a great sigh then, letting it go with a small whistle of a whine. Kara laid there absolutely frozen beneath him, too scared to move, or to even blink. She’d never been this close to Dog before. What on earth had made him just walk up and -flop- himself down onto her like that?

    His heavy exhales continued, blowing into Kara’s neck like a stiff summer wind. The way he whined every other breath immediately gripped her with a sense of unease— as if she weren’t already uneased enough by his strange behavior.

    “What is happening…?” Lena whispered from her seat. Her eyes were wide and alert, and Kara drew her own gaze there with a very careful shift underneath the large animal that sat on top of her.

    “I have no idea.” Kara replied quietly. Dog didn’t seem interested in their conversating, for once. If ever they were both around and he was also present, he would usually take his leave as soon as they started talking. Kara could never understand why, but he wasn’t overly fond of the sound of human voices. He would usually just wander off to go and do his own thing somewhere, as if people carrying on conversation in his presence simply annoyed him. Kara took a chance and let her hands come down on top of him, having held them out in surprise at first— but Dog didn’t move. He simply continued to heave one tired breath after another. She stroked him lightly and felt the way the bristles of his coat changed from soft to course where they grew in a ridge along his back. He was so heavy, enough so that it was difficult to breathe beneath him, but Kara was floored at the fact that she was finally petting him… And that he was finally letting her.

    Lena lurched forward slightly with her apprehension, “Kara—”

    “Wait, I think it’s okay.” Kara began to say as her hand slid down one of Dog’s long forelegs to take him by the paw. It was nearly the size of her own hand and had long tufts of light red fur that burst forth from between each pad. Even his paw was heavy. Kara bounced it up and down to jostle him slightly, speaking to him with a quiet voice, “Hey, boy. What’s wrong?”

    …Something wasn’t right.

    Kara had let go of Dog’s paw, and it fell back down with a heavy -thump- against the edge of the lounge. He took another great big breath in, and let it go with another long whine. Not so much that he sounded like he was in pain, only that he was so very tired.

    “Hey…” Kara stroked him with less caution that time, running her hand up into the mass of fur at his neck. She felt around at his ears and then smoothed her palm over the flat top of his head. Still, he didn’t care. Dog didn’t so much as move a single inch. Kara looked back up at Lena and felt her heart begin to beat loud and heavy behind her ears. A pit grew forth in her stomach like an alarm. “Actually, no. Something’s definitely wrong.” She said.

    “What do you mean?”

    “I don’t know. I can just feel it in my gut.” Kara shook her head and shifted once again to breathe better, “Will you call Alex, please?”

    “Yeah- Of course.”

     

     

    Morning had turned into noon, and Kara was still trapped under a veritable mountain of fur. Help had come quickly enough, but Dog was about as accepting of it as oil is to water.

    Each time Alex drew near him, he’d struggle to pull in enough of a breath to growl at her. Each time the deep rumble of it filtered into Kara’s chest, as if the sound itself was being made through the both of them. She’d never felt more like a part of him than she did now. He was most certainly a part of her, and had been for years, but now it felt as though they had come full circle. Everything aside, Kara was still concerned as to why he still wouldn’t move.

    Alex was attempting to look for wounds. It was uncommon for something like a snake bite to take down such a large creature, but in certain cases where enough venom was injected near a major enough vein, and whose fangs could somehow manage to pierce through a thick enough layer of fur… then, of course, bad things could eventually happen. But unfortunately, the way dog continued to growl every time Alex got close enough to touch him made searching for such a bite impossible.

    “I think he may still be holding a bit of a grudge…” Kara said quietly after Alex had been forced to back off for the umpteenth time.

    Her sister looped her stethoscope around the back of her neck and then sat her hands upon her hips with a long and vexed sort of breath. “Look, dude,” She said towards Dog, “…how many times do I have to say I’m sorry?”

    It was about that time that the screen door gave its springy -pop- whenever it opened, and out from it came Lena with Maggie right behind. Maggie gasped at the sight of Dog and then took a step back on the porch, likely having been ill-prepared for his massive size and the fact that she’d be so standing close to him. “Oh, my God,” She said from behind one of her hands in awe, “…he’s beautiful.”

    Alex caught sight of Lena from the corner of her eye and then turned to her with an idea, “Lena- Do you think he’ll let you touch him?”

    “I sorta can’t move.” Kara explained.

    “I mean,” Lena shrugged, “Maybe?”

    “Will you?”

    “Um, okay. Yeah. What do you need?”

    Alex sighed. “He’s still upset with me and won’t let me get close enough, so I need you to look him over for puncture wounds, or literally anything else.”

    “Alright…” Lena stepped forward slowly with her hands low to her sides. Kara felt Dog stir on top of her as if he wanted to move his elbows and sit up, but he flopped back down and forced another “oof” out from her chest. His tail began to wag weakly at her approach instead.

    Holy crap.

    Kara couldn’t believe it.

    He’s wagging his tail…

    He’s never wagged his tail before.

    Kara followed Dog’s large yellow-gold eyes as they drank Lena in, and she eventually came to kneel upon on the porch beside the lounge. Her hands went very carefully to pet him longways down his side, and then she waited for another long moment to see if he approved.

    His lack of growling seemed to be approval enough. That, and the clumsy tail wagging.

    “Puncture wounds, like snake bites?” Lena asked as she began to comb through the soft fur along his flank.

    “Yeah,” Alex replied anxiously with her arms folded, “If they’re there, they’ll be along his legs or his paws. I can see that his head’s fine, though. Canids mostly find themselves bitten on the joints near their paws because they usually end up walking over a sleeping cottonmouth, or something just as venomous.”

    “Makes sense. The statistic is backwards for humans, unsurprisingly enough.”

    Alex furrowed her brow for a moment while Lena continued to search, “What do you mean? Why is that unsurprising?”

    “Well, humans are the only creatures foolish enough to disturb venomous animals with no real sense of consequence. Where people are concerned, 85% of snake bites occur on the arms and hands, and only about 13% occur on the ankles or feet.”

    “That tracks.” Alex huffed a laugh, “How much of that statistic is found to occur in male patients compared to female patients?”

    Lena smirked while she stared intently between each of Dog’s large, clawed toes, “It’s a staggering ratio, honestly.”

    “I’ll bet it is.”

    “I’m not finding anything…” Lena said at last, dropping her hands back into her lap.

    Kara was almost relieved, but the sense of something still being very wrong was overwhelming. There was still the question why Dog refused to move.

    “Okay,” Alex craned her head a bit, “Can you show me his gums?”

    Lena reared her head back at that. “You want me to go near his teeth?”

    “He wagged his tail at you. I’m sure if you do it quickly, he won’t mind all that much.”

    Kara tried her best to lean forward, then she slunk a hand down to take one of Lena’s into her own, “It’s alright. I’ll help.”

    Lena pursed her lips slightly with a bit more thought, and then she gave a quick tuck of her chin with a nod. She went to stroke gently between Dog’s eyes while Kara took up the motion there with her own hand. Lena was eventually able to pull back Dog’s whiskered upper lip to reveal his rows of very intimidating, yellow teeth; some long and pointed, some cracked down their middles, but all of them seemed to sit beneath an expanse of pale gums. Lena gave a twist of her head back towards Alex and then shared a look with her that made the pit in Kara’s stomach grow even deeper.

    “What?” Kara asked breathily, her heart behind her ears again.

    Lena motioned towards Alex with an open hand, “Can I—”

    “Here you go.” Alex handed over her stethoscope in reply.

    Kara could hardly stand it. Lena and Alex seemed to be speaking a language she didn’t understand now.

    “What’s going on?” She asked.

    Instead of answering, Lena held Kara’s hand as she moved the stethoscope’s diaphragm around to the left side of Dog’s ribcage. Finding his heartbeat seemed to be a thing of ease with such a deep chest. Lena paused for a long moment and listened. Then, her eyes turned back up to Kara, and they began to well with tears.  

    “Lena—”

    “Kara, I’m so sorry.”

    “What do you mean ‘you’re sorry’- what’s wrong with him?” Kara’s eyes flitted to her sister again with a frantic feeling spreading out from her torso, “Alex, what’s wrong with him?!”

    Alex came as close as she could before Dog’s lip began to curl upwards at her, but he seemed to not possess any more energy to growl. She kneeled down onto the porch and clasped her hands, lacing her fingers together as if in prayer. “He’s old, Kara.”

    “I know that—”

    “So, he’s probably not gonna get back up.”

    “What do you mean?!” Kara immediately felt like sobbing. The lump in her throat was painful, so much so that it made it hard to speak in full sentences from there on out, “What are you saying? He’s sick?”

    “No, I don’t think so. It’s just nature’s way—”

    “No, please. Please don’t say that.”

    “Kara—”

    “No! I’m not ready!” Tears began to roll out from Kara’s eyes one at a time. They burned hot tracks down her cheeks, but still, she didn’t let herself sob. “I’m not ready.” She insisted.

    Alex shook her head and spoke with a hushed tone then that only Kara could hear, “You know as well as I do, the wood doesn’t care if you’re ready or not. It just does what it does.”

    “Take him, then.” Kara begged, doing her best to push Dog upward and away from her chest. He rolled a peeved sort of grumble at her and then heaved one more sigh once she relented. “Take him and get him some medicine or something. I don’t care anymore- you can take him to Saint Francis—”

    “Kara…”

    “Please—”

    Alex frowned. “He’s come home to die, Kara. It’s best just to let him go peacefully.”

    Abstaining from her weeping became harder and harder as the seconds continued to pass. The porch soon went silent.

    Kara eventually heard a quiet sniffle from where Maggie stood near the door. Then another from Lena. Lena’s eyes were rimmed red as she went to lay the side of her head flat upon Dog’s large neck. She skirted her hand into the back of Kara’s hair and swiped there lovingly with her thumb, bringing Kara’s head down to meet her own. Lena whispered there low enough for her to hear, “It’s okay. Go ahead.”

    And just like that, Kara broke open with a pitiful sob. They chugged out of her for what felt like hours, long enough for the sun to eventually set, and for the shallow breaths that lived atop her own to become no more.

    That was it…

    Dog was gone.

    …And Kara’s heart was broken.

     

     

    “Kara, don’t forget your lunchbox on the counter!”

    Mom was calling from across the house. Kara looked down at her hands and saw that they were empty. “Whoops!” She shouted back.

    “See you after school, sweetheart.”

    “Bye, Dad!”

    Kara twisted the knob of their front door hurriedly in order to catch her bus, but something feathery that lied upon the mat on the other side made her trip and fall all the way down. She grabbed at her knee and winced with a sharp breath pulled through her teeth, and then she turned on her hips to look back at what she’d stumbled over.

    A chicken…

    Left as a gift.

    A trail of slobber and feathers went down the front porch steps in front of her, and she suddenly knew exactly who to blame.

    “Dangit, Dog—!"

    Kara tossed and turned throughout the night, fighting tooth and nail just to make it until morning.

    She was on her dock next, fold out chair and short antennaed radio alongside. Something squirmed inside of her hand as the clawed -tick- -tack- of familiar steps took her attention towards the shoreline. A worm made its way onto her hook once she spied Dog from afar, snapping his long jaws in the air as if he were biting at an invisible snack.

    Flashes of yellowish light danced in front of him, taunting him this way and that with more toothy chomps. Kara felt herself chuckle at the sight of his foolishness. He’d grown large over the years and was always, dependably, not too far behind. He kept himself busy enough on most days, but whenever Kara fished, he liked sometimes just to sit and watch. Every once in a while, he’d get something out of it in the shape of a filet. The fireflies were enough to occupy him this time, though.

    Kara warned him to pay attention as he leapt and scraped after the small glowing bugs onto the dock, and after a while, she looked back to the lake to cast her line. The next thing she knew, she heard a great -splash- from behind her, and she spun around on a dime to witness Dog chewing pridefully as he trudged sopping wet from head to toe back onto the slippery bank.

    Kara nearly fell down into the water herself from all of her laughing.

    Once she woke up again, she decided that sleep would be pointless. There was nothing that could take her away from the pain of losing one of her life’s best friends. Kara rolled over and began to cry again as quietly as she could, but the tears through her nose proved to be too much. Soon, Lena rolled over to meet her.

    “Come here, baby.” She said, reaching into the dark to pull Kara close.

    Kara felt herself begin to cry more freely into the warm space of Lena’s neck, and eventually, the pounding in her head subsided, and she was able to sleep until sunrise without the plague of dreams to remind her of what she’d lost.

    She had the rest of her life to remember him. So, even though it felt monstrously unfair of her, all she wanted was just to forget. Just for a little while… Numbness was still a far sight better than devastation.

    Saying goodbye was a ready and waiting grenade, pin pulled and on its way with the breaking of dawn.

     

     

    She should have been glad that Dog’s tired heart had at least allowed him to come back home.

    She should have been glad that she didn’t have to hold him until he passed alone.

    She should have been glad that she’d finally been able to wrap her arms around him and hug him like she’d always wanted.

    Kara tried counting her blessings with each long stroke of her oars…

    But the grieving twelve year old little girl that lived inside of her wouldn’t allow it.

    She’d throttled quickly enough through denial, anger and then bargaining before Dog had so much as taken his last whine of a breath, so now she was wading knee-deep through the mire of her depression. Dog was one of the last links she had to her parents, and to her, losing him was like losing a piece of her identity. He was an extension of her. They shared a connection that words simply failed to describe, time and time again.

    It took Kara longer than she expected to find it again after so long, but by noon the following day, she was dragging the square bottom of her swamper boat up and onto a muddy shoal. Half an hour’s journey onto the dried bank and deep into the wood from there, she found it.

    The tree was still there.

    Kara remembered each handhold she’d made on her way up as a child, and where exactly her foot had slipped to bring her all the way back down. She felt waves of indecision roll over her on her way there with him, one hundred and sixty pounds of limp muscle slung over her shoulder. She’d stopped to take breaks and considered turning back each time just to inter him somewhere a bit more reasonable, but the feeling that pulled her bones in this direction was undeniable. It made her feel silly. It made her feel crazy. It made her feel unworthy…

    But still, she knew there was no better place to leave him. No matter how much she argued with herself, she always came back to the very same conclusion: the beginning was the only place to bring the end.

    Kara dug at the base of their tree for hours; the dirt at first was soft, and then hard, and then rocky, and then soft again. Dusk would be upon them soon, but she didn’t care. She’d stay out there all night and risk it being her own grave instead for not having the courage to cover her friend back up with so much earth. Kara eventually laid Dog upon the mossy ground beside it and panted tiredly over him, deciding just to lay down there with him and rest for a moment. Would she remember the way he smelled? The grayed whiskers upon his chin?

    Her eyes filled with tears again as the fading sunlight glittered through all of the hanging moss above. Kara hugged him once more, and then she sat up on her knees and waited.

    She just stared at him, as if he owed it to her to change back into the shape of a man.

    Would he look like how she had remembered? Or had she even really remembered anything at all? Maybe she’d just dreamt the whole thing? Would his hair be reddish-brown and chin length, and his eyes still be yellow?

    Kara placed her hands upon the barrel of Dog’s ribs and shook him, her tears dropping from the point of her chin now. “Come on…” She said with a wavering voice. Then she jostled him harder, “Come on!”

    Kara bent forward and sobbed over him again. Grief like this made her feel out of her mind. When would it stop? Would it ever?

    Little by little, she filled in the hole with her best friend inside and went to pluck large stones from the earth to place one by one in a small hill on top. She was covered with dirt all the way up to her neck, and tired as hell, so it was all she could do just to lay beside his grave one more time and weep.

    “I miss you already.” She cried.

    Her words were quickly swept up with a gust of wind that shook the needles of each pine, and rustled the long drapes of each mossy branch. It howled all around her as if it were in mourning. For all Kara cared, the ground beneath her could come right up and swallow her back down. She wouldn’t mind… But then she’d never see Lena again. Or anyone else she cared about, for that matter. Kara lifted her head from her bed of green moss and wiped at her swollen eyes. She could still see the trail out through the woods and toward the water.

    She would go back home.

    The wind, and now a chorus of “hoots” from the ever-watchful owls, along with an eerie yet unmistakable melody of howls soon filled her ears. Kara sat up, held her breath, and listened. The howls grew louder.

    The forest had joined Kara’s lament.

    Her loss was felt far and wide.

    Kara looked down at the oblong shaped stone she’d laid at the head of Dog’s grave and wiped at her eyes again. “You’ll always be my family, but I need to get back to everyone else. They still need me.” She touched the smooth edge that faced up at her, “I love you… thanks for everything. You were a very good boy.” Kara looked over her shoulder one last time as she walked away, comfortable with the fact that she’d always know right where he would be, “Goodbye.”

    She made her way back home feeling not like she was rowing away from a piece of herself, but towards more of them. Kara would take her memories with her, keeping them safe and sound until a day she could call upon them without any more pain.

    After all, Dog had taught her the most important lesson of all; Broken bridges and bones do not a bad life make.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    I usually make cover art to go with my favorite stories, so there. I'll put it at the beginning of the story as well.

    I know that the loss of a pet or a loved one can be very triggering for some, but since you read this through and made it all the way here, I would appreciate the emotional fortitude to not: yell at me/express your hurt feelings at me, or attempt to try and "educate" me, because this is my story and I will write it the way that I must. There's been a lot of loss in my life, and I find writing/reading about it to be a much needed catharsis. Everyone has their own way of handling grief. This is mine.

    Thanks for reading. <3
